Geo turned, away from Jordde, and stepped past him onto the deck, and Urson followed him. Ten steps farther on, he glanced back, and seeing that Jordde had emerged from the cabin and was walking in the other direction, he set Snake down on his feet. “All right, Little One. March!”
In the passage to the forecastle, Urson asked, “Hey, what’s going on?”
“Well, for one thing, our little friend here is no spy,” said Geo.
“How do you know?” asked Urson.
“Because she doesn’t know he can read minds.”
“How do you mean?” Urson asked.

===Chinese===
吉奥转过身，背向约德，侧身从他身边跨上甲板，厄尔森跟了上来。走出十步开外，他回头瞥了一眼，见约德已经出了舱门、正朝另一个方向走去，便把蛇放下地来站好。“好了，小家伙。开步走！”

在回水手舱的过道里，厄尔森问：“喂，这是唱的哪一出？”

“嗯，头一件事，咱们这位小朋友不是奸细。”吉奥说。

“你怎么知道？”厄尔森问。

“因为她不知道他能读人心。”

“这话怎么讲？”厄尔森问。

===Original===
“First of all, I was beginning to think something was wrong when I came back from talking to the priestess. You were too, and it lay in the same vein you were talking about. Why would our task be completely useless unless we accomplished all parts of her mission? Wouldn’t there be some value in just returning her sister, the rightful head of Leptar, to her former position? And I’m sure her sister may well have collected some useful information that could be used against Aptor, so that would be some value even if we didn’t find the jewel. It doesn’t sound too sisterly a thing to me to forsake the young priestess if there is no jewel in it for her. And her tone, the way she refers to the jewel as *hers*. There’s an old saying, from before the Great Fire even: Power corrupts, and absolute power corrupts absolutely. And I think she has not a little of the un-goddess-like desire for power first, peace afterwards.”

===Chinese===
“首先，我从同女祭司谈话回来的时候，就开始觉得哪里不对了。你也觉出来了，而且就出在你说的那个路子上。为什么非得把她使命的每一件都办成，我们这趟差事才算不白干？光是把她的妹妹——莱普塔（Leptar）名正言顺的元首——送回原来的位置上，难道就没有一点价值？而且我敢肯定，她妹妹多半也搜罗了一些能用来对付阿普托的有用情报，这样哪怕我们找不回珍宝，也总算有一点价值。依我看，只要珍宝落不到她自己手里，那位年轻的女祭司就可以撇下不管——这可不大像做姐姐的干的事。还有她的口气，她开口闭口把那珍宝叫作*她的*。有句老话，老到大火灾（the Great Fire）之前就有了：权力使人腐化，绝对的权力使人绝对地腐化。我看她身上绝不少那种先要权力、后要太平的欲望——一点也不像个女神。”

===Original===
“But that doesn’t mean this one isn’t an Aptor spy,” said Urson.
“Wait a minute. I’m getting there. At first I thought he was too. The idea occurred to me first when I was talking to the priestess and she first mentioned that there were spies from Aptor. The coincidence of his appearance, that he had even managed to steal the jewel in the first place, that he would present it to her the way he did; all this hinted something so strange, that spy was the first thing I thought of, and I’m sure it was the first thing she thought as well. And she especially would think this if she did not know that Snake could read minds and broadcast mentally, because ignorance of his telepathy removes the one other possible explanation of the coincidences. But, Urson, why did he leave the jewel with us before he went to see her?”
“Because he thought she was going to try and take it away from him.”
“Exactly. When she told me to send him up to her, I was fairly sure that was the main reason she wanted him. But if he was a spy, and knew how to work the jewel, then why not take it with him, present himself to Argo with the jewel, showing himself as an equal force, and then come calmly back, leaving her in silence and us still on his side, especially since he would be revealing to her something of which she was nine-tenths aware of already, and would watch him no more carefully than she would were it not confirmed.”

===Chinese===
“可那不代表这一个就不是阿普托的奸细。”厄尔森说。

“等等，我这就说到那儿。起初我也当他是。这个念头，是我同女祭司谈话、她头一回提到阿普托派有奸细的时候冒出来的。他露面的那点巧合，他当初居然能把珍宝偷到手，他又偏偏用那种方式把它献给她；这一切透着说不出的蹊跷，所以我头一个想到的就是奸细，而且我敢说她头一个想到的也是。尤其是，她若不知道蛇能读心、还能在心思里发声，她就更会这么想，因为不知道他有心电传意的本事，这些巧合便只剩‘奸细’这一个解释了。可是厄尔森，他去看她之前，为什么把珍宝留在我们这儿？”

“因为他当她要设法把它从他手里夺走。”

“正是。她吩咐我把他送上去的时候，我就十拿九稳，那就是她要他的主要缘故。可他若真是奸细，又懂得怎么驱动珍宝，那为什么不带着它去，携着珍宝在阿戈面前现身，亮出自己是一股对等的力量，然后从容回来，让她摸不着头脑，让我们照旧站在他一边？更何况他这样向她摊开的，不过是她本来就已想到九成的东西；摊开之后，她对他的提防，也未必就比不摊的时候多半分。”

===Original===
“All right,” said Urson, “why not?”
“Because he was not a spy, and didn’t know how to work the jewel. Yes, he had felt its power once. Perhaps he was going to pretend he had it hidden on his person. But he did not want her to get her hands on it for reasons that were strong, but not selfish.
“Here, Snake,” said Geo. “You know how to work the jewel now, don’t you; but you learned from Argo just now.”
The boy nodded.
“Here, then, why don’t you take it?” Geo lifted the jewel from his neck and held it out to him.

===Chinese===
“行啊。”厄尔森说，“为什么不来这一手？”

“因为他不是奸细，也不懂怎么驱动珍宝。是的，那威力他领教过一回。也许他还打算装作把它藏在身上。可他不让她碰着它，理由很硬，却不是为了自己。”

“来，蛇，”吉奥说，“你现在知道怎么驱动珍宝了，对吧；不过是方才从阿戈那儿学来的。”

男孩点了点头。

“那么来，你怎么不拿回去？”吉奥把珍宝从脖子上摘下来，朝他递过去。

===Original===
Snake drew back and shook his head violently.
Urson looked puzzled.
“Snake has seen into human minds, Urson. He’s seen things directly which the rest of us only learn from a sort of second hand observation. He knows that the power of this little bead is more dangerous to the mind of the person who wields it than it is to the cities it may destroy.”
“Well,” said Urson, “as long as she thinks he’s a spy, at least we’ll have one of them little beads and someone who knows how to use it. I mean if we have to.”

===Chinese===
蛇直往后缩，拼命摇头。

厄尔森一脸的迷惑。

“蛇看见过人的内心，厄尔森。我们旁人只能隔着二手的观察约莫知道的东西，他是直看进去的。他明白，这颗小珠子的力量，对挥动它的人的心智，比对它可能毁掉的那些城池，更危险。”

“唔，”厄尔森说，“只要她当他是奸细，咱们好歹就攥着一颗那样的小珠子，外加一个会使唤它的人。我是说，万一用得着的话。”

===Original===
“I don’t think she thinks he’s a spy any more, Urson.”
“Huh?”
“I give her credit for being able to reason at least as well as I can. Once she found out he had no jewel on him, she knew that he was as innocent as you and I are. But her only thought was to get it in any way she could. When we came in, just when she was going to put Snake under the jewel’s control, guilt made her leap backwards to her first and seemingly logical accusation for our benefit. Evil likes to cloak itself as good.”

===Chinese===
“我看她如今也不再当他是奸细了，厄尔森。”

“啊？”

“我信她的推理本事至少不输于我。她一旦发现他身上没有珍宝，就知道他跟你我一样清白。可她满脑子只有一个念头：不惜一切手段把它弄到手。我们闯进去的时候，恰恰赶上她就要用珍宝制服蛇，那份心虚逼得她朝后一跳，跳回她最早的那个、说给我们听似乎还合乎情理的罪名上。恶总爱把自己装扮成善。”

===Original===
They stepped down into the forecastle. By now a handful of sailors had come into the room, mostly drunk and snoring on berths around the walls. One had wrapped himself completely up in a blanket in the middle berth of the tier that Urson had chosen for the three. “Well,” said Urson to Snake, “it looks like you’ll have to move.”
Snake scrambled to the top bunk.
“Now look, that one was mine.”
Snake motioned him up.
“Huh? Two of us in one of those?” demanded Urson. “Look, if you want someone to keep warm against, go down and sleep with Geo there. It’s more room and you won’t get squashed against the wall. I’m a thrasher when I sleep.”

===Chinese===
他们跨下梯口进了水手舱。这会儿已有几个水手进了屋，多半醉醺醺的，在四壁的铺位上打着呼噜。有一个用毯子把自己整个裹了起来，占的正是厄尔森给他们仨挑的那一叠铺位的中间一张。“唉，”厄尔森对蛇说，“看来你只好挪窝了。”

蛇手脚并用地爬上了最上铺。

“喂，说好了，那张是我的。”

蛇朝他打手势，叫他上去。

“啊？我们俩挤一张铺？”厄尔森不答应了，“听着，你要是想找个暖身的，就下去跟吉奥睡去。那儿宽绰，你也用不着被挤扁在墙上。我睡觉可不老实。”

===Original===
Snake didn’t move.
“Maybe you better do what he says,” Geo said. “I have an idea that﻿ ﻿…”
“You’ve got another idea now?” asked Urson, “Oh, damn, I’m too tired to argue.” He vaulted up to the top bunk. “Now move over and be very small.” He stretched out, and Snake’s slight body was completely hidden. “Hey, get your elbows out of there,” Geo heard Urson mutter before there was only a gentle thundering of his snore.
---

===Chinese===
蛇不动窝。

“也许你还是依他的好。”吉奥说，“我有个念头……”

“你这会儿又冒出一个念头了？”厄尔森问，“哦，见鬼，我累得懒得争了。”他翻身跃上顶铺。“挪过去，把自己缩小点儿。”他直挺挺躺平，蛇那瘦小的身子整个被埋没了。“喂，把你的胳膊肘从那儿拿开。”吉奥听见厄尔森咕哝了一句，随后就只剩下他的鼾声，像一阵闷雷轻轻地滚。

---

===Original===
*Silver mist suffused the deck of the ship and wet lines glowed a phosphorescent silver; the sky was pale as ice; pricks of stars dotted over the whole bowl. The sea, once green, seemed bleached to blowing clouds of white powder. The door of a cabin opened and white veils flung forward from the form of Argo who emerged like silver from the bone-colored door. The whole movement of the scene made it look like a picture imagination fastens in the slow ripplings of gauze under breeze. One dark spot was at her throat, pulsing darkly, like a heart, like a black flame. She walked to the railing, peered over. In the white washing a skeletal hand appeared. It raised on a beckoning arm, then fell forward in the water. Another arm raised now, a few feet away, beckoning, gesturing. Then three at once; then two more.*
*A voice as pale as the vision spoke “I am coming. We sail in a hour. The mate has been ordered to put the ship out before dawn. You must tell me now, creatures of the water.”*
*Two glowing arms raised up, and then an almost featureless face. Chest high in the water, it listed backwards and sank again.*

===Chinese===
*银雾漫过船的甲板，湿漉漉的缆索泛着磷光似的银辉；天空苍白如冰；星子的尖芒缀满了整个天穹。海，原本是绿的，此刻仿佛漂成了大片大片翻卷的白粉。一扇舱门打开，阿戈的身影从骨色的门里走出来，通身像一线银光，白色的面纱向前翻飞。整幅景象的动静，像想象在微风里缓缓漾动的纱幕中定格下来的一幅画。她喉间有一个黑点，幽幽地搏动着，像一颗心脏，像一簇黑色的火焰。她走到栏杆边，向下窥望。白色的浪沫里，探出一只骷髅的手。它攀着一条召唤的手臂扬起，随即向前栽进水中。又一条手臂扬了起来，隔着几英尺，召唤着，比划着。接着是三条同时而起；又添两条。*

*一个和这幻象一样苍白的声音开了口：“我就来。我们一个钟头后启航。大副已奉命在黎明前把船开出海去。你们现在就得告诉我，水中的生灵。”*

*两条发亮的手臂扬了起来，随后是一张几乎看不出五官的脸。它在水里立到齐胸高，向后一侧，又沉了下去。*

===Original===
*“Are you of Aptor or Leptar?” spoke the apparitional figure of Argo again in the thinned voice. “Are your allegiances to Argo or Hama? I have followed thus far. You must tell me before I follow farther.”*
*There was a whirling of sound which seemed to be the wind attempting to say, “The sea﻿ ﻿… the sea﻿ ﻿… the sea﻿ ﻿…”*
*But Argo did not hear, for she turned away and walked from the rail, back to her cabin.*

===Chinese===
*“你是阿普托的，还是莱普塔的？”阿戈那幽灵似的身影又用那变得稀薄的声音说道，“你效忠的是阿戈，还是哈马（Hama）？我已经跟到了这里。再往前跟之前，你们必须告诉我。”*

*有一阵打旋的声响，像是风在努力说话：“海啊……海啊……海啊……”*

*可阿戈没有听见，因为她已经转过身，离开栏杆，回她的舱房去了。*

===Original===
*Now the scene moved, turned toward the door of the forecastle. It opened, moved through the hall, the walls more like polished steel than weathered wood, and went on. In the forecastle, the yellow oil lamp seemed a white flaring of magnesium.*
*The movement stopped in front of a tier of three berths; on the bottom one lay a young man with a starved, pallid face. His mop of hair was bleached white. On his chest was a pulsing darkness, a black flame, a dark heart, shimmering with the indistinctness of absolute shadow. On the top bunk a great form like a bloated corpse lay. One huge arm hung over the bunk, flabbed, puffy, without muscle.*
*In the center berth was an anonymous bundle of blankets completely covering the figure inside. On this the scene fixed, drew closer﻿ ﻿… and the paleness suddenly faded before darkness, into shadow, into nothing.*
---

===Chinese===
*这时景象移动起来，转向水手舱的舱门。门开了，它穿过过道——两壁与其说是风霜的旧木，不如说更像磨亮的钢铁——一路向前。到了水手舱，那盏黄黄的油灯仿佛成了镁光的一道白焰。*

*那移动在一叠三层的铺位前停住；最下面一张上躺着一个年轻人，一张饿得发白的脸。他那头浓密的乱发漂得雪白。他胸口有一团搏动的黑暗，一簇黑色的火焰，一颗黑的心脏，带着绝对暗影的那种朦胧，幽幽闪烁。顶铺上横着一个庞然的身形，像一具泡胀的尸首。一条巨大的胳膊垂在铺外，松塌臃肿，没有肌肉。*

*中间那张铺上是一卷无名的毯子，把里头的身影裹得严严实实。景象定在它上面，凑近，再凑近……那片苍白蓦地在黑暗前褪去，褪入暗影，褪入虚无。*

---

===Original===
Geo sat up and knuckled his eyes.
The dark forecastle was relieved by the yellow glow of the lamp. The gaunt mate stood across the room. “Hey, you,” he was saying to a man in one of the bunks, “up and out. We’re sailing.”
The figure roused itself from the tangle of bedding.
The mate moved to another. “Up, you dog face. Up, you fish fodder. We’re sailing.” Turning around, he saw Geo watching him. “And what’s wrong with you?” he demanded. “We’re sailing, didn’t you hear? Naw, you go back to sleep. Your turn will come, but we need experienced ones now.” He grinned briefly, and then went on to one more. “Eh, you stink like an old wine cask. Raise yourself out of your fumes. We’re sailing!”

===Chinese===
吉奥坐起身，用指节揉着眼睛。

昏暗的水手舱被油灯的黄光照亮了些。瘦削的大副站在舱室另一头。“喂，你，”他正对一个铺位上的人说，“起来，出去。咱们开船了。”

那身影从乱作一团的铺盖里挣起身来。

大副走向下一个。“起来，你这张狗脸。起来，你这块鱼食。咱们开船了。”一转身，他看见吉奥正望着他。“你怎么了？”他质问道，“开船了，没听见？得，回去接着睡你的。轮到你还早着呢，眼下我们要的是老手。”他咧嘴一笑，又走向下一个。“哎，你臭得像只老酒桶。从你那身酒气里把自己拔出来吧。咱们开船了！”

## IV / 四、怒海惊变

===Original===
“That dream,” Geo said to Urson a moment after the mate left. Urson looked down from his bunk.
“You had it too?”
Both turned to Snake.
“I guess that was your doing, eh?” Urson said.
Snake scrambled down from the upper bunk.
“Did you go wandering around the deck last night and do some spying?” Geo asked.
By now most of the other sailors had risen, and one suddenly stepped between Urson and Geo. “ ’Scuse me, mate,” he said and shook the figure in the second berth. “Hey, Whitey, come on. You can’t be that soused from last night. Get up or you’ll miss mess.” The young sailor shook the figure again. “Hey, Whitey.” The figure in the blankets was unresponsive. The sailor gave him one more good shake, and as he rolled over, the blanket fell away from the blond head. The eyes were wide and dull, the mouth half open. “Hey, Whitey,” the black sailor said again, and then he stepped back, slowly.
---

===Chinese===
大副走后片刻，吉奥（Geo）对厄尔森（Urson）开了口：“那个梦。”厄尔森从铺位上低头望下来。
“你也做了？”
两人一齐转向蛇（Snake）。
“我猜那是你干的好事，呃？”厄尔森说。
蛇从上铺爬了下来。
“你昨天夜里是不是在甲板上逛了一圈，搞了点侦察？”吉奥问。
这时其余的水手大都起了身，忽然有一个从厄尔森和吉奥中间挤了过去。“借光，伙计，”他说着，摇了摇第二张铺位里的人形。“喂，白佬（Whitey），快起来。昨晚那点酒不至于醉成这样吧。再不起可要误了开饭。”年轻水手又摇了摇那人。“喂，白佬。”毯子里的人毫无反应。水手又狠狠摇了一下，随着那身子翻过来，毯子从那颗金发的脑袋上滑落。眼睛睁得大大的，黯然无光，嘴巴半张着。“喂，白佬。”黑皮肤的水手又叫了一声，随即缓缓退了回去。
---

===Original===
Mist enveloped the ship three hours out from port. Urson was called for duty right after breakfast, but no one bothered either Snake or Geo that first morning. Snake would slip off somewhere and Geo would be left to wander the ship alone. He was walking beneath the dories when the heavy slap of bare feet on the wet deck materialized in Urson. “Hey,” greeted his friend. “What are you doing under here?”
“Nothing much,” Geo said.
Urson was carrying a coil of rope about his shoulder. Now he slung it down into his hand and leaned against the support shaft and looked out toward the fog. “It’s a bad beginning this trip has had,” he said. “What few sailors I’ve talked to don’t like it at all.”
“Urson,” said Geo, “have you any idea what actually happened this morning?”
“Maybe I have and maybe I haven’t,” Urson said. “What ones have you?”
“Do you remember the dream?” he asked.
Urson scrunched his shoulders as if suddenly cold. “I do,” he said.

===Chinese===
船出港三个钟头，雾便把它整个裹住了。早饭后厄尔森就被叫去当值，可头一个上午，谁也没来搅扰蛇和吉奥。蛇总是溜到什么地方去，吉奥便落得独自一人在船上晃荡。他正打小艇底下走过，湿甲板上一阵赤脚的沉重啪嗒声，凝成了厄尔森这个人。“嘿，”他的朋友打招呼道，“你在这底下干什么呢？”
“没什么。”吉奥说。
厄尔森肩上盘着一圈绳索。这时他把它甩落到手里，斜倚在支柱上，朝雾望去。“这趟航程开头开得不吉利，”他说，“跟我搭过话的那几个水手，没一个喜欢。”
“厄尔森，”吉奥说，“今早到底出了什么事，你心里有数吗？”
“也许有，也许没有。”厄尔森说，“你又有什么数？”
“你记得那个梦吗？”他问。
厄尔森缩起肩膀，像是突然觉出了冷。“记得。”他说。

===Original===
“It was like we were seeing through somebody else’s eyes, almost.”
“Our little four-armed friend sees things in a strange way if that’s the case.”
“Urson, that wasn’t Snake’s eyes we saw through. I asked him, just before he went off exploring the ship. It was somebody else. All he did was get the pictures and relay them into our minds. And what was the last thing you saw?”
“As a matter of fact,” Urson said, turning, “I think he was looking at poor Whitey’s bunk.”
“And who was supposed to be sleeping in poor Whitey’s bunk?”
“Snake?”
“Exactly. Do you think perhaps Whitey was killed?”
“Could be, I guess. But how, and why, and who?”

===Chinese===
“那情形，简直就像我们是在借别人的眼睛看东西。”
“要真是那样，咱们这位四臂小朋友看东西的方式可真够怪的。”
“厄尔森，我们借的并不是蛇的眼睛。他动身去探船之前，我问过他了。是另外的什么人。他只是把画面接过来，转发进我们的脑子里。你最后看见的是什么？”
“说起来，”厄尔森转过身说，“我看他当时正望着可怜的白佬那张铺位。”
“照理该睡在可怜的白佬铺位里的，又是谁？”
“蛇？”
“正是。你说白佬会不会是被害死的？”
“我看兴许是。可怎么下的手，图什么，又是谁？”

===Original===
“Somebody who wanted Snake killed. Maybe the same person who cut his tongue out a year and a half ago.”
“I thought we decided that we didn’t know who that was.”
“A man you know, Urson,” Geo said. “What man on this ship have you sailed with before?”
“Don’t you think I’ve been looking?” Urson asked. “There’s not a familiar face on deck, other than maybe one I’ve seen in a dockside bar, but never one whose name I’ve known.”
“Think, Urson, who on this ship you’ve sailed with before,” Geo asked again, more intently.
Suddenly Urson turned. “You mean the mate?”
“That’s just who I mean,” said Geo.

===Chinese===
“某个想要蛇的命的人。没准跟一年半前割掉他舌头的是同一个。”
“我记得咱们早认定了，不知道那是谁。”
“是个你认得的人，厄尔森。”吉奥说。“这条船上，哪个男人你从前同过船？”
“你以为我没留意找过？”厄尔森问，“甲板上没有一张熟脸，顶多哪张是在码头酒馆里见过的，可没有一个叫得出名字的。”
“想想，厄尔森，这条船上你从前跟谁同过船。”吉奥又问了一遍，语气更急切了。
厄尔森猛地转过身。“你是说大副？”
“我说的正是他。”吉奥说。

===Original===
“And you think he tried to kill Snake. Why didn’t Snake tell us?”
“Because he thought if we knew, we’d get in trouble with it. And he may be right.”
“How come?” asked Urson.
“Look, we know something is fishy about Argo. The more I think about it, the less I can put my hands on it. But if something is fishy about the mate too, then perhaps he’s in cahoots with her. What about when he came into Argo’s cabin last night when we were there?”
“Maybe he was just doing what we said we were; walking by when he heard a noise. If it was his eyes we were seeing through, then he sees things awfully funny, then.”
“Maybe he’s a strange one too, like Snake who ‘hears’ things funny. Not all strangeness shows,” Geo reminded him.
“You could be right,” said Urson. “You could be right.” He stood up from where he had leaned against a lifeboat support. “Well, you think some more friend, and I’ll listen. I’ll see you later.” He hauled up his rope again and started off in the mist.

===Chinese===
“那你觉得，他是想杀蛇。蛇当时怎么不告诉我们？”
“因为他觉得我们要是知道了，会因此惹上麻烦。他没准是对的。”
“怎么会？”厄尔森问。
“你瞧，咱们知道阿戈（Argo）有不对劲的地方。可我越想，越抓不住那究竟是什么。要是大副也有不对劲的地方，那他没准跟她是一伙的。昨晚咱们在她舱里的时候他闯进来，那一遭又怎么说？”
“也许他干的正是咱们自称在干的事；路过时听见响动。可要是咱们借的真是他的眼睛，那他看东西可真是怪透了。”
“也许他也是个异人，就像蛇‘听’东西听得古里古怪。异相不一定都摆在明面上。”吉奥提醒他。
“你说不定是对的。”厄尔森说，“说不定是对的。”他从斜倚着的救生艇支架上站起身。“好啦，朋友，你再接着想，我洗耳恭听。回头见。”他重新扛起绳索，走进雾里去了。

===Original===
Geo decided to search for Snake. A ladder led to the upper deck, and climbing it, he saw across the deck a tall, fog-shrouded figure. He paused, and then started forward. “Hello,” he said.
The captain turned from the railing and looked at him.
“Good morning sir,” Geo said. “I thought you might be the mate.”
The captain was silent for a while, and then said, “Good morning. What do you want?”
“I didn’t mean to disturb you if you were﻿ ﻿…”
“No disturbance,” said the captain.

===Chinese===
吉奥决计去找蛇。一道梯子通向上层甲板，爬上去，他望见甲板那头立着一个高高的、雾裹着的身影。他顿了顿，随即走上前去。“您好。”他说。
船长从栏杆边转过身来，望着他。
“早上好，先生。”吉奥说，“我还当您是大副呢。”
船长沉默了一阵，随后说：“早上好。你有什么事？”
“要是您正……我不是有意打扰。”
“不打扰。”船长说。

===Original===
“How long will it take us to get to Aptor?”
“Another three weeks. Shorter if this wind keeps up.”
“I see,” said Geo. “Have you any idea of the geography of Aptor?”
“The mate is the only one on board that has ever set foot on Aptor and come off it alive. Except Priestess Argo.”
“The mate, sir? When?”
“On a previous voyage he was wrecked there. He made a raft and drifted into the open sea where he had the good fortune to be picked up by a ship.”

===Chinese===
“到阿普托（Aptor）还要多久？”
“还得三个星期。这风要是不停，还能快些。”
“明白了。”吉奥说，“阿普托的地理，您知道多少？”
“船上只有大副一个人，踏上过阿普托又活着离开了。再有就是阿戈女祭司（Priestess Argo）。”
“大副，先生？什么时候？”
“早先一回跑船，他在那儿遭了难。他扎了个筏子，漂进外海，侥幸被一条船救起。”

===Original===
“Then he will lead whatever party goes to the place?”
“Not him,” said the captain. “He’s sworn never to set foot on the place again. Don’t even ask him to talk about it. Imagine what sort of a place it must be if probable death on the open sea is better than struggling on its land. No, he’ll pilot us through the bay to the river’s estuary, but other than that, he will have nothing to do with the place.
“Two other men we had on board who’d been there and returned. They went with the Priestess Argo in a boat of thirteen. Ten were dismembered and the pieces of their bodies were thrown in the water. Two survived to row the Priestess back to the boat. One was the sailor who died in the forecastle this morning. Not half an hour ago, I received news that the other one went overboard from the rigging and was lost in the sea. This is not a good trip. Men are not to be lost like coins in a game. Life is too valuable.”
“I see,” said Geo. “Thank you for your information and time, sir.”
“You are welcome,” the captain said. Then he turned away.
Geo descended the ladder again and walked slowly forward. Something touched him on the shoulder and he whirled.

===Chinese===
“那登岸的队伍，就由他领着去了？”
“他才不干。”船长说，“他发过誓，此生再不踏上那地方一步。连谈都别叫他谈。想想吧，那得是个什么地方——在外海上十有八九地死，都比在它的陆地上挣扎要强。不，他会领我们穿过海湾，到那条河的河口，可除了这个，他跟那地方再无半点干系。”
“船上原先还有两个人，到过那儿又回来了。他们随阿戈女祭司乘一条十三人的小艇去的。十个人被肢解，尸块被扔进了水里。两个活了下来，把女祭司划回了大船。其中一个，就是今早死在水手舱的那个水手。不到半个钟头前，我接到消息，另一个从索具上翻进了水里，在海中失踪了。这不是一趟好航程。人不该像赌局里的钱币一样白白折损。性命太贵重了。”
“明白了。”吉奥说，“多谢您的指点和时间，先生。”
“不必客气。”船长说。随后他转过身去。
吉奥重新下了梯子，慢慢朝前走。有什么东西碰了碰他的肩，他猛地回身。

===Original===
“Snake, God damn it, don’t do that!”
The boy looked embarrassed.
“I didn’t mean to yell,” Geo said, putting his arm around the boy’s shoulder. “Come on, though. What did you find? I’ll trade you what I know for what you do.”
You﻿ ﻿… sleep, came from Snake.
“I’m sorry, friend,” laughed Geo. “But I couldn’t take a nap now if you paid me. Now tell me, whose eyes were we seeing through last night? The captain’s?”
Snake shook his head.

===Chinese===
“蛇，该死的，别来这一套！”
男孩一脸窘迫。
“我不是有意吼你的。”吉奥说着，一只手臂搂住男孩的肩膀。“不过来吧。你探到了什么？我拿我知道的，换你知道的。”
你们……睡，蛇那边传来心声。
“对不住，朋友，”吉奥笑了，“可这会儿你给钱我也睡不着。告诉我，昨晚咱们借的是谁的眼睛？船长的？”
蛇摇了摇头。

===Original===
“The mate’s?”
Snake nodded.
“I thought so. Now, did he want to kill﻿ ﻿… wait a minute,” said Geo. “Can the mate read minds, too? Is that why you’re keeping things from us?”
Snake shrugged.
“Come on now,” Geo said. “Do a little yelling and explain.”
Don’t﻿ ﻿… know, Snake thought out loud. Can﻿ ﻿… see﻿ ﻿… what﻿ ﻿… he﻿ ﻿… sees﻿ ﻿… hear﻿ ﻿… what﻿ ﻿… he﻿ ﻿… hears. But﻿ ﻿… no﻿ ﻿… hear﻿ ﻿… thoughts﻿ ﻿…
“I see. Look, take a chance that he can’t read minds and tell me, did he kill the man in the bed you should have been in.”
Snake paused for a minute. Then nodded.

===Chinese===
“大副的？”
蛇点了点头。
“我就知道。那他想不想杀——慢着，”吉奥说，“大副是不是也会读人心？所以你才瞒着咱们？”
蛇耸了耸肩。
“来嘛，”吉奥说，“喊上两嗓子，解释解释。”
不……知道，蛇出声地想着。能……看见……他……看见的……听见……他……听见的。可……听不见……念头……
“明白了。听我说，赌一把他读不了心，告诉我：死在本该你睡的那张铺上的那个人，是不是他杀的？”
蛇顿了片刻。随后点头。

===Original===
“Do you think he was trying to kill you?”
Snake nodded again.
“Did you know that the man killed this morning in your place was one of the two men who came back from Aptor with the Priestess?”
Snake looked surprised.
“And that the other one drowned this morning, fell overboard, and was lost?”
Snake nearly jumped.
“What is it?”
Look﻿ ﻿… for﻿ ﻿… him﻿ ﻿… all﻿ ﻿… morning. He﻿ ﻿… not﻿ ﻿… dead﻿ ﻿… hear﻿ ﻿… thoughts﻿ ﻿… dim﻿ ﻿… low.

===Chinese===
“你觉得他要杀的是你？”
蛇又点了点头。
“你可知道，今早替你被杀的那个人，正是随女祭司从阿普托回来的两个人中的一个？”
蛇露出惊讶之色。
“还有，另一个今早淹死了——从船上翻进水里，没了踪影？”
蛇险些跳了起来。
“怎么了？”
找……他……整个……早上。他……没……死……听得见……念头……弱……低。

===Original===
“Who’s not dead?” Geo asked. “Which one?”
Second﻿ ﻿… man.
“Did you find him?” Geo asked.
Can’t﻿ ﻿… find, Snake said. But﻿ ﻿… alive﻿ ﻿… I﻿ ﻿… know.
“One other question,” Geo raised the jewel from where it hung against his chest. “How do you work this silly thing?”

===Chinese===
“谁没死？”吉奥问，“哪一个？”
第……二……个。
“你找到他了吗？”吉奥问。
找……不到，蛇说。可……活着……我……知道。
“还有一问。”吉奥把挂在胸前的珍宝托了起来。“这个傻玩意儿，你是怎么使的？”

===Original===
Think﻿ ﻿… through﻿ ﻿… it, said Snake.
Geo frowned. “What do you mean? Can you tell me how it works?”
You﻿ ﻿… have﻿ ﻿… no﻿ ﻿… words, Snake said. Radio﻿ ﻿… electricity﻿ ﻿… diode﻿ ﻿…
“Radio, electricity, diode?” repeated Geo, the sounds coming unfamiliarly to his tongue. “What are they?”
Snake shrugged.
---

===Chinese===
拿它……来……想，蛇说。
吉奥皱起眉头。“什么意思？能告诉我它是怎么运转的吗？”
你们……没有……那种……词，蛇说。收音机……电……二极管……
“收音机、电、二极管？”吉奥重复道，这些音节在他舌头上生涩得很。“它们是什么？”
蛇耸了耸肩。
---

===Original===
Geo got a chance to report his findings to Urson that evening and the big man was puzzled.
“Can you add anything?” Geo asked.
“All I’ve had a chance to do is work,” grumbled Urson. They were standing by the edge of the rail beyond which the mist steeped thickly, making sky and water indistinguishable and grave. “Hey, Four-arms,” Urson suddenly asked. “What are you looking at?”
Snake stared at the water but said nothing.
“Maybe he’s listening to something,” suggested Geo.
“You’d think there were better things to eavesdrop on than fishes,” said Urson. “I guess Argo’s given special orders that you two get no work. Some people! Let’s go eat.” As they started toward the convergence of sailors at the entrance of the mess hall, Urson said, “Oh, guess what?” He turned to Geo and picked up the jewel from the boy’s chest. “All you people are going around with such finery, I took my coins to the smithy and had him put chains on them. Now I’ll strut with the best of you.” He laughed, and then went through the narrow way, crowding with the other sailors into the wide hall.
---

===Chinese===
当天晚上，吉奥找着机会把自己探得的情况报给厄尔森，大汉听得满腹狐疑。
“你还能添上点什么吗？”吉奥问。
“我捞着的机会就只有干活。”厄尔森嘟囔道。两人站在栏杆边上，栏外的雾浓浓地泡着，天和水泡得难分难辨，一片沉沉。“喂，四臂，”厄尔森忽然问，“你在看什么？”
蛇盯着水面，一言不发。
“没准他在听什么。”吉奥提了一句。
“你倒想想，比鱼更值得偷听的东西有的是。”厄尔森说，“我看是阿戈下了特别的令，你们俩免了差事。有些人呐！吃饭去吧。”三人朝饭厅门口聚拢过去的水手们走，厄尔森说：“哦，你猜怎么着？”他转向吉奥，把那男孩胸前的珍宝托起来。“你们这帮人成天挂着这等阔气的玩意儿晃来晃去，我也把我的金币拿去铁匠铺，叫人给穿了链子。这下我也能同你们中间最阔气的一样招摇了。”他哈哈一笑，随即挤过那道窄口，同其余水手一起涌进宽敞的饭厅。
---

===Original===
For two weeks, nights without dreams left them early, and the boat rolled from beneath the fog. Dawn was gray, but clear; then, by one breakfast time the ragged slip of Aptor’s beach hemmed the horizon.
On the wheel deck the sailors clustered to the rail, and before them rocks struck like broken teeth from the water. Urson, in his new, triple neckchain, joined Snake and Geo at the rail. “Whew,” he said. “Getting through them is going to be fun.”
Suddenly heads turned. Behind them now, Argo’s dark veils, bloated with the breeze, filled about her as she mounted the steps to the wheel deck. The sailors moved away from her. Then, one hand on a stay rope, she stared across the gray water to the dark tongue of land.
From the wheel the captain spoke, “Jordde, disperse the men and take over the wheel.”
“Aye, sir,” said the mate. “You, you, and you to the tops.” He pointed among the men. “You also, and you. Hey, didn’t you hear me?”
“Me, sir?” Geo turned around.
“Yes, you, up to the top spar there.”

===Chinese===
此后两个星期，无梦的夜早早撇下他们离去，船从雾底下滚了出来。拂晓灰蒙蒙的，却晴朗；后来，到了某个早饭时分，阿普托海滩参差的窄边已在天边镶了一道线。
操舵甲板上，水手们聚在栏杆前，眼前的礁石像崩断的牙齿从水里戳出来。厄尔森挂着他新打的三串金链，到栏杆边同蛇和吉奥站作一处。“嚯，”他说，“从这些石头中间穿过去，可有得乐子了。”
忽然人头纷纷转动。这时阿戈正登上操舵甲板的阶梯，深色的面纱兜满微风，鼓胀着围拢在她周身。水手们纷纷避开她。随后，她一只手搭在支索上，凝目越过灰色的水面，望向那条深色的舌状陆地。
船长在舵轮旁开口：“约德（Jordde），把人遣散，接管舵轮。”
“是，先生。”大副应道，“你，你，还有你，上桅顶去。”他在人堆里点着。“你也去，还有你。喂，没听见我说话吗？”
“我吗，先生？”吉奥回过身。
“就是你，上那边顶桁去。”

===Original===
“You can’t send him up,” Urson called out. “He’s never been topside at all before. It’s too choppy for any lad’s first time up. He doesn’t even know﻿ ﻿…”
“And who asked you?” demanded the mate.
“Nobody asked me, sir,” said Urson, “but﻿—”
“Then you get below before I have you brigged for insubordination and fine you your three gold baubles. Don’t you think I recognize dead man’s gold?”
“Now look here,” Urson roared.
Geo glanced from Argo to the captain. The bewilderment that flooded the face of the Priestess shocked him.
Jordde suddenly seized up a marlin pin, raised it, and shouted at Urson, “Get down below before I break your skull open.”

===Chinese===
“你不能派他上去。”厄尔森喊道，“他从没上过桅顶。浪头这么颠，哪个小子头一回上都吃不消。他连……”
“谁问你了？”大副喝道。
“是没人问我，先生，”厄尔森说，“可是——”
“那你就趁我没以抗上之罪把你关进禁闭舱、罚没你那三件金宝贝之前，赶紧下舱去。你当我认不出死人身上的金子？”
“你给我站住说清楚，”厄尔森咆哮道。
吉奥的目光从阿戈扫向船长。女祭司脸上漫过的那阵惶惑错愕，令他震惊。
约德突然抄起一根索栓，高高举起，冲厄尔森吼道：“趁我没砸开你的脑壳，滚下舱去。”

===Original===
Urson’s fists sprang up.
“Calmly, brother bear,” Geo began.
“In a bitch’s ass,” snarled Urson and swung his huge arm forward. Something leaped on Jordde from behind﻿—Snake! The marlin pin veered inches away from Urson’s shoulder. The flung fist sunk into the mate’s stomach and he reeled forward, passing Urson, with Snake still clawing at his back. He reached the rail, bent double over it, and Snake’s legs flipped up. When Jordde rose, he was free of encumbrance.
Geo rushed to the edge and saw Snake’s head emerge in the churning water. Behind him, Urson yelled, “Look out!” Jordde’s marlin made an inch of splinters in the length of wood against which he had been leaning.
“Not him!” cried Argo. “No, no! Not him!”
But Jordde had seized Geo’s shoulder and whirled him back against the rail. Geo saw Urson grab a loose rope behind them and suddenly swing forward, intending to knock Jordde away with his feet. But suddenly Argo moved in the way of his flying body, turned, saw him, and raised her hands to push him aside so that he swung wide of them and landed on the railing a yard from where they struggled.
Geo’s feet slipped on the wet boards, and he felt his body suddenly hurled backwards onto the air. Then his back slapped water. As he broke surface, Urson, still on the rail called to him, “Hang on, friend Geo, I’m coming!” Urson’s arms swung back, and then forward as he dove into the sea.

===Chinese===
厄尔森的双拳腾地攥起。
“安静下来吧，熊兄弟。”吉奥起了个头。
“安静个屁。”厄尔森狞声骂道，抡起大臂就是一挥。有什么东西从背后扑上了约德——是蛇！索栓擦着厄尔森的肩膀偏开数寸。掷出的一拳深深砸进大副的肚子，他踉跄着朝前抢出，越过厄尔森，蛇还扒在他背上乱抓。他撞上栏杆，整个人对折着伏了下去，蛇的两条腿被掀上了天。等约德直起身来，身上已卸了累赘。
吉奥冲到栏杆边，看见蛇的脑袋从翻搅的水里冒出来。他背后，厄尔森大叫：“小心！”约德的索栓砸在他方才倚着的那根木料上，崩起一寸来长的木屑。
“不是他！”阿戈喊道，“不，不！不是他！”
可约德已经攥住吉奥的肩膀，把他猛地掼回栏杆上。吉奥看见厄尔森抄起身后一条松绳，猛然朝前荡去，打算用双脚把约德踹开。可阿戈突然挡进他飞荡的身形前，一转身看见了他，扬起双手把他推开，于是他荡偏了方向，落在离他们扭斗处一码远的栏杆上。
吉奥的脚在湿漉漉的船板上一滑，只觉身子猛然被向后抛到了空中。随后他的背拍在了水面上。他冒出头时，还攀在栏杆上的厄尔森朝他喊：“撑住，吉奥老友，我来了！”厄尔森双臂向后一扬，又向前一送，一头扎进了大海。

===Original===
Now Geo could see only Argo and Jordde at the rail. But they were struggling. Urson and Snake were near him in the water. The last thing he saw was Jordde suddenly wrest something from Argo’s neck and then fling it out into the sea. The Priestess’ hands reached for the flying jewel, followed its arc as she screamed toward the water.
Then hands were at his body. Geo turned in the water as Snake disappeared from beside him and Urson suddenly cried out. Hands were pulling him down.
Roughness of sand beneath one of his sides and the flare of sun on the other. His eyes were hot and his lids were orange over them. Then there was a breeze. He opened his eyes, and shut them quick, because of the light. Then he turned over, thought about pillows and stiff new sheets. Reaching out, he grabbed sand.
He opened his eyes and pushed himself up from the beach with both hands spread in warm, soft crumblings. Over there were rocks, and thick vegetation behind them. He swayed to his knees, the sand grating under his kneecaps. He looked at his arm in the sun, flecked with grains. Then he touched his chest.
His hand came to one bead, moved on, and came to another! He looked down. Both the chain with the platinum claw and the thong with the wire cage hung around his neck. Bewildered, he heaved to his feet, and immediately sat down again as the beach went red with the wash of blood behind his eyeballs. He got up again, slowly.

===Chinese===
此刻吉奥只望得见栏杆边的阿戈和约德。两人在扭打。厄尔森和蛇在水里，就在他近旁。他看到的最后一幕，是约德猛地从阿戈颈间夺下一件什么东西，扬手掷进了大海。女祭司的双手扑向那颗飞出去的珍宝，追着它的弧线，朝水面尖声惊叫。
随后，有手搭上了他的身体。吉奥在水中转过身，蛇从他身旁消失了，厄尔森突然叫出声来。那些手把他往下拽。
一侧身子底下是粗粝的沙，另一侧是炽亮的太阳。他的眼睛发烫，眼皮在眼前映成一片橙色。随后起了一阵风。
他睁开眼，又立刻闭上——光太强了。随后他翻了个身，恍恍惚惚想着枕头和浆得发硬的新床单。伸手一摸，抓到的是沙子。
他的手碰到一颗圆珠，继续往下，又碰到另一颗！他低头看去。白金爪座的链子和拴铁丝笼的皮绳，一齐挂在他的脖子上。他满心惶惑地撑起身来，可眼球后面涌起的血的潮浪霎时把海滩染成通红，他立刻又坐了下去。他又慢慢站了起来。

===Original===
Carefully Geo started down the beach, looking toward the land. When he turned to look at the water, he stopped.
At the horizon, beyond the rocks, was a boat with lowered sails. So they hadn’t left yet. He swung his eyes back to the beach: fifty feet away was another figure lying in the sun.
He ran forward, now, the sand splashing around his feet, sinking under his toes, so that it was like the slow motion running of dreams. Ten feet from the figure he stopped.
It was a young black, very dark, skin the color of richly humused soil. The long skull was shaved. Like Geo, he was almost naked. There was a clot of seaweed at his wrist, and the soles of his feet and one upturned palm were grayish and shriveled.
Geo frowned and stood for a full minute. He looked up and down the beach once more. There was no one else. Just then the man’s arm shifted across the sand.
Immediately Geo fell to his knees beside the figure, rolled him over and lifted his head. The eyes opened, squinted in the light, and the man said, “Who are you?”
“My name is Geo.”

===Chinese===
吉奥小心地沿岸滩走去，眼睛望着陆地那一面。待他转身望向水面，他站住了。
地平线上、礁石外面，泊着一条落了帆的船。这么说他们还没走。他把目光扫回海滩：五十英尺外，另有一个身影躺在阳光里。
这时他跑了起来，沙子在他脚边飞溅、在脚趾下塌陷，跑得活像梦里那种慢动作。离那身影十英尺，他停住了。
那是个年轻的黑人，肤色极深，皮肤是腐殖沃土的颜色。修长的头颅剃得精光。同吉奥一样，他几乎一丝不挂。手腕上缠着一团海草，脚底和一只朝上摊开的手掌泡得发灰、起了皱。
吉奥皱着眉，足足站了一分钟。他又把海滩上下打量了一遍。再没有别人。恰在这时，那人的手臂在沙上动了动。
吉奥立刻在那身影身旁跪下，把他翻过来，托起他的头。那人睁开眼，在光里眯着，开口道：“你是谁？”
“我叫吉奥。”

===Original===
The man sat up, and caught himself from falling forward by jamming his hands into the sand. He shook his head, and then looked up at Geo again. “Yes,” he said. “I remember you. What happened? Did we founder? Did the ship go down?”
“Remember me from where?” Geo asked.
“From the ship. You were on the ship, weren’t you?”
“I was on the ship,” Geo said. “And I got thrown overboard by that damned first mate in a fight. But nothing happened to the ship. It’s still out there, you can see it.” Suddenly Geo stopped. Then he said, “You’re the guy who discovered Whitey’s body that morning!”
“That’s right.” He shook his head again. “My name is Iimmi.” Now he looked out to the horizon. “I see them,” he said. “There’s the ship. But where are we?”
“On the beach of Aptor,” Geo told him.
Iimmi screwed his face up into a mask of dark horror. “No,” he said softly. “We couldn’t be. We were days away from her.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
那人坐起身，双手撑进沙里，才算没有向前栽倒。他摇了摇头，又抬眼望吉奥。“对了，”他说，“我记得你。出什么事了？我们失事了？船沉了？”
“在哪儿记得我的？”吉奥问。
“在船上啊。你当时在船上，对不对？”
“我是在船上，”吉奥说，“打架的时候，让那个该死的大副扔下了水。可船没出事。它还在那儿呢，你看得见。”吉奥忽然顿住。随后他说：“那天早上发现白佬尸体的，就是你！”
“没错。”他又摇了摇头。“我叫伊米（Iimmi）。”说着他望向地平线。“我看见了，”他说，“船在那儿。可我们这是在哪儿？”
“在阿普托的海滩上。”吉奥告诉他。
伊米把一张脸拧成了阴森可怖的面具。“不，”他轻声说，“不可能。我们离她还差着好几天呢……”

===Original===
“How did you fall in?”
“It was blowing up a little,” Iimmi explained. “I was in the rig when suddenly something struck me from behind and I went toppling. In all the mist, they didn’t see me, and the current was too strong for me, and﻿ ﻿…” He looked around.
“You’ve been on this beach once before, haven’t you?” Geo asked.
“Once,” said Iimmi. “Yes, once.”
“Do you realize how long you’ve been in the water?” Geo asked.
Iimmi looked up.
“Over two weeks,” Geo said. “Come on, see if you can walk. I’ve got a lot of things to explain, if I can, and we’ve got some hunting to do.”
Iimmi steadied himself once more, and together they started up the beach.

===Chinese===
“你是怎么落水的？”
“当时正起风，”伊米解释道，“我在索具上，忽然有什么东西从背后打了我一下，我便栽了下去。雾那么大，他们没瞧见我，水流又太急，我敌不过，后来……”他环顾四周。
“这片海滩你从前到过一回，是不是？”吉奥问。
“一回，”伊米说，“对，一回。”
“你知道你在水里泡了多久吗？”吉奥问。
伊米抬起头。
“两个多星期。”吉奥说。“来，看看你能不能走。我有好些事要解释——要是解释得清的话——而且咱们还有一摊搜寻的活儿要干。”
伊米又稳了稳身子，两人一道沿滩往上走去。

===Original===
“What are you looking for?” Iimmi asked.
“Friends,” Geo said.
Two hundred feet up, the rocks and torpid vegetation came down to the water, cutting off the beach. Scrambling over boulders and through vines, they emerged on a rock embankment that dropped fifteen feet into the wide estuary of a ribbon of water that wound back into the jungle. Twenty feet further, the bank dropped to the river’s surface, and they both fell flat at the edge of a wet table of rock and sucked in cool liquid, watching blue stones and the white and red pebbles shivering six feet below clear ripples.
There was a sound. Both sprang back from the water, turned, and crouched on the rock.
“Hey,” Urson said, through leaves. “I was wondering when I’d find you.”
Light through branches lay on the gold coins hung against his hairy chest. “Have you seen Snake?”

===Chinese===
“你们在找什么？”伊米问。
“找朋友。”吉奥说。
往上两百英尺，礁石和蔫沉沉的草木一路铺到水边，把海滩截断了。两人翻过大圆石、钻过藤蔓，登上一道石堤；堤下十五英尺，是一条宽阔的河口，一带流水蜿蜒着绕回丛林深处。再往前二十英尺，堤岸直落到河面，两人趴在一方湿漉漉的平石边缘，咕咚咕咚喝起清凉的水，望着六英尺下清澈涟漪里微微颤动的蓝色石子和红白卵石。
响起了一个声音。两人从水边弹开，转过身，蹲踞在石头上。
“嘿，”厄尔森的声音穿过树叶传来，“我正犯嘀咕什么时候能找着你们呢。”
透过枝叶漏下的光，落在他毛茸茸的胸脯前挂着的那几枚金币上。“见着蛇了吗？”

===Original===
“I was hoping he was with you,” said Geo. “Oh, Urson, this is Iimmi, the other sailor who died two weeks ago.”
Both Iimmi and Urson looked puzzled. “Have a drink of water,” Geo said, “and I’ll explain as best I can.”
“Don’t mind if I do,” said Urson.
While the bear man lay down to drink, Geo began the story of Aptor and Leptar for Iimmi. When he finished, Iimmi asked, “You mean those fish things in the water carried us here? Whose side are they on?”
“Apparently Argo isn’t sure either,” Geo said. “Perhaps they’re neutral.”
“And the mate?” asked Iimmi. “You think he pushed me overboard after he killed Whitey?”

===Chinese===
“我还指望他跟你在一块儿呢。”吉奥说。“哦，厄尔森，这位是伊米，两个星期前‘死了’的那位水手。”
伊米和厄尔森都露出困惑的神色。“先喝口水，”吉奥说，“我尽力给你们解释。”
“那我就不客气了。”厄尔森说。
狗熊般的汉子趴下去喝水，吉奥便对伊米讲起阿普托和莱普塔（Leptar）的始末。讲完了，伊米问：“你是说，是水里那些鱼东西把咱们送到这儿来的？他们算哪头的？”
“看样子阿戈也拿不准。”吉奥说，“没准他们是中立的。”
“那大副呢？”伊米问，“你觉得是他杀了白佬之后，又把我推下水去的？”

===Original===
“I thought you said he was trying to kill Snake,” said Urson, who had finished drinking.
“He was,” explained Geo. “He wanted to get rid of all three. Probably Snake first, and then Whitey and Iimmi. He wasn’t counting on our fishy friends, though. I think it was just luck that it was Whitey he got rather than Snake. If he can’t read minds, which I’m pretty sure he can’t, he probably overheard you assigning the bunks for us to sleep in, Urson. When he found out he had killed Whitey instead, it just urged him to get Iimmi out of the way more quickly.”
“I could easily have been pushed,” Iimmi agreed. “But I still don’t see why.”
“If there is a spy from Aptor on the ship, then Jordde is it,” said Geo. “The captain told me he had been to Aptor once before. It must have been then that he was enjoined into their forces. Iimmi, both you and Whitey had also been on Aptor’s shore, if only for a few hours. There must be something that Jordde learned from the island that he was afraid you might learn, something you might see. Something dangerous, dangerous for Aptor, something you might see just from being on the beach. Probably it was something you wouldn’t even recognize, something you’d maybe not see the significance of until much later. But probably something very obvious.”

===Chinese===
“我记得你说的是他要杀蛇。”这时喝完了水的厄尔森说。
“是要杀蛇。”吉奥解释道，“他想把三个都除掉。多半先杀蛇，然后是白佬和伊米。只是他没算到咱们那些鱼朋友。我看，死的成了白佬而不是蛇，纯属运气。他既然读不了人心——这一点我相当有把握——多半是偷听到你分派铺位了，厄尔森。等他发觉杀错成了白佬，就只催得他更急地要把伊米除掉。”
“要推我下去是轻而易举，”伊米承认道，“可我还是不明白图什么。”
“假如船上真有阿普托的奸细，那便只能是约德。”吉奥说，“船长告诉我，他从前到过一回阿普托。准就是那时候，他被收进了他们的势力。伊米，你和白佬也都踏上过阿普托的海岸，哪怕只待了几个钟头。约德必定在那座岛上知道了什么，生怕你们也知晓，生怕你们看见什么。一样危险的东西——对阿普托危险的东西，一样光是站在这海滩上就可能看见的东西。多半还是一样你们根本认不出来的东西，一样要过许久才咂摸出分量来的东西。可多半又是一样明晃晃摆在眼前的显眼东西。”

===Original===
Now Urson spoke. “What did happen when you were on Aptor? How were those ten men killed?”
Though the sun was warm, Iimmi shivered. He waited for a moment, and then he began. “We took a skiff out from the ship and managed to get through the rocks somehow. It was evening when we started and the moon, I remember, had risen just above the horizon, though the sky was still deep blue. ‘This light of the full moon is propitious to the White Goddess Argo,’ she said from her place at the bow of the boat. By the time we landed, the sky was black behind her, and the beach was all silvered by the light, up and down. Whitey and I were left to guard the skiff at the water’s edge, and sitting on the gunwales, shoulders hunched in the slight chill, we watched the others go up the beach, five and five, with Argo behind them.
“Suddenly there was a scream, and the first man fell. They came from the air like vultures. The moon was overhead by now, and a cloud of them darkened the white disk with their wings. They scurried after the fleeing men, over the sand. All we could really make out was a dark battling against the silver. There were swords raised in the white light, screams, and howls that nearly sent us back into the ocean. But Argo and a handful of those men left began to run toward the boat. They followed them down to the edge of the water, loping behind them, half flying, half running, hacking one after another down with swords. I saw one man fall forward and his head roll from his body while blood squirted ten feet along the sand, crimson under the moon. One actually caught at her veils, but she screamed and slipped from it into the water now, and climbed back into the boat, panting. You would think a woman would collapse, but no. She stood in the bow while we rowed our arms off. They would not come over the water, apparently, and somehow we managed to get the skiff back to the ship without foundering against the rocks.”

===Chinese===
这时厄尔森开口了。“你们在阿普托那回到底出了什么事？那十个人是怎么被杀的？”
太阳虽暖，伊米还是打了个寒噤。他等了片刻，才开口。“我们从大船上放下一只舢板，好歹从礁石中间挤了过去。动身时是傍晚，我记得月亮刚升上地平线，天还是深蓝色。‘满月的这道光，于白女神阿戈是吉兆。’她在船头自己的位置上说道。等我们登了岸，她身后的天已经黑透，海滩上上下下都被月光镀成了银色。白佬和我被留在水边看守舢板，我们坐在舷缘上，肩头在微微的寒意里缩着，望其他人往海滩上走，五人一排、五人一排，阿戈缀在他们身后。”
“忽然一声惨叫，头一个人栽倒了。他们像秃鹫一样从空中扑下来。这时月亮已经升到头顶，黑压压的一群用翅膀把那轮白盘都遮暗了。他们追逐奔逃的人，掠过沙面。我们真正看得清的，只有一团黑影在同银色搏斗。白光里刀剑高举，惨叫和长嚎几乎把我们吓得退回海里去。可阿戈和剩下的寥寥几人开始朝小船跑来。他们一路追到水边，在他们身后半扑半跑地纵跃，挥剑把人一个接一个砍翻。我看见一个人朝前扑倒，脑袋从身子上滚落，血顺着沙地喷出十英尺远，在月色下猩红刺目。有一个当真抓住了她的面纱，可她尖叫一声，从那东西爪里挣脱，滑进水里，随即气喘吁吁地爬回船中。换了谁都要以为一个女人这会儿早瘫倒了，可她没有。她立在船头，我们把手臂都快划断地猛划。他们显然不肯越过水面，而我们好歹没让舢板在礁石上撞散，把它弄回了大船。”

===Original===
“Our aquatic friends may have had something to do with that,” said Geo. “Iimmi, you say her veils were pulled off. Tell me, do you remember if she were wearing any jewelry or not?”
“She certainly wasn’t,” Iimmi said. “She stood there in only her dark robe, her throat as bare as ivory.”
“She wasn’t going to bring the jewel to Aptor where those monsters could get their hands on it again,” said Urson. “But Geo, if Jordde’s the spy, why did he throw the jewel in the sea?”
“Whatever reason he had,” said Geo, “our friends have given it to me now.”
“You said Argo didn’t know whose side these sea creatures were on, Leptar’s or Aptor’s,” said Iimmi. “But perhaps Jordde knows, and that’s why he threw it to them.” He paused for a moment. “Friend, I think you have made an error; you tell me you are a poet, and it is a poet’s error. The hinge in your argument that Snake is no spy is that Argo must have dubious motives to send you on such an impossible task, without protection, saying that it would be meaningful only if all its goals were accomplished. You reasoned, how could an honest woman place the life of her sister below the value of a jewel﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“咱们这些水族朋友，没准就在里头出了力。”吉奥说，“伊米，你说她的面纱被扯掉了。告诉我，你还记不记得，她身上戴没戴什么珠玉首饰？”
“她肯定没戴。”伊米说，“她站在那儿，只裹着一身深色长袍，脖颈光洁如象牙。”
“她原就不打算把珍宝带上阿普托，让那些怪物再把它抓到爪里。”厄尔森说，“可是吉奥，约德既然是奸细，他又为什么把珍宝扔进海里？”
“不管他存着什么缘故，”吉奥说，“如今咱们那些朋友已经把它交给了我。”
“你说过，阿戈不知道这些海里生灵站在哪一头，是莱普塔的，还是阿普托的，”伊米说，“可约德没准知道，所以他才把珍宝扔给他们。”他顿了片刻。“朋友，我看你犯了一个错误；你自己说你是诗人，这是诗人才会犯的错误。你断定蛇不是奸细，这论证的枢机在于：阿戈派你们来干这么一件办不到的差事，不给半点保护，还说唯有全部目的都达成，此事才算有意义——那她必是动机可疑。你当时推想，一个诚实的女人，怎会把妹妹的性命摆在一颗珍宝的价值之下……”

===Original===
“Not just her sister,” interrupted Geo, “but *the* Goddess Argo Incarnate.”
“Be patient,” said Iimmi. “Only if she wished to make permanent her temporary condition, you thought, could she set such an impossible task. There may be some truth in what you say. But she herself would not bring the jewel to the shores of Aptor, though it was for her own protection. Thanks to you, all three jewels are now in Aptor, and if any part of her story is true, Leptar is now in more danger than it has been in five hundred years. You have the jewels, two of them, and you cannot use them. Where is your friend Snake who can? Both Snake and Jordde could easily be spies and the enmity between them feigned, so that while you focused on one, you could be misled by the other. You say he can move into men’s minds? Perhaps he clouded yours.”
They sat silent for the lapsing of a minute.
“Argo may be torn by many things,” continued Iimmi. “But you, in watching some, may have been deluded by others.”
Light from the river quivered on the undersides of leaves. Urson spoke now. “I think his story is better than yours, Geo.”

===Chinese===
“不只是她妹妹，”吉奥打断道，“而是*阿戈女神在世的化身*。”
“耐心些。”伊米说，“你当时想，唯有她盼着把暂时的身份变为永久，才可能布置下这样一件办不到的差事。你的话兴许有几分道理。可她自己说什么也不肯把珍宝带上阿普托的海岸，哪怕那是为了她自身的安全。多亏了你，如今三颗珍宝全到了阿普托，而她的故事但凡有一部分是真的，莱普塔眼下的险境便是五百年来未有。珍宝在你手里，两颗，你却不会用它们。那个会使的朋友蛇又在哪儿？蛇和约德两个都大可以同为奸细，两人之间的仇怨也是装出来的，这样，你的心思盯死一个的时候，便容易被另一个引入歧途。你说他能钻进人的心里？没准他搅浑的正是你的心。”
一分钟悄悄流走，三人静坐无言。
“阿戈也许正被许多事撕扯着，”伊米接着说，“可你呢，提防着这一些人的时候，说不定早被另外一些人蒙蔽了。”
河面的光在叶子的背面颤动。厄尔森这时开了口：“我觉得他的说法比你的高明，吉奥。”

===Original===
“Then what shall we do now?” asked Geo, softly.
“Do what the Goddess requests as best we can,” said Iimmi. “Find the Temple of Hama, secure the stone, rescue the young Goddess, and die before we let the jewels fall into hands of Aptor.”
“From the way you describe this place,” muttered Urson, “that may not be far off.”
“Still,” mused Geo, “there are things that don’t mesh. Like why were you saved too, Iimmi? Why were we brought here at all? And why did Jordde want to kill you and the other sailor?”
“Perhaps,” said Iimmi, “the god Hama has a strange sense of humor and we shall be allowed to carry the jewels up to the temple door before we are slaughtered, dropping them at his feet.” He smiled. “Then again, perhaps your theory is the correct one, Geo, and I am the spy, sent to sway your reason.”
Urson and Geo glanced at each other.

===Chinese===
“那我们现在怎么办？”吉奥轻声问。
“尽力照女神的吩咐去做。”伊米说，“找到哈马（Hama）神庙，取得那颗石头，救出年轻的女神，宁死也不让珍宝落进阿普托之手。”
“照你形容这地方的口气，”厄尔森咕哝道，“那‘宁死’的日子怕是不远了。”
“不过，”吉奥沉吟道，“还有些环节扣不上。比如，为什么连你也给救了，伊米？为什么偏偏要把我们送到这儿来？约德又为什么要杀你和另一个水手？”
“没准，”伊米说，“哈马神有股古怪的幽默感，会容我们把珍宝一路捧到神庙门口，再把我们宰了，让珍宝跌落在他脚边。”他微微一笑。“再没准，你的推测才是对的，吉奥——奸细其实是我，被派来摇撼你的推理。”
厄尔森和吉奥对望了一眼。

===Original===
“There are an infinite number of theories for every set of facts,” said the Negro. “Rule number one: assume the simplest; that includes all the known conditions to be true until more conditions arise for which your theory no longer holds. Rule number two: then, and not until, change it.”
“Then we go on into the jungle,” Geo said.
“I guess we do,” said Urson.
“Since we’ve got this job, we’ve got to trust ourselves and do it right. Let’s see if we can put one more of those things around your neck before we’re through.” He pointed to the two jewels hanging at Geo’s chest. Then he laughed. “One more and you’ll be all the way up to me,” and he rattled his own triple necklace.

===Chinese===
“同一堆事实，能配出无穷多的理论。”那黑人说，“规矩一：采信最简单的那个；它把一切已知条件都视为真，直到冒出新条件、它罩不住了为止。规矩二：到那时——且得到那时——就换掉它。”
“那我们就进丛林去。”吉奥说。
“看来只好如此。”厄尔森说。
“既然揽了这桩差事，就得信得过自己，把它办得漂亮。瞧瞧等咱们收工之前，能不能再往你脖子上挂一颗那样的玩意儿。”他指了指吉奥胸前挂着的两颗珍宝。随后他笑了。“再挂一颗，你就一路追上我啦。”说着，他把自己那三串项链摇得哗啦作响。

## IX / 九、火山圣母

===Original===
Snake reached down, picked the beads up from Urson’s hand. The sound of wings had stopped.
“Where do we go now?” Urson asked.
“Follow the general rule, I guess,” said Iimmi. “Since we know Hama does have a temple somewhere, we try to find it, get the third jewel, and rescue Argo Incarnate. Then get back to the ship.”
“In three days?” asked Urson. They had related the rest of what they had found to him by now. “Well, where do we start looking?”

===Chinese===
蛇（Snake）俯身，从厄尔森（Urson）手里拾起那串珠子。振翅之声已经停了。

“现在咱们往哪儿走？”厄尔森问。

“我看还是照老规矩来，”伊米（Iimmi）说，“既然咱们知道哈马（Hama）在某处确实有一座神庙，咱们就设法找到它，取到第三颗珍宝，救出阿戈化身（Argo Incarnate）。然后回船上去。”

“三天之内？”厄尔森问。到这时，他们已把其余的发现都讲给他听了。“好吧，咱们从哪儿着手找？”

===Original===
“The Priestess said something about a band of Hama’s disciples behind the fire mountain. That must mean the volcano we saw from the steps in the City of New Hope.” Iimmi turned to Snake. “Did you read her mind enough to know if she was telling the truth?”
Snake nodded.
Iimmi paused for a moment. “Well, since the river is that way, we should head,” he turned and pointed, “… in that direction.”
They fixed their stride now and started through the moon-brushed foliage.

===Chinese===
“女祭司提起过，火山的后头有一伙哈马的门徒。那想必就是咱们在新望城（the City of New Hope）的台阶上望见的那座火山。”伊米转向蛇。“你把她的心思读得够不够，知道她说的是不是实话？”

蛇点了点头。

伊米顿了顿。“唔，既然河在那边，咱们就该朝——”他转身一指，“……那个方向走。”

此刻他们定下步子，穿过月光拂照的枝叶丛，上路了。

===Original===
“I still don’t understand what was going on back at the monastery,” Geo said. “Were they really priestesses of Argo? And what was Jordde doing?”
“I’d say yes on the first question, and guess that Jordde was a spy for them for an answer to the second.”
“But what about Argo﻿—I mean Argo on the ship?” asked Geo. “And what about Snake here?”
“Argo on the ship apparently doesn’t know about Argo on Aptor,” said Iimmi. “That’s what Jordde meant when he reported to the priestesses that she was bewildered. She probably thinks just like we did, that he’s Hama’s spy. And this one here,” he gestured to Snake, “I don’t know. I just don’t know.”

===Chinese===
“我还是不明白修道院里头到底是怎么回事，”吉奥（Geo）说，“她们真是阿戈（Argo）的女祭司吗？约德（Jordde）又是在干什么？”

“第一个问题，我说答案是肯定的；第二个问题，我猜约德是她们安插的奸细。”

“可是阿戈呢——我是说船上的阿戈？”吉奥问，“还有这里的蛇呢？”

“船上的阿戈显然不知道阿普托（Aptor）的这一位阿戈，”伊米说，“约德向那些女祭司禀报说她一脸茫然，说的就是这个。她多半跟咱们原先想的一样，当他是哈马的奸细。至于这一位嘛，”他朝蛇一摊手，“我就不知道了。我就是不知道。”

===Original===
In the distance was a red glow in which they could make out the faint lines of the volcano’s cone. Snake made lights with the jewels, and once more they began to pick their way over the terrain, barer and barer of vegetation. The earth became cindery and the air bore the acrid smell of old ashes.
Soon the rim of the crater hung close above them.
Iimmi gazed up at the red haze above them. “I wonder what it’s like to look into that thing in the middle of the night?” Twenty feet later Snake’s light struck a lava cliff that sheered up into the darkness. Going on beside it, they found a ledge that made an eighteen-inch footpath diagonally up the face.

===Chinese===
远处有一片红光，从中已能辨出火山锥朦胧的轮廓。蛇用珍宝照出光来，他们重新开始在这片草木越来越稀的土地上择路而行。泥土变得像炉渣，空气里带着陈年灰烬的酸辛味。

不多时，火山口的边缘已低低压在他们头顶。

伊米仰望头顶那片红霾。“不知道半夜里朝那东西望进去，会是个什么光景？”又走出二十英尺，蛇的光打在一面熔岩峭壁上，壁身陡然直上，没入黑暗。沿着壁根前行，他们发现一道岩脊，斜贯壁面，构成一条十八英寸宽的窄径。

===Original===
“We’re not going to climb that in the dark, are we?” asked Geo.
“Better than in the light,” said Urson. “This way you can’t see how far you have to fall.”
Thirty feet on, instead of petering out and forcing them to go back, the lip of rock broadened into a level stretch of ground and again they could go straight forward toward the red light above them.

===Chinese===
“咱们不是要摸黑爬那玩意儿吧？”吉奥问。

“总比亮堂堂的时候爬强，”厄尔森说，“这样你就看不见自己要跌多深。”

再走三十英尺，那道岩唇非但没有断头逼他们折返，反而放宽成一段平整的地面，他们又能径直朝头顶的红光走去了。

===Original===
“This is changeable country,” Urson muttered.
“Men change into animals,” said Geo, “jungles turn to mountains.” He reached around and felt the stub of his arm in the dark. “I’ve changed too, I guess.”
Iimmi recited:

===Chinese===
“这地方真说变就变。”厄尔森咕哝道。

“人变成了兽，”吉奥说，“丛林变成了山。”他伸手绕过去，在黑暗中摸了摸臂上的残桩。“我想，我也变了。”

伊米吟诵道：

===Original===
> “Change is neither merciful nor just.
 

 They say Leonard of Vinci put his trust
 

 in faulty paints: Christ’s Supper turned to dust.”

“What’s that from?” Geo asked.
“That’s one of my bits of original research,” Iimmi explained. “It comes from a poem dating back before the Great Fire.”

===Chinese===
> “变化既不仁慈，也不公正。
 

 据说文西的列奥纳德（Leonard of Vinci）轻信了
 

 有瑕的颜料：基督（Christ）的晚餐化作尘土。”

“这是打哪儿来的？”吉奥问。

“这是我自己的一点独门考据，”伊米解释道，“它出自一首诗，年代远在大火灾（the Great Fire）之前。”

===Original===
“Who was Leonard of Vinci?” Geo asked.
“An artist, another poet or painter, I suppose,” said Iimmi. “But I’m not really sure.”
“Who’s Christ?” Urson asked.
“Another god.”
---

===Chinese===
“文西的列奥纳德是谁？”吉奥问。

“一个艺术家吧，多半又是个诗人，或者画家，”伊米说，“不过我也说不准。”

“基督是谁？”厄尔森问。

“另一位神。”
---

===Original===
There were more rocks now, and Geo had to brace his stub against the walls of fissures and hoist himself up with his good hand. The igneous structures were sharp in his palm.
Through the night the glowing rim dropped toward them. With it came a breeze that pushed sulfa powder through their hair and made the edges of their nostrils sting.
The earth became scaley and rotten under their feet. Fatigue tied tiny knots high in their guts so that their stomachs hung like stones.

===Chinese===
如今岩石多了，吉奥只得把残臂抵住裂隙的壁面，用那只完好的手把自己撑上去。火成岩的棱角硌得他掌心生疼。

一夜之间，发亮的火山口缘朝他们压了下来。随之而来的一阵风，把硫磺粉末吹进他们的头发，刺得鼻翼生疼。

脚下的泥土变得鳞片般酥烂。疲乏在他们腹中高处打下一个个小结，胃便像坠了石头。

===Original===
“I didn’t realize how big the crater was,” Iimmi said. The red glow cut off at the bottom and took up a quarter of the sky.
“Maybe it’ll erupt on us,” Urson muttered. He added, “I’m thirsty.”
They climbed on. Once Urson looked back and saw Geo had stopped some twenty feet behind them at a niche in the ledge. He turned around and dropped back himself. There was sweat on the boy’s upturned face as the big man came toward him. He could see it in the red haze from the rim.

===Chinese===
“我原先没想到火山口有这么大，”伊米说。那片红光在底下截断，占了四分之一的天空。

“没准它会在咱们头顶喷发，”厄尔森咕哝道。又补了一句：“我渴了。”

他们继续攀爬。有一回厄尔森回头，看见吉奥落在后面二十来英尺，停在岩径的一处凹龛前。他自己也转身退了回去。大汉朝他走近时，少年仰起的脸上挂着汗珠——凭火山口缘映来的红霾，他看得清楚。

===Original===
“Here,” Urson said. “Give me a hand.”
“I can’t,” Geo said softly, “or I’ll fall.”
Urson reached down, now, caught the boy around the chest, and hoisted him over the cropping of rock. “Take it easy,” Urson instructed. “You don’t have to race with anybody.” Together they made their way after the others.

===Chinese===
“来，”厄尔森说，“把手给我。”

“不行，”吉奥低声说，“我会摔下去的。”

厄尔森这才探下身去，一把搂住少年的胸口，把他提过了那道突出的岩石。“放轻松点，”厄尔森叮嘱道，“你不用跟谁赛跑。”两人一同朝前面的人追去。

===Original===
Iimmi and Snake cleared the crater rim first; then Urson and Geo joined them on the pitted ledge. Together they looked into the volcano as red and yellow light fell over their chests and faces.
Gold dribbled the internal slope. Tongues of red rock lapped the sides, and the swirling white basin belched brown blobs of smoke which rose up the far rocks and spilled over the brim a radian away. Light leapt in wavering pylons of blue flame, then sank back into the pit. Winding trails of light webbed the crater’s walls, and at places ebon cavities jeweled among the light.
Wind fingered the watchers’ hair.
---

===Chinese===
伊米和蛇先翻上了火山口缘；随后厄尔森和吉奥也登了上来，站到坑坑洼洼的岩台上。四人一同朝火山里望去，红黄相间的光落在他们的胸口和脸上。

金浆顺着内坡滴淌。红岩的火舌舐着坡侧，白浪翻卷的熔盆喷出一团团棕色的烟，烟顺着对面的岩壁升上去，漫过一弧度外的口缘。光在摇曳的蓝焰塔柱里跳跃，随即又沉回深坑。蜿蜒的光迹在火山四壁上织成罗网，间或有些乌黑的洞窟，如宝石一般嵌在光网之间。

风拨弄着观火人的头发。
---

===Original===
Iimmi saw her first, two hundred feet along the rim. Her drapes, dyed red and orange in the flame, blew about her as she walked toward them. Iimmi pointed to her, and the others looked up.
As she neared, Geo saw that though she stood very straight, she was old. Her short white hair snapped at the side of her head in the warm breeze. Firelight and shadow fell deeply into the wrinkles of her face. As she approached them, light running like liquid down the side of her winded robe, she smiled and held out her hand.
“Who are you?” Geo suddenly asked.

===Chinese===
伊米第一个看见她，在口缘那头两百英尺开外。她的衣袂被火光染得红一阵橙一阵，在她朝他们走来时翻卷飘动。伊米指了指她，其余的人都抬眼望去。

她走近了，吉奥才看清：她身子挺得笔直，人却已经老了。短短的白发在暖风里贴着头侧翻飞。火光和暗影深深落进她脸上的皱纹。她朝众人走来，光像液体一样顺着风吹拂的袍子一侧流淌，她微笑着，伸出了手。

“你是谁？”吉奥突然问。

===Original===
> “Shadows melt in light of sacred laughter,
 

 Hands and houses shall be one hereafter.”

recited the woman in a calm, low voice.
She paused. “I am Argo Incarnate, of Leptar.”

===Chinese===
> “神圣欢笑之光照处，阴影消融。
 

 手与手、家与家，从此一体。”

女子用平静的低声吟诵道。

她顿了顿。“我是阿戈化身，来自莱普塔（Leptar）。”

===Original===
“But I thought﻿ ﻿…” Iimmi started.
“What did you think?” inquired the elderly woman, gently.
“Nothing,” said Iimmi.
“He thought you were a lot younger,” Urson said. “We’re supposed to take you home.” Suddenly he pointed in to the volcano. “Say, this isn’t any of that funny light like back in the city that burned our hands, only this time it made you old?”

===Chinese===
“可我原以为……”伊米开了口。

“以为什么呢？”老妇人温和地问。

“没什么。”伊米说。

“他以为你要年轻得多呢，”厄尔森说，“我们是来接你回家的。”他猛地指向火山里头。“哎，这总不会又是城里那种邪门的光吧？那回灼了咱们的手，这回倒把你变老了？”

===Original===
She glanced at the pool of light. “This is natural fire,” she assured them, “a severed artery of the earth’s burning blood. But wounds are natural enough.”
Geo shifted his feet and rubbed his stump.
“We were supposed to take the younger sister of the present Argo Incarnate and return with her to Leptar,” Iimmi explained.
“There are many Argos,” smiled the woman. “The Goddess has many faces. You have seen quite a few since you arrived in this land.”

===Chinese===
她瞥了一眼那片光泊。“这是天然的火，”她让他们放心，“是大地沸血的一条断脉。而伤口，本就是再自然不过的东西。”

吉奥挪了挪脚，揉了揉残臂。

“我们本是来接当今阿戈化身的妹妹、带她回莱普塔去的，”伊米解释道。

“阿戈有的是，”女人微笑道，“女神有许多副面孔。你们踏上这片土地以来，已经见过不少了。”

===Original===
“I guess we have,” Urson said.
“Are you a prisoner of Hama?” asked Iimmi.
“I am with Hama,” said the woman.
“We are supposed to secure the third jewel and bring it back to the ship. We don’t have much time.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“这个不假，”厄尔森说。

“你是哈马的囚徒吗？”伊米问。

“我与哈马同在，”女人说。

“我们得拿到第三颗珍宝，带回船上去。咱们时间不多了……”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Argo.
“Hey, what about that nest of vampires down there,” Urson said, thumbing viciously toward the black behind them. “They said they worshiped Argo. What have you got to do with them? I don’t trust anything on this place very much.”
“The nature of the Goddess is change,” said the woman, looking sadly toward the slope, “from birth, through life, to death,” she looked back up at them, “to birth again. As I said, Argo has many faces. You must be very tired.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”阿戈说。

“嘿，山下那窝吸血鬼算怎么回事，”厄尔森说着，狠狠朝身后的黑暗竖了竖大拇指，“她们说她们敬拜的是阿戈。你跟她们又是什么干系？这地方的东西，我一样也不大信得过。”

“女神的本性就是变化，”女人说着，哀伤地望向山坡，“从生，经生，到死，”她又抬眼看他们，“再到生。我说过了，阿戈有许多副面孔。你们一定累坏了。”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Geo.
“Then come with me. Please.” She turned, and began to walk back along the rim. Snake and Iimmi started after her, and then came Geo and Urson.
“I don’t like any of this,” the big man whispered to Geo as they came along. “Argo doesn’t mean the same thing in this land like she means on Leptar. There’s nothing but more evil to come out of this. She’s leading us into a trap, I tell you. I say the best thing to do is take the jewels we have, turn around, and get the hell out of here. I tell you, Geo.﻿ ﻿…”
“Urson,” Geo said.

===Chinese===
“是累坏了，”吉奥说。

“那就跟我来吧。请。”她转过身，沿着口缘往回走去。蛇和伊米先跟上，然后是吉奥和厄尔森。

“这里头的事我一件也不喜欢，”大汉边走边对吉奥耳语，“在这片土地上，阿戈跟她在莱普塔的意思不一样。这事除了更深的凶险，什么也出不来。她这是把咱们往陷阱里领，我跟你说。依我看，顶好是带上咱们手里的珍宝，掉转头，赶紧离开这个鬼地方。我跟你说啊，吉奥……”

“厄尔森，”吉奥说。

===Original===
“Huh?” the big man asked.
“Urson, I’m very tired.”
They walked silently for a few steps more. Then Urson heaved up a half disgusted breath, and put his arm around Geo’s shoulder. “Come on,” he grunted, supporting Geo against his own great form as they progressed along the rocky ledge, following the new Argo.

===Chinese===
“啊？”大汉问。

“厄尔森，我实在太累了。”

他们又默默走了几步。随后厄尔森半是懊恼地长吁一口气，把胳膊搭上吉奥的肩头。“走吧，”他咕哝一声，让吉奥倚着自己魁伟的身躯，沿着岩径前行，跟随这位新的阿戈。

===Original===
At last she turned down a trail that dropped into the crater. “Walk carefully here,” she said as they turned into the huge pit.
“Something is not right,” Urson said softly. “It’s a trap I tell you. How does that thing go? I could use it now. *Calmly brother bear﻿ ﻿…*”

===Chinese===
末了，她拐上一条陷进火山口的小径。“这里脚下当心，”他们转进这巨坑时她说。

“总有哪里不对劲，”厄尔森低声说，“我跟你说，这是个陷阱。那玩意儿怎么念来着？这会儿正用得上。*安睡吧，熊兄弟……*”

===Original===
> “Calm the winter sleep,
 

 Fire shall not harm,”

continued Geo.
“Says who,” mumbled Urson glancing into the bowl of flame. Geo went on:

===Chinese===
> “安度这冬眠。
 

 火不能伤，”

吉奥接着往下念。

“谁说的，”厄尔森咕哝一声，眼望那盆火。吉奥继续念：

===Original===
> “water not alarm.
 

 While the current grows,
 

 amber honey flows,
 

 golden salmon leap.”

“Like I once said before,” mused Urson, “in a﻿ ﻿…”
“In here,” came the voice of Argo. They turned into the dark mouth of one of the caves which pocked the crater’s inside wall. “No,” she said to Snake, who was about to use the jewels for illumination. “They have been used too much already.”

===Chinese===
> “水不能惊。
 

 洪流涨处，
 

 琥珀蜜淌，
 

 金鲑腾跃。”

“就像我从前说过的，”厄尔森沉吟道，“在那……”

“从这儿进，”传来阿戈的声音。他们转身钻进一个黑洞洞的洞口——这样的洞穴，在火山口的内壁上斑斑点点皆是。蛇正要用珍宝照明，她对他说：“不。它们用得太多了。”

===Original===
With a small stick taken from a pocket in her robe, she struck a flame against the rock, then raised it to an ornate, branching candelabra that hung from the stone ceiling by brass chains. Flame leapt from cast oil cup to oil cup, from the hand of a demon to a monkey’s mouth, from a nymph’s belly to the horns of a satyr’s head. Chemicals in the cups caused each flame to burn a different color; green, red, blue, and orange white light filled the small chapel and played across the tops of the benches. On the altar sitting on one side of the room were two statues of equal height: a man sitting, and a woman kneeling. Iimmi looked at the altar. Geo and Urson stared at the candelabra.
“What is it?” Iimmi asked when he saw where their eyes were fixed.
“There’s one of those things in Argo’s cabin on board the ship,” Geo said. “And look over there. Where did we see one of those before?” It was a machine with an opaque glass screen, identical to the one in the monastery of Argo.

===Chinese===
她从袍子口袋里取出一根小木条，在岩石上擦出火苗，然后举向一座华美的分枝烛台——它由黄铜链从石顶垂吊下来。火焰从一只只铸油盏跳向另一只，从一只魔怪的手跳进一张猿猴的嘴，从一位宁芙（nymph）的肚腹跳上一颗萨提尔（satyr）头颅的双角。油盏里的药剂让每一簇火焰烧出各不相同的颜色；绿的、红的、蓝的、橙白的光注满这间小圣堂，在长凳的凳面上游动。房间一侧的祭坛上并坐着两尊等高的塑像：一尊是坐着的男人，一尊是跪着的女人。伊米望着祭坛。吉奥和厄尔森则盯着那座烛台。

“那是怎么回事？”伊米见他们俩的目光钉在那里，便问。

“船上阿戈的舱房里就有一件这玩意儿，”吉奥说，“再瞧那边。咱们从前在哪儿也见过一台来着？”那是一台装着不透明玻璃屏的机器，同阿戈修道院里那台一模一样。

===Original===
“Sit down,” Argo said. “Sit down.”
They sank to the benches; the climb, once halted, knotting their calves and the low muscles on their backs.
“Hama has allowed you the privilege of a chapel even in captivity,” commented Iimmi, “but I see you have to share your altar with him.”
“But I am Hama’s mother,” smiled Argo.

===Chinese===
“坐吧，”阿戈说，“坐吧。”

他们跌坐到长凳上；攀爬一旦停下，痉挛便在小腿和背腰的深层肌肉里打了结。

“哈马倒仁至义尽，让你身陷囚禁还享用得上一间小圣堂，”伊米评道，“只是我看，你的祭坛还得跟他合用。”

“可我是哈马的母亲啊，”阿戈微笑道。

===Original===
Geo and Urson frowned.
“The rituals say that Argo is the mother of all things, the begetter and bearer of all life. I am the mother of all gods as well.”
“Those blind women down in the ground,” asked Urson, “they aren’t really your priestesses, are they? They wanted to kill us. I bet they were really dupes of Hama.”

===Chinese===
吉奥和厄尔森皱起了眉。

“仪典上说，阿戈是万物之母，是一切生命的孕育者和分娩者。我也是众神之母。”

“地底下那些瞎眼的女人，”厄尔森问，“她们总不至于真是你的女祭司吧？她们是要杀咱们的。我打赌，她们其实是哈马的傀儡。”

===Original===
“It isn’t so simple,” replied Argo. “They are really worshipers of Argo, but as I said, I have many faces. Death as well as life is my province. The dwellers in that convent from which you escaped are a﻿—how shall I say, a degenerate branch of the religion. They were truly blinded by the fall of the City of New Hope. To them, Argo is only death, the dominator of men. For not only is Argo the mother of Hama, she is his wife and daughter.”
“Then it’s like we figured,” said Iimmi. “Jordde isn’t a spy for Hama. He’s working for the renegade priestesses of Argo.”
“Yes,” returned Argo, “except that renegade is perhaps the wrong word. They believe that their way is correct, and a respect for belief is essential to the understanding of Man. And it is through understanding that the mysteries that still remain in your mind will be solved.”

===Chinese===
“没那么简单，”阿戈答道，“她们确实是敬拜阿戈的人，可我说过，我有许多副面孔。死与生一样，都归我管辖。你们逃出来的那座修女院（convent）里的住户，是一支——怎么说呢，一支蜕化了的教派。新望城的陷落真正弄瞎了她们的眼。在她们看来，阿戈唯有死亡，是主宰人的威权。因为阿戈不单是哈马的母亲，还是他的妻子和女儿。”

“那就跟咱们猜的一样了，”伊米说，“约德不是哈马的奸细。他是给阿戈的叛教女祭司们效力的。”

“是啊，”阿戈答道，“只是‘叛教’这个词也许用得不对。她们相信自己走的路才是正道，而尊重他人的信仰，是理解人类所必需的。你们心里至今悬着的那些谜，也要靠理解才能解开。”

===Original===
“Then they must be responsible for all that was going on in Leptar, only somehow blaming it on Hama,” said Iimmi. “They were probably just after the jewels, too. You don’t look like a prisoner. That must be the whole thing. You’re here in league with Hama to prevent the priestesses of Argo from taking over Leptar.”
“Nothing could be simpler,” said the Goddess. “Unfortunately you are wrong in nearly every other point.”
“But then why did Jordde throw the jewel after us when he tore it from Argo’s﻿—I mean the other Argo’s throat?”

===Chinese===
“那莱普塔那边闹的一切，多半也是她们干的，只是想法子栽到了哈马头上，”伊米说，“八成她们也是冲着珍宝来的。你看上去不像囚徒。事情必定就是这么回事：你跟哈马联手，好不让阿戈的女祭司们夺了莱普塔。”

“再简单不过了，”女神说，“可惜除此之外，你几乎句句都错。”

“那约德把珍宝从阿戈的——我是说另一位阿戈的喉咙上扯下来之后，为什么又朝咱们扔过来？”

===Original===
“When he snatched the jewel from around my daughter’s neck,” added Argo, “he threw it to the creatures of the sea because he knew they would take it back to Aptor. With it once again in the island, the priestesses would have a better chance of getting it; my daughter, acting Argo Incarnate in my absence and her sister’s, does not know that what she is fighting is another face of Argo. As far as she is concerned, all her efforts are against the mischief Hama has caused, and truly caused, in Leptar. This ignorance is far greater than you imagine, for beyond these blind creatures is a far greater enemy that she must vanquish.”
“Hama﻿ ﻿… ?” began Iimmi.
“Greater than Hama,” said old Argo. “It is herself. It is hard for me to watch her and not occasionally call out a word of guidance. With the science here in Aptor it would not be difficult. But I must refrain. I suppose she has actually done well. But there is so much more to do. She has directed you well, and assigned your tasks properly. And until now you have carried them out well.”

===Chinese===
“他从我女儿颈上抢走珍宝的时候，”阿戈补充道，“把它抛给了海里的生灵，因为他知道它们会把它带回阿普托。珍宝一回到岛上，女祭司们得手的机会就更大；我的女儿，在我不在、她妹妹也不在时暂代阿戈化身之职，并不知道她奋力抵抗的，是阿戈的另一副面孔。在她看来，她的全部心力，都用在对抗哈马在莱普塔惹下的祸患——那也确是实实在在惹下的祸患。这份无知远比你们想象的深重，因为在这些盲眼生灵之外，还有一个她必须制服的、强大得多的敌人。”

“哈马……？”伊米刚开口。

“比哈马更强大，”年迈的阿戈说，“就是她自己。我看着她，很难忍住不时喊出一句指点的言语。凭阿普托的这门学问，这本来不难。可我必须忍住。我想，她其实做得不错。但要做的事还多得很。她把你们指引得很好，差事也分派得妥当。而你们呢，直到如今，都完成得很好。”

===Original===
“She said we were to steal the final jewel from Hama and return with you to the ship,” said Geo. “Can you help us with either of these things?”
“The moment I compliment you,” laughed Argo, “you completely confuse your mission. Once the jewel is stolen, whom are you supposed to take back to Leptar?”
“Argo Incarnate,” Urson said.

===Chinese===
“她说我们要从哈马那里偷出最后一颗珍宝，然后带你回船上去，”吉奥说，“这两件事，你能帮上哪一件吗？”

“我刚夸完你们，”阿戈笑道，“你就把自己的使命搅了个稀里糊涂。珍宝到手之后，你们该带谁回莱普塔？”

“阿戈化身，”厄尔森说。

===Original===
“You said that Argo back in the ship was your daughter,” said Geo, “but she said you were her younger sister.”
“She said nothing of the sort,” Argo corrected. “I have two daughters. You have already met one. Now you must rescue the other. When my youngest daughter was﻿ ﻿… kidnapped here to Aptor, I was already here, waiting for her. Look.”
She turned a dial beneath the screen and lights flickered over the glass until they formed a sleeping figure. She had short red hair, a splash of freckles over a blunt nose, and her hand lay curled in a loose fist near her mouth. A white sheet covered the gentle push of adolescent breasts, and on the table beside her bed was a contraption made of a U-shaped piece of metal mounted on a board, an incomplete coil of wire, and a few more bits of metal, all sitting on top of a crumpled paper bag.

===Chinese===
“你说过船上的那位阿戈是你的女儿，”吉奥说，“可她说你是她妹妹。”

“她绝没有说过这样的话，”阿戈纠正道，“我有两个女儿。你们已经见过一个了。现在你们必须去救另一个。我最小的女儿被……绑架到阿普托来的时候，我已经先在这里等她了。看。”

她拧了拧屏下的旋钮，光点在玻璃上闪烁，最后凝成一个睡着的人影。她一头红色短发，塌鼻梁上洒着一片雀斑，一只手松松攥成拳，蜷在嘴边。白床单覆着少女胸脯轻轻的隆起；床边桌上摆着个拼凑的玩意儿：一块木板上装着个U形金属件、一圈没绕完的金属丝，还有几片碎金属，全都搁在一只揉皱的纸袋上。

===Original===
“That is my youngest daughter,” Argo said, switching off the picture. “She is the one you must take back to the ship.”
“How shall we steal the jewel?” asked Geo.
Argo turned to Snake. “I believe that was your task.” Then she looked around at the other three. “You will need rest. After that you can see about the jewel and my daughter. Come with me, now. Pallets have been set up for you in the far room where you may sleep.” She rose and led them to a further chamber. The blankets over the loose boughs seemed to pull them down. Argo pointed to a trickle of water that ran from a basin carved in the rock wall. “This stream is pure. You may drink from it.” She pointed to a cloth sack in the corner. “There is fruit in there if you become hungry.”

===Chinese===
“那就是我最小的女儿，”阿戈说着，关掉了画面。“你们要带回船上去的，就是她。”

“我们怎么偷珍宝？”吉奥问。

阿戈转向蛇。“我记得那是你的差事。”随后她又环视另外三人。“你们需要休息。之后再去操心珍宝和我的女儿。现在跟我来。里间已经给你们支好了铺位，可以睡。”她起身领他们走进更深的一间石室。铺在散乱枝条上的毯子，仿佛要把他们往下拽。阿戈指了指石壁上凿出的一方水盆里淌下的一线细流。“这泉水是净的。你们可以喝。”她又指了指角落里的一只布袋。“饿了的话，那里头有果子。”

===Original===
“Sleep!” said Urson, jammed his two fists in the air, and yawned.
As they settled, Argo said, “Poet?”
“Yes?” answered Geo.
“I know you are the tiredest, but I must talk to you alone for a moment or two.”

===Chinese===
“睡觉！”厄尔森说着，双拳朝天一举，打了个哈欠。

众人安顿下来时，阿戈唤道：“诗人？”

“嗯？”吉奥应道。

“我知道你最累，可我必须单独跟你谈上一两句。”

===Original===
As Geo raised himself, Urson stood up too. “Look,” he said to Argo, “he needs the rest more than any of us. If you want to question him about rituals and spells, take Iimmi. He knows just as much as Geo.”
“I need a poet,” smiled Argo, “not a student. I need one who has suffered as he has. Come.”
“Wait,” Urson said. He picked the jewel from Geo’s chest where Snake had returned it when they entered the chapel. “You better leave this with me.”
Geo frowned.

===Chinese===
吉奥撑起身子，厄尔森也站了起来。“喂，”他对阿戈说，“他比咱们谁都更需要休息。你要是想问他仪典和咒文，带伊米去。他懂得不比吉奥少。”

“我要的是诗人，”阿戈微笑道，“不是学者。我要一个像他那样吃过苦的人。来吧。”

“慢着，”厄尔森说。他从吉奥胸口拿起那颗珍宝——进圣堂时蛇刚把它归还给他。“这个你最好留在我这儿。”

吉奥皱起眉。

===Original===
“It still may be a trap,” said Urson.
“Leave it with him,” suggested Argo, “if it eases him.”
Geo let the great hand lift the thong from his neck.
“Now come with me,” said Argo.

===Chinese===
“这说不定仍是个陷阱，”厄尔森说。

“就留在他那儿吧，”阿戈提议道，“要是这样能让他安心。”

吉奥由着那只大手从自己颈上解下了细索。

“这就跟我来吧，”阿戈说。

===Original===
They left the room and walked back through the chapel to the door. Argo stood in the entrance, looking down at the molten rock. The light sifted through her robe, leaving the darker outline of her body. Without turning, she began to speak. “The fire is a splendid symbol for life, do you agree?”
“And for death,” said Geo. “One of Aptor’s fires burned my arm away.”
“Yes,” she turned now. “You and Snake have had the hardest time. Both of you have left your flesh to rot in Aptor. I guess that gives you a closeness to the land.” She paused. “You know, he had a great deal more pain than you. Do you know how he lost his tongue? I watched it all from this same screen inside the chapel, and could not help. They jammed their knuckles in his jaws and when the mouth came open, Jordde caught the red flesh with pincers that closed all the way through, and stretched it out as far as it would go. Then he looped the tongue with a thin wire, and then he threw a switch. You do not know what electricity is, do you?”

===Chinese===
他们出了石室，穿过小圣堂走回门口。阿戈立在门洞里，俯视着底下熔融的岩石。光从她的袍子间筛落，勾出她身子更暗的轮廓。她没有回头，开口说道：“火是生命一个绝妙的象征，你同意吗？”

“也是死亡的，”吉奥说，“阿普托的一蓬火，烧掉了我的胳膊。”

“是啊，”她这才转过身来。“你和蛇吃的苦头最深。你们俩都把血肉留在了阿普托腐烂。我想，这倒叫你们跟这片土地亲近了几分。”她顿了顿。“你可知道，他受的苦比你要多得多。你知道他的舌头是怎么没的吗？我就在这间圣堂里，从这同一面屏上望见了全程，却帮不上忙。他们把指节抵进他的牙关，嘴巴一张开，约德就用钳子夹住那红色的肉，钳齿一直咬穿到底，再把它抻到不能再长。然后他用一根细金属丝套住舌头，随后扳下了闸刀。你们不知道电是什么，对吧？”

===Original===
“I have heard the word.”
“Let me just say that when a great deal of it is passed through a thin wire, the wire becomes very hot, white hot. And the white hot loop was tautened until the rope of muscle seared away and just the roasted stump was left. But the child had fainted already. I wonder if the young can really bear more pain than older people.”
“Jordde and the blind priestess did that to him?”
“Jordde and some men on the boat that picked up the two of them from the raft on which they had left Aptor.”

===Chinese===
“这个词我听说过。”

“那我这么说吧：大量的电通过一根细金属丝时，金属丝会变得滚烫，白热。那道白热的线圈被绷紧，直到那条肌肉的绳索烙断，只剩一截烧焦的残桩。其实那孩子早就晕死过去了。我不知道，年轻人是不是真的比上年纪的人更熬得住痛。”

“是约德和那个瞎眼女祭司对他下的手？”

“是约德，还有那条船上的几个人——那条船把他们俩从离开阿普托的木筏上救了起来。”

===Original===
“Who is Jordde?” Geo asked. “Urson knew him before this as a first mate. But Urson’s story told me nothing.”
“I know the story,” Argo said, “and it tells you something, but something you would perhaps rather not know.” She sighed. “Poet, how well do you know yourself?”
“What do you mean?” Geo asked.
“How well do you know the workings of a man, how he manages to function? That is what you will sing of if your songs are to become great.”

===Chinese===
“约德到底是谁？”吉奥问，“厄尔森早先就知道他，是个大副。可厄尔森讲的那段事，什么也没告诉我。”

“我知道那段事，”阿戈说，“它倒也告诉了你一点什么——只是那一点，你也许宁愿不知道。”她叹了口气。“诗人，你有多了解你自己？”

“这话什么意思？”吉奥问。

“你有多了解一个人的运转，了解他是怎么撑着自己活下去的？你的歌若要成为伟大的歌，唱的就该是这个。”

===Original===
“I still don’t﻿ ﻿…”
“I have a question for you, a poetic riddle. Will you try to answer it?”
“If you will answer a not too poetic riddle for me.”
“Will you do your best to answer mine?” Argo asked.

===Chinese===
“我还是不明……”

“我有个问题问你，一个诗谜。你愿意试着答答看吗？”

“只要你也肯回答我一个不太诗意的谜。”

“你会尽力回答我的谜吗？”阿戈问。

===Original===
“Yes.”
“Then I will do my best to answer yours. What is your question?”
“Who is Jordde and why is he doing what he’s doing?”
“He was at one time,” Argo explained, “a very promising novice for the priesthood of Argo in Leptar, as well as a scholar of myths and rituals like Iimmi and yourself. He also took to the sea to learn of the world, but his boat was wrecked, and he and a few others were cast on Aptor’s shore. They strove with Aptor’s terrors as you did, and many succumbed. Two, however, a four-armed cabin boy whom you call Snake, and Jordde were each exposed to the forces of Argo and Hama as you have been. One, in his strangeness, could see into men’s minds. The other could not. Silently, one swore allegiance to one force, while one swore allegiance to the other. The second part of your question was *why*. Perhaps if you can answer my riddle, you can answer that part yourself. I do know that they were the only two who escaped. I do know that Snake would not tell Jordde his choice, and that Jordde tried to convince the child to follow him. When they were rescued, I know that the argument continued, and that Snake held back with childish tenacity both his decision and his ability to read minds, even under the hot wire and the pincers. The hot wire, incidentally, was something Jordde brought with him from the blind priestesses, according to him, to help the people of Leptar with. It could have been a great use. But recently all he has done with the electricity is construct a larger weapon with it. However, Jordde became a staunch first mate in a year’s time. Snake became a waterfront thief. Both waited. Then, when the opportunity arose, both acted. Why? Perhaps you can tell me, poet.”

===Chinese===
“会。”

“那我也尽力回答你的。你的问题是什么？”

“约德是谁，他做的这一切又是为了什么？”

“他曾经，”阿戈解释道，“是莱普塔阿戈祭司团里一个前程远大的见习修士，也像伊米和你一样，是个研究神话与仪典的学者。他同样出海去见识世界，可他的船失事了，他和另外几个人被抛上了阿普托的海岸。他们像你们一样同阿普托的恐怖搏斗，许多人折损了。然而有两个人——一个被你们唤作蛇的四臂见习水手，一个就是约德——像你们一样，分别经受了阿戈与哈马两股力量的触及。一个，凭着他的异禀，能看透人心。另一个不能。一个默默向一股力量宣誓效忠，另一个则向另一股宣誓。你问题的后半截是*为什么*。也许你能解了我的谜，也就自己解开了那一半。我确知，他们俩是仅有的两个逃出来的人。我确知，蛇不肯把自己的抉择告诉约德，而约德曾设法说服那孩子跟随他。获救之后，我知道那场争执还在继续，蛇怀着孩子式的顽强，把自己的决定和读心的本事一并咬住不放——哪怕在热线和钳子底下。顺带一说，那热线，据约德自己讲，是他从盲眼女祭司们那里带来的，本是要用来帮助莱普塔的百姓。它本可以派上大用场。可近来他用那电做的全部事，不过是造出一件更大的武器。总之，不过一年工夫，约德成了一名忠实可靠的大副。蛇成了滨水区的小偷。两个都在等。后来时机一到，两个都动了手。为什么？也许你说得出，诗人。”

===Original===
“Thank you for telling me what you know,” Geo said. “What is your question?”
She glanced at the flame through the door once more and then recited:

===Chinese===
“谢谢你把你知道的告诉我，”吉奥说，“你的谜是什么？”

她又望了一眼门外的火光，随即吟诵道：

===Original===
> “By the dark chamber sits its twin,
 

 where the body’s floods begin;
 

 and the two are twinned again,
 

 turning out and turning in.
> In the bright chamber runs the line
 

 of the division, silver, fine,
 

 diminishing along the lanes
 

 of memory to an inward sign.
> Fear floods in the turning room;
 

 Love breaks in the burning dome.”

“It is not one that I have heard before,” Geo said. “I’m not even sure I know what the question is. I’m familiar with neither its diction nor style.”

===Chinese===
> “幽暗的居室旁，坐着它的孪生，
 

 人身的洪流由此发端；
 

 这两者再度结成孪生，
 

 一个向外转，一个向内转。
> 明亮的居室里行着一道线，
 

 那是分界，银亮，纤细，
 

 沿着一条条记忆的巷道渐渐隐没，
 

 化作一枚向内的记认。
> 恐惧涌入旋转的房间；
 

 爱撞破燃烧的穹顶。”

“这首我从没听过，”吉奥说，“我甚至拿不准它问的是什么。它的措辞和格调，我都不熟。”
===Original===
“I doubted very much that you would recognize it,” smiled Argo.
“Is it part of the pre-purge rituals of Argo?”
“It was written by my youngest daughter,” Argo said. “The question is, can you explain it?”
“Oh,” said Geo. “I didn’t realize.﻿ ﻿…” He paused. “By the dark chamber sits its twin, moving in and out; and that’s where the floods of the body begin. And it’s twinned again. The heart?” he suggested. “The four-chambered human heart? That’s where the body’s flood begins.”

===Chinese===
“我就料到你多半认不出来，”阿戈微笑道。

“它是阿戈清洗前仪典里的一篇吗？”

“它是我最小的女儿写的，”阿戈说，“问题在于：你能解它吗？”

“哦，”吉奥说，“我没料到……”他顿了顿。“幽暗的居室旁坐着它的孪生，一出一进；人身的洪流便由此发端。而且还再度结成孪生。心脏？”他试着说，“人那颗四个心腔的心脏？人身的洪流就是从那儿发端的。”
===Original===
“I think that will do for part of the answer.”
“The bright chamber,” mused Geo. “The burning dome. The human mind, I guess. The line of division, running down the lane of memory﻿—I’m not sure.”
“You seem to be doing fairly well.”

===Chinese===
“我想，这够得上答案的一部分了。”

“明亮的居室，”吉奥沉吟道，“燃烧的穹顶。我猜，是人的心灵。那道分界线，沿着记忆的巷道一路向下——我说不准。”

“你好像解得相当不错。”
===Original===
“Could it refer to something like ‘the two sides of every question’?” Geo asked. “Or something similar?”
“It could,” Argo said, “though I must confess I hadn’t thought of it in that way. But it is the last two lines that puzzle me.”

===Chinese===
“它会不会是指诸如‘每个问题的两面’这类意思？”吉奥问，“或者类似的东西？”

“也许吧，”阿戈说，“不过我得承认，我没想到过那一层。”
===Original===
“*Fear floods in the turning room*,” repeated Geo; “*Love breaks in the burning dome.* I guess that’s the mind and the heart again. You usually think of love with the heart, and fear with the mind. Maybe she meant that they both, the heart and the mind, have control over both love and fear.”
“Perhaps she did,” Argo smiled. “You must ask her﻿—when you rescue her from the clutches of Hama.”
Before turning back to the room with his companions, he looked once more out at the fires of the volcano. Light whirled white and red. Blue tongues licked at black rock siding. He turned away now and went back into the darkness.

===Chinese===
“可真正让我困惑的，是最后那两句。”“*恐惧涌入旋转的房间*，”吉奥复述道，“*爱撞破燃烧的穹顶。*我猜，这说的还是心灵与心脏。人通常把爱联想给心脏，把恐惧联想给心灵。也许她的意思是：这两者，心脏与心灵，对爱与恐惧都有主宰之力。”

“也许是这样，”阿戈微笑道，“你得亲自问她——等你把她从哈马的魔掌里救出来的时候。”

在转回同伴们所在的石室之前，他又朝火山的烈焰望了一眼。白光与红光回旋。蓝色的火舌舔着黑色的岩壁。他这才转过身，走回黑暗里去。

## Prologue / 序幕

===Original===
Afterwards, she was taken down to the sea.
She didn’t feel too well, so she sat on a rock down where the sand was wet and scrunched her bare toes in and out of the cool surface.
She turned away, looked toward the water, and hunched her shoulders a little. “I think it was awful,” she said. “I think it was pretty terrible. Why did you show it to me? He was just a little boy. What reason could they have possibly had for doing that to him?”

===Chinese===
后来，她被人带到了海边。

她身子不太舒服，便在下面湿沙处的一块岩石上坐下，光着的脚趾在凉浸浸的沙面上踩进去、拔出来。

她转过脸去，望着水面，微微耸起肩膀。“我觉得那太可怕了，”她说，“我觉得那真是骇人。你为什么要放给我看？他不过是个小男孩。他们那样对他，能有什么理由呢？”

===Original===
“It was just a film,” he said. “We showed it to you so you would learn.”
“But it was a film of something that really happened.”
“It happened several years ago, several hundred miles away.”
“But it did happen; you used a tight beam to spy on them, and when the image came in on the vision screen, you made a film of it, and﻿—But why did you show it to me?”
“What have we been teaching you?”

===Chinese===
“那不过是一部影片，”他说，“放给你看，是为了让你学习。”

“可它拍的是真实发生过的事。”

“那是几年前的情形了，离这儿几百英里。”

“可它到底发生过；你们用窄波束窥探他们，影像一传到显像屏上，你们就把它录成了影片，还有——可你为什么偏偏放给我看？”

“我们一直在教你的是什么？”

===Original===
But she couldn’t think, and only had the picture in her mind, vivid movements, scarlets, and bright agony. “He was just a child,” she said. “He couldn’t have been more than eleven or twelve.”
“You are just a child,” he said. “You are not sixteen yet.”
“What was I supposed to learn?”
“Look around you,” he said. “You should see something.”
But the picture in her mind was still too vivid, too bright.

===Chinese===
可她想不下去，脑海里只剩下那幅画面：鲜明的动作，猩红，明晃晃的痛苦。“他只是个孩子，”她说，“顶多不过十一二岁。”

“你也只是个孩子，”他说，“你还不到十六岁。”

“那我究竟该学到什么？”

“看看你的四周，”他说，“你应该能看出点什么。”

可她脑海里那幅画面还是太鲜明、太刺眼。

===Original===
“You should be able to learn it right here on this beach, in the trees back there, in the rocks, in the bleached shells around your feet. You do see it; you just don’t recognize it.” Suddenly he changed his tone. “Actually you’re a very fine student. You learn quickly. Do you remember anything about telepathy? You studied it months ago.”
“ ‘By a method similar to radio broadcast and reception,’ ” she recited, “ ‘the synapse patterns of conscious thoughts are read from one cranial cortex and duplicated in another, resulting in similar sensual impressions experienced﻿—’ ” Suddenly she broke off. “But I can’t do it, so it doesn’t help me any!”
“What about history, then?” he said. “You did extremely well during the examination. What good does knowing about all the happenings in the world before and after the Great Fire do you?”

===Chinese===
“就在这片海滩上、在后面那片树林里、在岩石间、在你脚边那些漂白的贝壳上，你本该学得到它。你其实看得见，只是认不出来罢了。”他忽然换了口气。“其实你是个很不错的学生，学得快。心灵感应（telepathy）还记得多少？几个月前你学过的。”

“‘以类似无线电广播与接收的方法，’”她背诵道，“‘可将自觉思维的突触图谱从一个大脑皮层读出，复制到另一个之中，从而产生相似的感官印象——’”她突然刹住了。“可我做不到，所以它一点也帮不上我！”
