“那么历史呢？”他说，“那次考试你考得极好。知道大火灾（the Great Fire）之前与之后世上发生的种种事情，对你有什么用处？”

===Original===
“Well, it’s﻿ ﻿…” she started. “It’s just interesting.”
“The film you saw,” he said, “was, in a way, history. That is, it happened in the past.”
“But it was so﻿—” Again she stopped. “﻿—horrible!”
“Does history fascinate you because it’s just interesting?” he asked. “Or does it do something else? Don’t you ever want to know what the reason is behind some of the things these people do in the pages of the books?”
“Yes, I want to know the reasons,” she said. “Like I want to know the reason they nailed that man to the oaken cross. I want to know why they did that to him.”

===Chinese===
“这个嘛，它是……”她起了个头，“就是觉得有意思。”

“你看到的那部影片，”他说，“从某种意义上说，就是历史。也就是说，它发生在过去。”

“可它是那么——”她又停住了。“——可怕！”

“历史吸引你，仅仅因为它有意思吗？”他问，“还是它另有一点什么？你就从不想知道，书页里那些人做的种种事情背后，究竟是什么样的缘由？”

“想，我想知道那些缘由，”她说，“就像我想知道他们为什么把那个人钉上橡木十字架。我想知道他们为什么那样对他。”

===Original===
“A good question,” he mused. “Which reminds me, at about the same time as they were nailing him to that cross, it was decided in China that the forces of the universe were to be represented by a circle, half black, half white. But to remind themselves that there was no pure force, no purely unique reason, they put a spot of white paint in the black half and a spot of black paint in the white. Isn’t that interesting?”
She looked at him and wondered how he had gotten from one to the other. But he was going on.
“And do you remember the goldsmith, the lover, how he recorded in his autobiography that at age four, he and his father saw the Fabulous Salamander on their hearth by the fire; and his father suddenly smacked the boy ten feet across the room into a rack of kettles, saying something to the effect that little Cellini was too young to remember the incident unless some pain accompanied it.”
“I remember that story,” she said. “And I remember that Cellini said that he wasn’t sure if the smack was the reason he remembered the Salamander, or the Salamander the reason he remembered the smack.”

===Chinese===
“问得好，”他沉吟道，“这倒叫我想起，大约就在他们把那个人钉上十字架的同一时期，中国有人定下：宇宙的种种力量，要用一个圆来表示，半黑，半白。但为了提醒自己世上没有纯粹的力量，没有独一无二、纯而又纯的缘由，他们又在黑的那半里点了一粒白，白的那半里点了一粒黑。这岂不有趣？”

她望着他，纳闷他怎么从一个话题跳到了另一个。他却已经接着讲了下去。

“你还记得那个金匠、那个情人吧，他在自传里记下：四岁那年，他和父亲看见神奇的火蜥蜴（Salamander）蹲在他们炉火旁的灶台上；父亲突然一巴掌把他扇得横飞十英尺，撞进一架子水壶里，大意是说，小切利尼（Cellini）年纪太小，若不配上点疼痛，是记不住这件事的。”

“我记得那个故事，”她说，“我还记得，切利尼说过，他拿不准究竟是那一巴掌让他记住了火蜥蜴，还是火蜥蜴让他记住了那一巴掌。”

===Original===
“Yes, yes!” he cried. “That’s it. The reason, the reasons﻿ ﻿… Don’t you see the pattern?”
“Only I don’t know what a Salamander is,” she told him.
“Well, it’s like the blue lizards that sing outside your window sometimes,” he explained. “Only it isn’t blue, and it doesn’t sing.”
“Then why should anyone want to remember it?” she grinned. It was an attempt to annoy him, but he was not looking at her, and was talking of something else.
“And the painter,” he was saying, “he was a friend of Cellini, you remember, in Florence. He was painting a picture of *La Gioconda*. As a matter of fact, he had to take time from the already crumbling picture of *The Last Supper* of the man who was nailed to the cross of oak to paint her. And he put a smile on her face of which men asked for centuries, ‘What is the reason she smiles so strangely?’ Yes, the reason, don’t you see? Just look around.”

===Chinese===
“对，对！”他叫道，“就是这个。缘由，种种缘由……你难道看不出这个模式吗？”

“只是我不知道火蜥蜴是什么。”她告诉他。

“嗯，它就像有时在你窗外唱歌的那种蓝色蜥蜴，”他解释道，“只不过它不是蓝的，也不唱歌。”

“那谁还费劲去记它？”她咧嘴一笑。她是存心惹恼他，可他并没有看她，已经说起了别的。

“还有那位画家，”他正说着，“他是切利尼的朋友，你记得的，在佛罗伦萨（Florence）。他当时在画一幅《拉·乔孔达》（*La Gioconda*）。说起来，他是从那幅那时便已开始剥落的《最后的晚餐》（*The Last Supper*）——被钉上橡木十字架的那个人画的——里匀出时间来画她的。他在她脸上添了一抹微笑，几个世纪以来，人们一直问：‘她笑得这么奇异，缘由是什么？’是的，缘由，你明白吗？只管看看四周。”

===Original===
“What about the Great Fire?” she asked. “When they dropped flames from the skies and the harbors boiled, that was reasonless. That was like what they did to that boy.”
“Oh no,” he said to her. “Not reasonless. True, when the Great Fire came, people all over the earth screamed, ‘Why? Why? How can man do this to man? What is the reason?’ But just look around you, right here. On this beach.”
“I guess I can’t see it yet,” she said. “I can just see what they did to him, and it was awful.”
“Well,” said the man in the dark robe, “perhaps when you stop seeing what they did so vividly, you will start seeing why they did it. I think it’s time for us to go back now.”

===Chinese===
“那大火灾呢？”她问，“他们从天上掷下火焰，海港都沸腾了，那总没有缘由了吧。那就跟他们对那孩子干的事一样。”

“哦不，”他对她说，“并非没有缘由。不错，大火灾降临时，全世界的人都在尖叫：‘为什么？为什么？人怎么能这样对待人？缘由到底是什么？’可你只管看看你的周围，就在这儿。在这片海滩上。”

“我想我还看不见它，”她说，“我只看得见他们对他做了什么，而那太可怕了。”

“唔，”黑袍男子说，“也许等你不那么逼真地看见他们做了什么，你就会开始看见他们为什么那样做了。我想，我们该回去了。”

===Original===
As she slid off the rock and started walking beside him, barefooted in the sand, she asked, “That boy﻿—I wasn’t sure, he was all tied up, but he had four arms, didn’t he?”
“He did.”
“You know, I can’t just go around saying it was awful. I think I’m going to write a poem. Or make something. Or both. I’ve got to get it out of my head.”
“That wouldn’t be a bad idea,” he mumbled as they approached the trees in front of the river. “Not at all.”
And several days later, and several hundred miles away﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
她从岩石上滑下来，赤着脚在沙地上走在他旁边，问道：“那个孩子——我先前拿不准，他当时被捆得结结实实的——他有四条胳膊，对不对？”

“他有。”

“你知道，我不能光是逢人便说那有多可怕。我想我要写一首诗。或者做点什么。或者两样都来。我必须把它从脑子里倒出去。”

“这主意不坏，”他们走近河前那片树林时，他咕哝道，“一点也不坏。”

而几天之后，在几百英里之外……

## V / 五、废墟夜袭

===Original===
Light lowered in the sky as they walked beside the river, keeping close to the rocky edge and brushing away vines that strung into the water from hanging limbs. Urson broke down a branch as thick as his wrist and as tall as himself and smote the water with it, playfully. “That should put a welt on anyone’s head who wants to bother us.” He raised the stick from the water and drops ran along the bark, moving sparks at the ends of dark lines.
“We’ll have to turn into the woods for food soon,” said Iimmi, “unless we wait for animals who come down to drink.”
Urson tugged at another branch, and it twisted loose from fibrous white pulp. “Here,” he handed it to Iimmi. “I’ll have one for you in a moment, Geo.”
“And maybe we could explore a little, before it gets dark,” Geo suggested.
Urson handed him the third staff. “There’s not much here I want to see,” he muttered.

===Chinese===
天光低垂，他们沿着河岸行走，紧贴嶙峋的石岸，一路拨开从低垂的枝条间挂进水里的藤蔓。厄尔森折下一根树枝——足有他的手腕粗、他本人那么高——拿它戏耍似的拍打水面。“谁想来找咱们的麻烦，这一下管保叫他脑袋上起个包。”他把木棍从水里提起来，水珠顺着树皮滚落，在暗色纹路的尽头迸成流动的火星。
“咱们很快就得拐进林子找吃的了，”伊米说，“要不然，就蹲守那些下河饮水的野兽。”
厄尔森又拽下一根树枝，它从纤维状的白色木髓里拧脱出来。“拿着。”他把树枝递给伊米。“你的马上就弄，吉奥。”
“还有，趁天没黑，咱们不妨稍稍探上一探。”吉奥提议道。
厄尔森把第三根棍子递给他。“这地方没什么我想看的。”他咕哝道。

===Original===
“Well, we can’t sleep on the bank. We’ve got to find a place hidden in the trees.”
“Can you see what that is through there?” Iimmi asked.
“Where?” asked Geo. “Huh﻿ ﻿… ?” Through the thick growth was a rising shadow. “A rock or a cliff?” he suggested.
“Maybe,” mused Urson, “but it’s awfully regular.”
Geo started off into the underbrush, and the others followed. Their goal was further and larger than it had looked from the river. Once they passed across a section of ten or twelve stones, rectangular and side by side, like paving. Small trees had pushed up between some of them, but for thirty feet, before the edge sank beneath the soft jungle floor it was easier going. Suddenly the growth became thin again and they were at the edge of a relatively clear area. Before them loomed the ruins of a great building. Six girders cleared the highest wall, implying an original height of eighteen or twenty stories. One wall was completely sheared away and fragments of it chunked the ground. The revealed dark caves of broken rooms and cubicles suggested an injured granite hive. They approached slowly.

===Chinese===
“话虽这么说，咱们总不能睡在河岸上。总得找个树林里遮着掩着的地方。”
“你从那边空隙里看出来那是什么了吗？”伊米问。
“哪儿？”吉奥问，“嗯……？”茂密的草木后面，耸起一道影子。“是块岩石，还是道崖壁？”他猜道。
“兴许吧，”厄尔森沉吟道，“可它规整得邪门儿。”
吉奥一头钻进灌木丛，其余两人跟上。那目标比从河边望去的模样更远，也更大。途中，他们跨过一段十来块长方形的石头，一块挨一块，像铺砌过的路面。有几块石缝间已钻出小树，但在这段路缘沉入松软的丛林腐土之前，那三十来英尺走起来还算轻省。忽然草木又稀疏下来，他们已站在一片相对空旷的林间地带边缘。面前耸立着一座巨大建筑的废墟。六根钢架高过最高的那面残墙，可见它原先足有十八二十层之高。一面墙被整个削去，碎块狼藉满地。残垣内一间间破败的房间与小隔间黑洞洞地敞着，像一座受了重创的花岗岩蜂巢。他们缓缓走近。

===Original===
To one side a great metal cylinder lay askew a heap of rubbish. A flat blade of metal transversed it, one side twisting into the ground where skeletal girders shown beneath ripped plating. A row of windows like dark eyes lined the body, and a door gaped in an idiotic oval halfway along its length.
Fascinated, they turned toward the injured wreck. As they neared, a sound came from inside the door. They stopped, and their staves leapt a protective inch from the ground. In the shadow of the door, ten feet from the ground, another shadow moved, resolving itself into an animal head, long, muzzled, gray. Then they could see the forelegs. It looked like an immense dog, and it was carrying a smaller animal, obviously dead, in its mouth. It saw them, watched them, was still.
“Dinner,” Urson said softly. “Come on.” They moved forward again. Then they stopped.

===Chinese===
一侧的垃圾堆上，斜卧着一具巨大的金属圆筒。一片扁平的金属桨叶横贯筒身，一端扭进地里，撕裂的蒙皮底下露出嶙峋的骨架。筒身一侧排着一列窗，宛如一双双黑眼睛；半腰上一扇门，咧成一个呆傻的椭圆。
他们心驰神往地朝那具受创的残骸转过身去。将近门口时，门里传出一声响动。他们站住，手里的木棍警觉地弹离地面一寸。门洞的阴影里，离地十英尺高处，又一道影子动了动，凝成一颗兽头——长长的口鼻，灰色的。接着他们看见了前腿。那模样像一条硕大无朋的狗，嘴里叼着一头较小的兽，显然已经死了。它望见他们，注视着他们，一动不动。
“晚饭。”厄尔森轻声道，“上。”他们又向前挪。随即又停住了。

===Original===
Suddenly the beast sprang from the doorway. Shadow and distance had made them completely underestimate its size. Along the sprung arc flowed a canine body nearly five feet long. Urson struck up at it and knocked it from its flight with his stick. As it fell, Iimmi and Geo were upon it with theirs, clubbing its chest and head. For six blows it staggered and could not gain its feet. Then, as it threatened to heave to standing, Urson rushed forward and brought his stave straight down on the chest: bones snapped and tore through the brown pelt, only to have their blue sheen covered a moment later by a well of blood. It howled, kicked its hind feet at the stake with which Urson held it to the ground, and then stretched out its limbs and quivered. The front legs stretched, and stretched, while the torso seemed to pull in on itself, shrinking in the death agonies. The long mouth, which had dropped its prey, gaped open as the head flopped from side to side, the pink tongue lolling, shrinking.

===Chinese===
冷不防，那野兽从门洞里扑了出来。阴影和距离让他们把它的身量完全估小了。扑跃的弧线上涌过一条将近五英尺长的犬类躯体。厄尔森挥棍向上迎击，一棒把它从半空打落。它坠地的同时，伊米和吉奥的棍子也到了，劈头劈胸一顿猛砸。挨了六记，它踉踉跄跄，怎么也撑不起身。待它攒足劲要挣扎立起，厄尔森抢步上前，木棍当胸直捣下去：骨头断裂，戳穿棕色皮毛，蓝白的骨茬不多时便被一泓涌出的血盖没。它嚎叫着，后爪乱踢厄尔森摁住它身子的木桩，随后四肢一伸，抽搐起来。前腿越伸越长，躯干却仿佛朝内里收缩，在垂死的痛楚中瘪了下去。那长嘴早松开了猎物，脑袋左右甩荡，豁然张着，粉红的舌头耷拉在外，越缩越小。

===Original===
“My God,” said Geo.
The sharp muzzle blunted now and the claws in the padded paw stretched, opened into human fingers and a thumb. The hairlessness of the underbelly had spread to the entire carcass. Hind legs lengthened, joints reversed themselves, and bare knees bent as human feet dragged themselves through fragments of brown leaves over the ground and a human thigh gave a final contraction, stilled, and then one leg fell out straight again. A shaggy, black-haired man lay still on the ground, his chest caved and bloody. In one last throw, he flung his hands up to grasp the stake and pull it from his chest, but too weak, they slipped down as his lips curled back from his mouth revealing a row of perfectly white, blunt teeth.
Urson stepped back, and then back again. The stave fell, pulled loose with a sucking explosion from the ruined mess of lung. The bear man had raised his hand to his own chest and seized his triple, gold token. “In the name of the Goddess,” he finally said.
Iimmi walked forward now, picked up the carcass of the smaller animal that had been dropped, and turned away. “Well,” he said, “I guess dinner isn’t going to be as big as we thought.”
“I guess not,” Geo said.

===Chinese===
“天哪。”吉奥说。
尖削的口鼻此刻钝了下去，肉垫脚掌里的爪子伸展开来，张成人的手指和一根拇指。腹部原有的无毛处已蔓延到整具躯体。后腿拉长，关节反转，赤裸的膝弯屈起，一双人脚拖着自己踏过满地碎裂的褐色树叶，一条人类的大腿最后痉挛了一下，僵住，随即一条腿又直直摊开。一个毛发蓬乱的黑发男人静静躺在地上，胸口塌陷，血肉模糊。最后一挣，他双手扬起，想去抓住木桩，把它从自己胸口拔出来——可是太虚弱了，双手滑落下去，嘴唇向外翻卷，露出一口齐整雪白的钝牙。
厄尔森退了一步，又退了一步。木桩倒下，从那团毁烂的肺腑里拔出来时，带出一声湿浊的爆响。那熊一样的汉子已抬手按在自己胸口，攥住了他那三枚一套的金币。“以女神之名。”他终于开口道。
这时伊米走上前，拾起那具被丢下的小兽尸体，转过身去。“唉，”他说，“我看晚饭没有咱们想的那么丰盛了。”
“我看也是。”吉奥说。

===Original===
They walked back to the ruined building, away from the corpse.
“Hey, Urson,” Geo said at last to the big man who was still holding his coins, “Snap out of it. What’s the matter?”
“The only man I’ve ever seen whose body was that broken in that way,” he said slowly, “was one whose side struck into by a ship’s spar.”
---

===Chinese===
他们走回那片建筑废墟，远离尸体。
“喂，厄尔森，”吉奥终于对这个手里还攥着金币的大汉说，“回魂吧。到底怎么了？”
“我这辈子见过被那样毁坏的身体，只有一副，”他缓缓说道，“那人被船上的一根桅桁捅进了肋侧。”
---

===Original===
They decided to settle that evening at the corner of one of the building’s ruined walls. They produced fire with a rock against a section of slightly rusted girder. And after much sawing on a jagged metal blade protruding from a pile of rubble, they managed to quarter the animal and rip most of the pelt from its red body. With thin branches to hold the meat, they did a passable job of roasting. Although partially burned, partially raw, and without seasoning, they ate it, and their hunger ceased. As they sat huddled by the wall, ripping red juicy fibers from the last bones with their teeth, night swelled through the jungle, imprisoning them in the shell of orange flicking from their fire.
“Shall we leave it going?” asked Urson.
“Fire keeps animals away,” Iimmi said.
On leaves piled together now they stretched out by the wall of the broken building. There was quiet﻿—an insect hum, no un-namable chitterings, except for the comforting rush of the river’s water.

===Chinese===
当晚，他们决定在建筑一面断墙的墙角安顿下来。他们拿一块石头敲一段微微生锈的钢梁，打出了火。又对着一堆瓦砾里戳出的一柄锯齿金属刃拉锯了半晌，总算把那头兽卸成四块，从赤红的躯体上撕下大半张皮。拿细树枝串起肉，烤得还算像样。虽然半焦半生，又没盐没作料，他们照样吃了，饥饿止住了。他们挤靠墙根坐着，用牙齿从最后几根骨头上撕下红润多汁的肉丝，夜色这时涨满了丛林，把他们囚在篝火闪动的那一层橙色薄壳里。
“火留着不灭？”厄尔森问。
“火能赶野兽。”伊米说。
他们在断墙边把落叶堆拢，躺了上去。四下安静——只有虫鸣，再没有那些叫不出名字的窸窣，只有河水教人安心的奔流做伴。

===Original===
Geo was first to awake, his eyes filled with silver. The entire clearing had been flooded by white light from the huge disk of the moon that sat on the rim of the trees. Iimmi and Urson beside him looked uncomfortably corpse-like, and he was about to reach over and touch Iimmi’s outstretched arm when there was a noise behind him, like beaten cloth. He jerked his head around, and was staring at the gray wall by which they had camped. He looked up at the spreading plane that tore off raggedly against the night. Fatigue had washed into something unpleasant and hard in his belly that had little to do with tiredness. He stretched his arm in the leaves once more and put his cheek down on the cool flesh of his shoulder.
The beating sound came again and continued for a few seconds. He rolled his face up and stared at the sky. Something crossed on the moon. It seemed to expand a moment, spread its wings, and draw them in again.
He reached out, his arm over the leaves like thunder, and grabbed Iimmi’s black shoulder. Iimmi grunted, started, then rolled over on his back, and opened his eyes. Geo saw the black chest drop with expelled breath, the only recognition given. A few seconds later the chest rose again. Iimmi turned his face to Geo and raised his finger to his lips. Then he turned his face back up to the night. Three more times the flapping sounded behind them, behind the wall, Geo realized. Once he glanced down again and saw that Iimmi had raised his arm and put it over his eyes.
They passed years that way. Then a flock suddenly leapt from the wall. Some of them fell twenty feet before their wings filled with air and they rose again. They circled wider and before they returned, another flock dropped off into the night.

===Chinese===
吉奥第一个醒来，满眼银光。巨大的月轮搁在树梢上，惨白的光淹没了整片林间空地。身旁的伊米和厄尔森看上去像两具死尸，让人心里发毛。他正要伸手去碰伊米摊开的胳膊，忽听背后一声响动，像布帛扑打。他猛地回头，直瞪着他们露宿的那面灰墙。他抬眼，望向那面朝夜色参差撕裂开去的高墙。疲惫已在他肚子里沉淀成一种又硬又难受的东西，跟困倦没多大关系。他又把手臂伸回落叶里，脸颊贴上自己肩头凉凉的皮肉。
扑打声再度响起，持续了几秒。他仰起脸，盯着天空。有什么东西掠过月面。它似乎胀了一瞬，张开翅膀，又收拢回去。
他探出手去，手臂扫过落叶，声如闷雷，一把抓住伊米黑亮的肩膀。伊米哼了一声，惊起，随即翻过身仰面躺着，睁开了眼。吉奥看见那黑色的胸膛随着一口呼出的气塌下去——这是他仅有的回应。几秒之后，胸膛重新鼓起。伊米把脸转向吉奥，竖起一根手指抵在唇上。然后他又仰面朝向夜空。又是三下，扑翼声就在他们背后、墙的那一边响，吉奥明白了。有一次他朝下瞥了一眼，看见伊米抬起手臂，遮住了眼睛。
他们就这样熬过了不知多少年。忽然，一群影子从墙头跃起。有几只跌落了二十英尺，翅膀才兜住空气，重新升起。它们盘旋的圈子越转越大，还没等它们绕回来，又一群扎进了夜色。

===Original===
As they fell this time, Geo suddenly grabbed Iimmi’s arm and pulled it down from his eyes. The figures dropped through the dark like kites, sixty feet above them, forty feet, thirty; then there was a thin, piercing shriek. Iimmi was up on his feet in a second, and Geo beside him, their staffs in hand.
“Here it comes,” breathed Iimmi. He kicked at Urson, but the big man was already on his knees, and then feet. The wings beat insistently and darkly before them as they stood against the wall. The figures flew toward them and at the terrifying distance of five feet, reversed. “I don’t think they can get in at the wall,” said Iimmi.
“I hope the hell they can’t,” Urson said.
The figures dropped to the ground, black wings crumpling to their bodies in the moonlight. In the growing hoard of shadow in front of them, light snagged on a metal blade.

===Chinese===
这一回落下来时，吉奥突然抓住伊米的手臂，把它从他眼睛上拽了下来。那些身影像风筝一样穿过黑暗坠落，离他们六十英尺，四十英尺，三十英尺；随即响起一声尖细刺耳的尖叫。伊米眨眼间便跳了起来，吉奥立在他身边，两人手里都攥着木棍。
“来了。”伊米低声道。他踢了厄尔森一脚，可大汉已经跪起，跟着站直了。他们背墙而立，翅膀在眼前执拗而幽暗地扑扇。那些身影朝他们飞来，到了五英尺这个吓人的距离，猛地折返。“我看它们贴不拢墙根。”伊米说。
“我他妈但愿贴不拢。”厄尔森说。
那些身影落到地上，黑色的翅膀在月光下折叠贴向身躯。他们面前那团越积越厚的阴影里，有光在一柄金属刃上挂了一下。

===Original===
Then two of the creatures detached from the others and hurled themselves forward, swords arcing suddenly above their heads.
They swung their staffs as hard as they could, catching both beasts on the chest. They fell backwards in a sudden expansion of rubbery wings, as though they had stumbled into billowing dark canvas.
Three more now leapt over the fallen ones, shrieking. As they came, Urson looked up and jammed his staff into the belly of a fourth monster who was about to fall on them from above. One got past Iimmi’s whistling staff and Geo had to stop swinging and grab a furry arm. He pulled it to the side, overbalancing the huge, sailed creature. It dropped its sword as it lay for a moment, struggling on its back. Geo grabbed the blade and brought it straight from the ground up into the gut of another of the creatures who spread open its wings and staggered back. He wrested the blade free, and then turned it down into the body of the fallen one; it made a thick sound like a crushed sponge. As the blade came out again and he hacked into a shadow on his left, a voice suddenly sounded, but inside his head.

===Chinese===
随即其中两头脱离群伙，猛扑上来，长剑忽然在头顶抡出弧光。
他们抡圆了木棍拼命打去，正中两头野兽的前胸。它们猛地张开橡皮似的翅膀向后跌去，仿佛一头栽进了翻涌的黑帆布。
又有三头越过倒地的同类扑来，尖声嘶叫。它们扑到时，厄尔森抬眼一望，木棍直捅进第四头怪兽的肚子——那家伙正要从头顶上砸落下来。有一头避开了伊米呼啸的木棍，吉奥只得停了挥击，一把抓住它一条毛茸茸的臂膀。他往旁边一带，那头鼓翼的庞然大物顿时失了平衡。它仰面倒地挣扎的当口，剑脱了手。吉奥抄起剑来，从地上直撩进另一头野兽的肚腹，那兽张开双翅，踉跄后退。他拧出剑刃，又反手扎进倒地那具躯体；声音沉浊厚实，像捏烂一块海绵。剑再抽出来，他朝左边一道黑影劈去，就在这时，一个声音蓦地响起——却是在他的脑袋里。

===Original===
The﻿ ﻿… jewels﻿ ﻿…
“Snake!” bawled Geo. “Where the hell are you?” He was still holding his staff, and now he flung it forward, spear-like, into the face of an advancing beast. Struck, it opened up like a black parachute, knocking away three of its companions, before it fell.
In the view, cleared for an instant, Geo saw a slight, spidery form, dart from the jungle edge into the clearing. With his free hand Geo ripped the jewels from his neck and flung the confused handful of thong and chain over the heads of the shrieking beasts. The beads made a double eye in the light at the top of their arc before they fell on the leaves beyond. Snake picked them up and held them above his head.
Fire leapt from the boy’s hands in a double bolt that converged in the center of the dark bodies. A red flare silhouetted the jagged edge of a wing. A wing flamed, waved flame, and the burning beast tried to take air before it fell, splashing fire about it. Orange light caught sharp on brown faces chiseled with shadow, caught in the terrified red bead of an eye or along double fangs behind dark lips.
Burning wings withered on the ground; dead leaves had sparked now, and whips of light ran on the clearing floor. The beasts retreated and the three men stood against the wall, panting.

===Chinese===
珍……宝……
“蛇！”吉奥大吼，“你到底在哪儿？”他手里还攥着木棍，此刻挺棍掷出，像掷一杆长矛，正中一头逼近的野兽的脸。挨了这一击，那兽像顶黑降落伞似的炸开，撞翻了三个同伴，方才栽倒在地。
趁着视野豁然清亮的一瞬，吉奥看见一个蛛脚细手般的瘦小身影，从丛林边缘蹿进空地。吉奥腾出一只手，把珍宝从脖子上扯下来，皮绳锁链缠作一团，扬手抛过那群尖啸野兽的头顶。两颗珠子飞到弧线顶点，在光里合成一双眼睛，随即落进远处的落叶。蛇拾起它们，高举过头顶。
火焰从少年双手里跃出，化作两道并射的火光，汇拢在那些黑色躯体的正中。一团红光映出一面翅膀参差的轮廓。翅膀烧了起来，火焰翻卷，燃烧的野兽想在坠地之前腾身而起，火屑四下溅洒。橙光锐利地照亮一张张凿满阴影的棕色面孔，落进一只只惊恐的红眼睛，或者顺着暗色嘴唇后的两对獠牙滑过。
燃烧的翅膀在地上蜷焦；枯叶此刻也迸出火星，一条条光鞭在空地上奔窜。野兽们退走了，三条汉子背墙而立，喘着粗气。

===Original===
“Watch out!” Iimmi suddenly called.
Snake looked up as the great wings tented over him, hiding him momentarily. Red flared beneath them, and suddenly the beasts fell away, their sails sweeping over the dead leaves, moved by wind or life, Geo couldn’t tell. Dark flappings rose on the moon, grew further away, and were gone.
Away from the wall, they saw the fire had blown up against the wall and was dying. They ran quickly toward the edge of the forest. “Snake,” said Geo when they stopped. “This is Iimmi, this is Snake. We told you about him.”
Iimmi extended his hand. “Glad to meet you.”
“Look,” said Geo, “he can read your mind, so if you still think he’s a spy﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“当心！”伊米忽然喊道。
巨大的翅膀在蛇头顶张成帐篷，一时把他整个罩住，他抬头仰望。帐下红光一闪，野兽们陡然跌开，一双双翼帆扫过枯叶——是风势还是活气推着，吉奥分辨不出。黑色的扑翼升上月亮，越去越远，终于没了踪影。
离开墙根，他们看见那堆火已被吹到墙边，正奄奄将熄。他们快步跑向林缘。“蛇，”站定之后吉奥说，“这位是伊米。伊米，这位是蛇。我们跟你说起过他。”
伊米伸出手。“幸会。”
“听着，”吉奥说，“他能读你的心思。所以你要是还当他是奸细……”

===Original===
Iimmi grinned. “Remember the general rule? If he is a spy, it’s going to get much too complicated trying to figure why he saved us like that.”
Urson scratched his head. “If it’s a choice between Snake and nothing, we better take Snake. Hey, Four Arms, I owe you a thrashing.” He paused, then laughed. “I hope some day I get a chance to give it to you.”
“Where have you been, anyway?” Geo asked. He put his hand on the boy’s shoulder. “You’re wet.”
“Our water friends again?” suggested Urson.
“Probably,” said Geo.

===Chinese===
伊米咧嘴一笑。“记得那条通则吗？他要真是奸细，那要想明白他为什么这样救咱们，事情可就复杂得没边儿了。”
厄尔森挠了挠头。“要是在蛇和两手空空之间挑一样，那咱们还是要蛇吧。喂，四条胳膊的，我还欠你一顿揍呢。”他顿了顿，笑了。“但愿哪天有机会补上。”
“话说回来，你这些天到底跑哪儿去了？”吉奥问。他把手搭在少年肩上。“你浑身是湿的。”
“又是咱们那些水里的朋友？”厄尔森猜道。
“多半是。”吉奥说。

===Original===
Snake now held one hand toward Geo.
“What’s that? Oh, you don’t want to keep them?”
Snake shook his head.
“All right,” said Geo. He took one jewel and put it around his neck.
Geo took the wrought chain with the platinum claw from his neck and hung it around Iimmi’s. The white eye shown on his dark chest in the moonlight. Now Snake beckoned them to follow him back across the clearing. They came, stopping to pick up swords from the shriveled darknesses on the ground about the clearing. As they passed around the edge of the broken building, Geo looked for the corpse they had left there, but it was gone.

===Chinese===
这时蛇朝吉奥伸出一只手。
“这是干什么？哦，你不想留着它们？”
蛇摇了摇头。
“好吧。”吉奥说。他取过一颗珍宝，挂回自己脖子上。
吉奥又从颈上摘下那条带白金爪座的锻金链子，挂到伊米颈上。白眼球似的珠子在他黝黑的胸膛上映着月光。这时蛇招手让他们跟自己穿过空地往回走。他们跟上，一路停下来，从空地四周地上那一个个干瘪的黑影里捡起长剑。绕过断楼的墙角时，吉奥去找他们先前留下的那具尸体——尸体不见了。

===Original===
“Where are we going?” asked Urson.
Snake only motioned them onward. They neared the broken cylinder and Snake scrambled up the rubble under the dark hole through which the man-wolf had leaped earlier that evening.
At the door, Snake turned and lifted the jewel from Geo’s neck, and held it aloft. The jewel glowed now, with a blue-green light that seeped into the corners and crevices of the ruined entrance. Shreds of cloth hung at the windows, most of which were broken. Twigs and rubbish littered the metal floor. They walked between double seats toward a door at the far end. Effaced signs still hung on the walls.

> N﻿ ﻿… SM﻿ ﻿… K﻿ ﻿… G

The door at the end was ajar, and Snake opened it all the way. Something scuttered through a cracked window. The jewel’s light showed two seats broken from their fixtures. Vines covered the front window in which only a few splinters of glass hung on the rim. Draped in rotten fabric, a few metal rings about wrists and ankles, two skeletons with silver helmets had fallen from the seats. Snake pointed to a row of smashed glass disks in front of the broken seats.

===Chinese===
“咱们这是上哪儿去？”厄尔森问。
蛇只招手让他们继续走。他们走近那只破裂的圆筒，蛇攀上黑洞底下的瓦砾堆——当天傍晚，那狼人正是从这洞里扑出来的。
到了门口，蛇转过身，从吉奥颈间举起珍宝，高举过头。珍宝此刻亮了，泛出蓝绿色的光，渗进残破入口的角角落落与缝隙。窗上挂着布条，大半的窗都破了。金属地板上散落着细枝和垃圾。他们在成对的座椅间穿行，朝尽头的一扇门走去。墙上还挂着字迹磨灭的标牌。

> N……SM……K……G

尽头那扇门虚掩着，蛇把它整个推开。什么东西窸窸窣窣窜过一扇裂窗。珍宝的光照出两只从底座上脱落的座椅。藤蔓爬满了前窗，窗框边只挂着几片碎玻璃。两具戴银盔的骸骨从座椅上跌落，裹着朽烂的织物，手腕脚踝上套着几只金属环。蛇指着破座椅前一排砸碎的玻璃圆盘。

===Original===
Radio﻿ ﻿… they heard in their minds.
Now he reached down into the mess on the floor and dislodged a chunk of rusted metal. Gun, he said, showing it to Geo.
The three men examined it. “What’s it good for?” asked Urson.
Snake shrugged.
“Are there any electricities, or diodes around?” asked Geo, remembering the words from before.
Snake shrugged again.

===Chinese===
无线电……他们在脑海里听到了这个词。
他又俯下身，在地板那堆杂物里翻拣，抠出一块锈蚀的金属疙瘩。枪，他说，把它举给吉奥看。
三条汉子凑上去端详。“这玩意儿顶什么用？”厄尔森问。
蛇耸了耸肩。
“这儿还有没有电啊、二极管什么的？”吉奥问，想起先前听来的那些字眼。
蛇又耸了耸肩。

===Original===
“Why did you want to show us all this?” Geo asked.
The boy only turned and started back toward the door. When they were standing in the oval entrance, about to climb down, Iimmi pointed to the ruins of the building ahead of them. “Do you know what that building was called?”
Barracks, Snake said.
“I know that word,” said Geo.
“So do I,” said Iimmi. “It means a place where they used to keep soldiers all together. It’s from one of the old languages.”
“Where to now?” Urson asked Snake.

===Chinese===
“你为什么要把这些都指给我们看？”吉奥问。
少年只是转身，朝门口往回走。他们站在那个椭圆形门洞里，正要爬下去，伊米指着前方那片建筑废墟。“你知道那座建筑从前叫什么吗？”
兵营，蛇说。
“这个词我知道。”吉奥说。
“我也是。”伊米说，“意思是从前把士兵们集中安置在一处的地方。是老语言里的词。”
“现在去哪儿？”厄尔森问蛇。

===Original===
The boy climbed back down into the clearing and they followed him into the denser wood where only pearls of light scattered through the trees. They emerged at a broad ribbon of silver, the river, broken by rocks.
“We were right the first time,” Geo said. “We should have stayed here.”
The sound of rippling, sloshing, the full whisper of leaves and foliage along the edges of the forest﻿—these accompanied them as they lay down on the dried moss behind the larger rocks. And with the heaviness of release on them, they dropped, like stones down a well, the bright pool of sleep.
---

===Chinese===
少年爬下瓦砾，回到空地，领着他们钻进更密的林子——那里只有一粒粒珍珠似的光斑从树隙间洒落。他们从林中钻出，来到一条宽阔的银色缎带跟前——是那条河，被岩石划破成段。
“咱们头一回就选对了地方，”吉奥说，“早该待在这儿不走的。”
水声淙淙，浪花泼溅，林缘草木叶片满满的低语——这些声音陪着他们，在大块岩石后面干燥的苔藓上躺下。卸下重负的沉坠感罩住他们，他们像石子落井，坠进了睡眠那一泓亮潭。
---

===Original===
*The bright pool of silver grew and spread and wrinkled into the familiar shapes of mast, the rail of the deck, and the whiteness of the sea beyond the ship. The scene moved down the deck, until another gaunt figure approached from the other direction. The features, though strangely distorted by whiteness and pulled to grotesquerie, were recognizable as those of the captain as he drew near.*
*“Oh, mate,” said the captain.*
*Silence, while the mate gave an answer they couldn’t hear.*
*“Yes,” answered the captain. “I wonder what she wants, too.” His voice was hollow, etiolated like a flower grown in darkness. The captain turned and knocked on Argo’s cabin door. It opened, and they stepped in.*
*The hand that opened the door for them was thin as winter twigs. The walls of the room seemed draped in spider webs and hangings insubstantial as layered dust. The great desk seemed spindly, grotesque, and the papers on top of it were tissue thin, threatening to scutter and crumble with a breath. The chandelier above gave more languishing white smoke than light, and the arms, branches, and complexed array of oil cups looked like a convocation of spiders.*
*Argo spoke in a pale white voice that sounded like the whisper of thin fingers tearing webs.*
*“So,” she said. “We will stay at least another seven days.”*

===Chinese===
*那泓银色的亮潭涨开、铺展，皱成一个个熟悉的形状：桅杆，甲板的栏杆，还有船外海面的白。景象顺着甲板移动，直到另一个瘦削的身影从另一头迎面走来。那张脸虽被一片惨白扭曲得怪诞，走近时仍认得出是船长。*
*“哦，大副。”船长说。*
*沉默。大副答了话，他们听不见答的是什么。*
*“是啊，”船长答道，“我也纳闷她想要什么。”他的声音空洞，像暗处开出的花一样褪尽了颜色。船长转身，敲了敲阿戈的舱门。门开了，两人走了进去。*
*为他们开门的那只手，细得像冬天的枯枝。舱房的四壁仿佛挂满了蛛网，垂着层积的浮尘般虚薄的帷幔。那张大书桌显得细脚伶仃，怪诞不经；桌上的纸薄得像蝉翼，仿佛一口气就能叫它们四散碎裂。头顶的吊灯放出的惨白浓烟多于光亮，灯臂、灯杈和繁复交错的油盏，看着像一场蜘蛛的集会。*
*阿戈开口了，声音苍白，像纤细的手指撕开蛛网时的窸窣。*
*“这样吧，”她说，“我们至少再待七天。”*

===Original===
*“But why?” asked the captain.*
*“I have received a sign from the sea.”*
*“I do not wish to question your authority, Priestess,” began the captain.*
*“Then do not,” interrupted Argo.*
*“My mate has raised the objection that﻿ ﻿…”*

===Chinese===
*“可这是为什么？”船长问。*
*“我从海上得到了一个征兆。”*
*“我无意质疑您的权威，女祭司，”船长开口道。*
*“那就别质疑。”阿戈打断道。*
*“我的大副提出的异议是……”*

===Original===
*“Your mate has raised his hand to me once,” stated the Priestess. “It is only in my benevolence﻿ ﻿…” Here she paused, and her voice became more unsure, “… that I do not destroy him where he stands.” Beneath, her veil, a face could be made out that might have belonged to a dried skull.*
*“But,” began the captain.*
*“We wait here by the island of Aptor another seven days,” commanded Argo. She looked away from the captain now, in a direction that must have been straight into the eyes of the mate. From behind the veil, hate welled like living liquid from the seemingly empty sockets. They turned to go, and once more on deck, they stopped to watch the sea. Near the indistinct horizon, a sharp tongue of land outlined itself with mountains. The cliffs were chalky on one side, then streaked with red and blue clays on the other. There was a reddish glow beyond one mountain, like the shimmering of a volcano. And dark as most of it was, it was a distinct darkness, backed with purple, or broken by the warm, differing grays of individual rocks. Even through the night, at this distance, beyond the silver crescent of the beach, the jungle looked rich, green even in the darkness, redolently full and quiveringly heavy with life.*
---
And then the thin screams﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
*“你的大副有一次曾对我抬起了手，”女祭司一字一顿地说，“全凭我一片慈悲……”说到这里她顿了顿，声音反倒不那么笃定了，“……才没有叫他当场毁灭。”面纱底下，依稀辨得出一副面容，说不定本就属于一具干枯的骷髅。*
*“可是——”船长刚要开口。*
*“我们就在阿普托岛边再待上七天。”阿戈命令道。这时她把目光从船长身上移开，移向一个方向——那必定是直直望进了大副的眼睛。面纱后面，憎恨像活着的液体，从看似空空如也的眼窝里涌出来。两人转身离去，重新站上甲板，又停下来眺望大海。在模糊的天际线近旁，一道尖尖的陆地探出来，勾出群山的轮廓。悬崖一面白垩斑斑，另一面却浸染着一道道红黏土与蓝黏土。一座山后透出淡红的光晕，像火山在隐隐闪动。那片土地虽有大半浸在黑暗里，却是一种轮廓分明的黑暗：深处衬着紫色，又不时被一块块山岩各自不同的暖灰打破。纵然隔着夜色，隔着这样的距离，在沙滩银色新月之外，丛林依然显得丰饶，黑暗中也是绿的，馥郁饱满，沉甸甸地颤动着生命。*
---
接着，响起了细细的尖啸……

## VI / 六、古庙惊魂

===Original===
Geo rolled over and out of sleep, stones and moss beneath his shoulder. He grabbed his sword and was on his feet instantly. Iimmi was also standing with raised blade. The river sloshed coldly behind them.
The thin screaming came again, like a hot wire drawn down the gelid morning. Snake and Urson were also up, now. The sounds came from the direction of the ruined barracks. Geo started forward, cautiously, curiosity drawing him toward the sound, fear sending him from the relatively unprotected bank and into the woods. The others followed him.
Abruptly they reached the edge of the forest’s wall, beyond which was the clear space before the broken building. They crouched now, behind the trees, watching, fascinated.
Between ape and man, it hovered at the edge of the forest in the shadow. It was Snake’s height, but more of Urson’s build. An animal pelt wrapped its middle and went over its shoulder, clothing it more fully than either of the four humans were clothed. Thick-footed, great-handed, it loped four steps into the clearing, uttered its piercing shriek, and fell on a hunk of flesh that last night’s beasts had dropped from the sky. Its head rocked back and forth as it tore at its food. Once it raised its head and a sliver of flesh shook from its teeth before the face dropped again to devour.
They watched the huge fingers upon broad flat palms, tipped with bronze-colored claws, convulse again and again, reflexively, into the gray, fibrous meat while the fanged mouth ripped.
Whether it was a shift of breeze, or a final reflex, Geo couldn’t tell, but one of the membranous sails raised darkly and beat about the oblivious animal that fed on its corpse.

===Chinese===
吉奥（Geo）一个翻身，脱离了睡乡——肩下硌着石头和苔藓。他一把抓起剑，霎时站直了身子。伊米（Iimmi）也已持剑而立。河水在他们身后冷冷地哗荡。
那尖细的尖叫又响起来，像一根烧红的铁丝顺着冷凝的清晨划下来。这会儿蛇（Snake）和厄尔森（Urson）也起来了。声音来自废营房的方向。吉奥小心翼翼地向前挪，好奇心把他拽向声音，恐惧又把他从几乎无遮无挡的河岸赶进树林。其余的人跟在他身后。
冷不丁地，他们到了林墙的边缘，墙外便是那座残破建筑前的空地。他们蹲下来，躲在树后，看入了迷。
那东西介于猿与人之间，在林缘的阴影里悬立不动。它有蛇的身高，却更近厄尔森的体格。一张兽皮裹住它的腰腹，又搭上肩头，把它裹得比四个人类中的任何一个都严实。厚脚板，大手掌，它颠着步子窜进空地，走了四步，发出一声刺耳的尖叫，扑向一块昨夜的怪兽从天上掉下来的肉。它撕食的时候脑袋前后摇晃。有一次它抬起头，一条肉丝从牙齿间抖落，随后那张脸又埋下去大嚼。
他们看着那宽阔扁平的手掌上的巨指——指尖带着青铜色的爪——一阵又一阵地、反射性地在灰色的纤维状肉块里痉挛抽动，长着獠牙的嘴同时撕扯不休。
是气流偏转，还是临死前最后一记抽搐，吉奥分辨不出，总之那尸体上的一扇膜翅黑沉沉地扬起，扑打着那头浑然不觉、只顾埋头吃尸的野兽。

===Original===
“Come on,” Urson said. “Let’s go.”
A thin scream sounded behind them, and they whirled.
It crouched apishly, the bronze-clawed fingers opened and closed like breathing, and the shaggy head was knotted with dirt and twigs. The breath hissed from the faintly moving, full lips.
Urson reached for his sword, but Iimmi saw him and whispered, “No, don’t.”
The Negro extended his hand and moved slowly forward. The hulking form took a step back, and mewed.
Geo suddenly caught the idea. Coming up beside Iimmi, he made a quick series of snaps with his fingers and said in a coaxing, baby voice. “Come, come, come.” He laughed softly to Urson back over his shoulder. “It won’t hurt us,” he said.
“If we don’t hurt it,” added Iimmi. “It’s some sort of necrophage.”

===Chinese===
“走吧，”厄尔森说。“咱们走。”
一声尖细的尖叫在他们背后响起，众人霍然回身。
它猿似的蹲着，带青铜爪的手指一开一合，像在呼吸；乱蓬蓬的脑袋上纠结着泥土和断枝。气息从微微翕动的厚唇间嘶嘶流出。
厄尔森伸手去摸剑，伊米看见了他，低声道：“别，别拔。”
那黑人伸出手，慢慢向前挪。那庞大的身影退了一步，喵喵地叫。
吉奥猛然领会了。他凑到伊米身旁，手指头飞快地打了一串响，用哄娃娃的腔调说：“来，来，来。”他回头朝厄尔森轻轻一笑。“它不会伤害咱们的。”他说。
“只要咱们不伤害它。”伊米补上一句，“这是某种食尸兽（necrophage）。”

===Original===
“A what?” asked Urson.
“It only eats dead things,” Geo explained. “They’re mentioned in some of the old legends. Apparently, after the Great Fire, so the story goes, there were more of these things around than anything else. In Leptar, though, they became extinct.”
“Come here, cutie,” said Iimmi. “Nice little, sweet little, pretty little thing.”
It mewed again, bowed its head, came over and rubbed against Iimmi’s hip. “Smells like hell,” the Negro observed, scratching behind its ear. “Watch out there, big boy!” The beast gave a particularly affectionate rub that almost upset Iimmi’s balance.
“Leave your pet alone,” said Urson, “and let’s get going.”
Geo patted the apelike skull. “So long, beautiful,” he said. They turned toward the river again.

===Chinese===
“食什么？”厄尔森问。
“它只吃死了的东西。”吉奥解释道，“一些古老传说里提到过它们。据说大火灾（the Great Fire）过后，这类东西遍地都是，比什么都多。不过在莱普塔（Leptar），它们早就绝迹了。”
“过来，小可爱。”伊米说，“乖乖的、甜甜的、漂漂亮亮的小东西。”
它又喵喵叫了两声，低下头，凑过来在伊米的胯上蹭。“臭得要命。”那黑人评价道，搔着它的耳根。“哎哟当心，大家伙！”那野兽献了个特别亲热的殷勤，差点把伊米蹭得站不稳。
“别理你那宠物了。”厄尔森说，“咱们赶路吧。”
吉奥拍了拍那猿一样的头骨。“再会了，美人儿。”他说。他们重又朝河边走去。

===Original===
As they emerged on the rocky bank, Geo said, “Well, at least we know we have seven days to get to the Temple of Hama and out again.”
“What do you mean?” asked Iimmi.
“Don’t you remember the dream, back on the ship?”
“Who was thinking that?” asked Iimmi.
“Jordde, the first mate.”
“He makes everybody look dead. I thought I was having a nightmare. I could hardly recognize the captain.”
“You see one reason for believing he’s a spy?”
“Because of the way he sees things?” Again he smiled. “A poet’s reason, I’m afraid. But I see.”

===Chinese===
走上乱石河岸时，吉奥说：“好歹咱们知道了一件事：去哈马（Hama）神庙，再出来，一共有七天。”
“这话怎么说？”伊米问。
“你不记得在船上时做的那个梦了？”
“那是谁在想？”伊米问。
“大副约德（Jordde）。”
“他瞧谁，谁都像死了一样。我还当自己在做噩梦，连船长都快认不出来了。”
“你这就明白我们为什么信他是个奸细了吧？”
“就凭他看东西的方式？”他又笑了。“恐怕这是诗人的理由。不过我明白了。”

===Original===
The thin shriek sounded behind them, and they turned to see the hulking form crouched on the rocks above them.
“Uh-oh,” said Urson, “there’s your cute friend.”
“I hope we haven’t picked up a tag-a-long for the rest of the trip,” said Geo.
It loped down over the rocks and stopped just before them.
“What’s it got?” Iimmi asked.
“I can’t tell,” said Geo.
Reaching into the bib of its animal skin, it brought out a gray hunk of meat and held it toward them.

===Chinese===
尖细的尖叫又在背后响起，他们回头，看见那庞大的身影蹲在上方的岩石上。
“哎哟，”厄尔森说，“你那可爱的朋友来了。”
“但愿咱们没有一路捡个小尾巴，甩也甩不掉。”吉奥说。
它颠着步子翻下岩石，在他们跟前站住了。
“它拿着什么？”伊米问。
“看不出来。”吉奥说。
它把手伸进兽皮围兜里，掏出一大块灰扑扑的肉，朝他们递过来。

===Original===
Iimmi laughed. “Breakfast,” he said.
“That!” demanded Urson.
“Can you suggest anything better?” Geo asked. He took the meat from the beast’s claws. “Thanks, gorgeous.”
It turned, looked back, and bounded up the bank and into the forest again.
With fire from the jewels, and wooden spits from the woods, they soon had the meat crackling and brown and the grease bubbling down its sides and hissing onto the hot stones they had used to rim the flame. Urson sat apart, sniffed, and then moved closer, and finally scratched his big fingers through his hairy stomach and said, “Damn it, I’m hungry.” They made room for him at the fire without comment.
Sun struck the tops of the trees for the first time that morning and a moment later splashed copper in concentric curves on the water by the rock’s edge, staining it further with dull gold.

===Chinese===
伊米笑了。“早饭。”他说。
“就这个！”厄尔森抗议道。
“你能推荐点更好的？”吉奥问。他从野兽的爪子里接过肉。“多谢啦，大美人。”
它转过身，回头望了一眼，纵身跃上河岸，又钻进了森林。
用珍宝取的火，加上林子里削的木签，他们很快就把肉烤得噼啪作响、焦黄喷香，油脂顺着肉块鼓泡淌下，嘶嘶地滴在围火的热石上。厄尔森起先坐在一边，抽了抽鼻子，随即挪近了些，最后终于把大手指插进毛茸茸的肚皮里挠着，说：“见鬼，我饿了。”众人在火边给他腾出个位置，谁也没多话。
那天早晨，阳光头一回打上树梢，片刻之后，便在岩石边的水面上泼洒出一圈圈铜色的涟漪，又给它染上一层黯淡的金。

===Original===
“You seem to know your way around awfully well. Have you ever been on Aptor before?” Iimmi asked Snake suddenly.
Snake paused for a moment. Then he nodded, slowly.
They were all silent now.
Finally Geo asked, “What made you ask that?”
“Something in your first theory,” Iimmi said. “I’ve been thinking it for some time, and I guess you knew I was thinking it too, Four Arms. You thought Jordde wanted to get rid of me, Whitey, and Snake, and that it was just an accident that he caught Whitey first instead of Snake. You thought he wanted to get rid of Whitey and me because of something we’d seen, or might have seen, when we were on Aptor with Argo. I just thought perhaps he wanted to get rid of Snake for the same reason. Which meant he might have been on Aptor before, too.”

===Chinese===
“你对这一带的路数好像熟得很。你以前来过阿普托（Aptor）？”伊米突然问蛇。
蛇顿了片刻。然后他点了点头，很慢。
这下众人都不作声了。
末了吉奥问：“你怎么会想到问这个？”
“是你那第一套推想里的一个破绽。”伊米说，“这事我想了有些日子了，我猜你也早知道我在想——对吧，四臂（Four Arms）。你原先认为，约德想除掉我、小白（Whitey）和蛇，他先得手的是小白而不是蛇，纯属偶然。你认为他要除掉小白和我，是因为我们跟着阿戈（Argo）在阿普托的时候见过、或者可能见过什么。我就想，或许他要除掉蛇，也是同一个缘故。这么一来，他没准从前也到过阿普托。”

===Original===
“Jordde was on Aptor before,” said Urson. “You said that’s when he became a spy for them.”
They all turned to Snake who stood quietly.
“I don’t think we ought to ask him any more questions,” said Iimmi. “The answers aren’t going to do us any good, and no matter what we find out, we’ve got a job to do, and seven, no﻿—six and a half days to do it in.”
Snake quietly handed the metal chain with the pendant jewel back to Iimmi. The dark man put it around his neck once more and they turned up the river.
---

===Chinese===
“约德从前就是去过阿普托的。”厄尔森说，“你说过，他就是那时候给他们当了奸细。”
众人都转向静静站着的蛇。
“我看咱们不该再问他什么了。”伊米说，“答案对咱们没什么好处。再说不管查出什么，手头的差事总得办——七天，不对——还有六天半。”
蛇默默地把那条拴着坠形珍宝的金属链子递还给伊米。黑皮肤汉子重新把它挂上脖颈，众人溯河而上。

---

===Original===
By twelve, the sun had parched the sky. Once they stopped to swim and cool themselves. Chill water gave before reaching arms and lowered faces. They even dove in search of their aquatic helpers, but grubbed the pebbly bottom of the river with blind fingers instead, coming up with dripping twigs and smooth wet stones. Soon, they were in a splashing match, of which it is fair to say, Snake won﻿—hands down.
Hunger thrust its sharp finger into their abdomens once more, only a mile on. “Maybe we should have saved some of that stuff from breakfast,” muttered Urson.
Iimmi suddenly broke away from the bank toward the forest.
“Come on,” he said. “Let’s get some food.”
The building they suddenly came upon had tongues of moss licking twenty to fifty feet up the loosely mortared stones. A hundred yards from the water, the jungle came right to its edges. The whole edifice had sunk a bit to one side in the boggy soil. It was a far more stolid and primitive structure than the barracks. They scraped and hacked in front of the entrance where two great columns of stone, six feet across at the base, rose fifty feet to a supported arch. The stones of the building were rough and unfinished.

===Chinese===
到晌午，太阳把天烤得干透。他们停下来游了一回泳，凉快凉快。冰凉的水在伸出的手臂和低俯的脸孔跟前让开。他们还潜下水去找那些水族的帮手，结果却只用盲摸索的手指翻检河底的卵石，捞上来的是滴水的树枝和溜光的湿石头。不多时，他们打起了水仗——公道地说，蛇赢了——赢得干净利落。
才走出一英里，饥饿又把尖尖的手指戳进他们的肚腹。“早知道该把早饭那些东西留下一部分。”厄尔森嘀咕道。
伊米突然离开河岸，朝森林斜插过去。
“来吧。”他说。“咱们找点吃的。”
他们猛然撞见的那座建筑上，苔藓的舌头正沿着灰浆松散的石头，舔到二十到五十英尺高处。离水一百码，丛林一直逼到它的边沿。整座建筑在泥沼般的软土里微微陷向一侧。比起营房来，它要拙重、原始得多。入口前立着两根巨大的石柱，基座六英尺粗，拔起五十英尺，托着一道拱。他们砍的砍、扒的扒，在入口前清出一条道。建筑的石料粗粝，未经琢磨。

===Original===
“It’s a temple,” Geo suddenly said.
And again they fell back to work. What spots of light spilled through the twisted net of jungle stopped at the total shadow beneath the great arch. A line of blackness up one side of the basalt door showed that it was ajar. Now they mounted the steps, moving aside a fallen branch which chattered leaves at them. Geo, Iimmi, then Snake, and at last Urson, squeezed through the door.
Ceiling blocks had fallen from the high vault so that three shafts of sun struck through the continual shift of dust to the littered floor.
“Do you think it’s Hama’s temple?” Urson asked. His voice came back in the stone room, small and hollow.
“I doubt it,” said Iimmi. “At least not the one we’re supposed to find.”

===Chinese===
“是座神庙。”吉奥忽然说。
他们又埋头干起活来。从纠结如网的丛林里漏下的光斑，到大拱底下那片全然的黑暗处便止了步。玄武岩门一侧透出一线黑，说明门虚掩着。他们登上台阶，挪开一根落下来的树枝——满枝叶子朝他们喋喋不休。吉奥、伊米，然后是蛇，最后是厄尔森，侧身挤进了门。
高高的拱顶塌落了几方石块，三道阳光柱穿过不断浮游的尘埃，直落在狼藉的地面上。
“你说这是哈马的神庙？”厄尔森问。他的声音在石室里荡回来，又细又空。
“我拿不准。”伊米说，“至少不是咱们要找的那一座。”

===Original===
“Maybe it’s an abandoned one,” said Geo, “and we can find out something useful from it.”
Something large and dark suddenly flapped through a far shaft of sun. They stepped back. After a moment of silence, Geo handed his jewel to Snake. “Make some light in here,” he said.
The blue green glow flowed from the upraised jewel in Snake’s hand. As the light flared, and flared brighter, they saw that the flapping had come from a medium-sized bird that was perched harmlessly on an arch that ran between two columns. It ducked its head at them, cawed harshly, and then flapped from its perch and out one of the apertures in the ceiling, the sound of its wings still thrumming in echo seconds after it was gone.
There were doors between the columns, and one far wall had not withstood time’s sledge. A gaping rent was nearly blocked with vines except for a dim, green-tinted shimmer that broke in here and there through the uneven foliage.
Behind a twisted metal rail and raised on steps of stone, the ruins of a huge statue sat. Carved from black rock, it represented a man seated cross-legged on a dais. An arm and shoulder had broken off and lay in pieces on the altar steps. The hand, its fingers as thick as Urson’s thigh, lay just behind the altar rail. The head was completely missing. Both the hand still on the statue and the one in front of them on the steps looked as though they had once held something, but whatever it was had been removed.

===Chinese===
“也许这是座废弃的。”吉奥说，“咱们兴许能从里头打听点有用的东西。”
一团黑乎乎的大东西突然扑棱着穿过远处一道光柱。他们倒退了一步。静了片刻，吉奥把他的珍宝递给蛇。“给这儿弄点亮。”他说。
蓝绿色的辉光从蛇手中高举的珍宝里流淌出来。那光一闪，越闪越亮，他们这才看清，方才扑棱的是一只中等个头的鸟，正安安稳稳栖在两柱之间的一道拱上。它朝他们点点头，哑着嗓子叫了一声，随即扑棱着离开栖处，从穹顶的一个孔洞飞了出去——它走后好几秒，翅膀的扑打声还在回响里嗡嗡震颤。
柱子之间还有一扇扇门，而远处一面墙没能扛住时间的重锤。一道豁开的大口子几乎被藤蔓堵死，只剩一片幽暗的、泛绿的微光，从参差的枝叶间这儿那儿地透进来。
一道扭曲的金属栏杆后面，几级石阶托着一尊巨大塑像的残躯。它由黑岩雕成，是个盘腿坐在台座上的人。一条手臂连着肩膀断落下来，摔成几截散在祭坛的台阶上。那只手，指头有厄尔森的大腿粗，就躺在祭坛栏杆后面。脑袋则全然不见了。塑像上残留的那只手，同台阶上摆在他们面前的这只手一样，看上去都曾握过什么东西，只是那东西已被取走了。

===Original===
Iimmi was moving along the rail to where a set of stone boxes were placed like foot stones along the side of the altar. “Here, Snake,” he called. “Bring a light over here.” Snake obeyed, and with Geo’s and Urson’s help, he loosened one of the lids.
“What’s in there?” Urson asked.
“Books,” said Iimmi, lifting out one dusty volume. Geo peered over his shoulder while the dark fingers turned the pages. “Old rituals,” Iimmi said. “Look here,” and he pointed to one of them. “You can still read them.”
“Let me see,” Geo said. “You know I studied with Eadnu at the University of Olcse Olwnh.”
Iimmi looked up and laughed. “I thought some of your ideas sounded familiar. I was a pupil of Welis.”
“You were at Olcse Olwnh too?” Geo asked.

===Chinese===
伊米沿着栏杆挪到一处，那里摆着一排石匣，像搁脚石似的沿祭坛一侧排开。“这儿，蛇。”他喊道，“把灯举过来。”蛇依言过去，在吉奥和厄尔森的帮助下，撬松了一只匣盖。
“里头是什么？”厄尔森问。
“是书。”伊米说着，捧出一册积灰的书。他黑色的手指翻动书页，吉奥从他肩后探看。“古老的仪式。”伊米说，“瞧这儿，”他指向其中一页。“上面的字还认得出来。”
“让我看看。”吉奥说，“你知道的，我师从埃阿德努（Eadnu），在奥尔克塞·奥尔温大学（University of Olcse Olwnh）念的书。”
伊米抬起头笑了。“我就说你有些念头听着耳熟。我是韦利斯（Welis）的学生。”
“你也上过奥尔克塞·奥尔温？”吉奥问。

===Original===
“Um-hm,” said Iimmi turning the pages. “I signed aboard this ship as a summer job. If I’d known where we’d end up, I don’t think I’d have gone, though.”
Stomach pangs were forgotten.
“These rituals are not at all like those of the Goddess,” Iimmi observed.
“Apparently not,” agreed Geo. “Wait!” Iimmi had been turning pages at random. “Look there!” Geo pointed.
“What is it?” Iimmi asked.
“The lines,” Geo said. “The ones Argo recited.” He read out loud:

===Chinese===
“嗯哼。”伊米一边翻着书页一边说，“我上那条船，本是暑期打工。不过要是早知道会落到这步田地，我看我就不去了。”
肚子饿的绞痛被忘到了脑后。
“这些仪式跟女神的那套完全不一样。”伊米说。
“显然不一样。”吉奥附和道。“等等！”伊米一直在随手翻页。“看那儿！”吉奥指着。
“怎么了？”伊米问。
“是那几句诗。”吉奥说，“阿戈念过的那几句。”他朗声读道：

===Original===

> “Forked in the heart of the dark oak
 

 the circlet of his sash
 

 rimmed where the eye of Hama broke
 

 with fire, smoke, and ash.
> Freeze the drop in the hand
 

 and break the earth with singing.
 

 Hail the height of a man
 

 and also the height of a woman.
> The eyes have imprisoned a vision.
 

 The ash tree dribbles with blood.
 

 Thrust from the gates of the prison
 

 smear the yew tree with mud.”

“It’s the other version of the poem I found in the pre-purge rituals of Argo. I wonder if there were any more poems in the old rituals of Leptar that parallel those of Aptor and Hama?”

===Chinese===

> “在黑橡木的心间分杈，
 

 他腰带上的圆环，
 

 镶在哈马之眼迸裂处，
 

 带着火、烟和灰烬。
> 把手中的水滴冻结，
 

 用歌声击碎大地。
 

 欢呼男人的身量，
 

 也欢呼女人的身量。
> 众眼囚住了一个幻象。
 

 白蜡树滴淌着血。
 

 把它逐出牢狱之门，
 

 拿泥涂抹紫杉。”

“这是那首诗的另一个版本，我在阿戈清洗前的仪式里发现过它。不知莱普塔的古老仪式里，还有没有别的诗，跟阿普托和哈马的这些对得上？”

===Original===
“Probably,” Iimmi said. “Especially if the first invasion from Aptor took place just before, and probably caused, the purges.”
“What about food?” Urson suddenly asked from where he now sat on the altar steps. “You two scholars have the rest of time to argue. But we may starve before you can enjoy the leisure.”
“He’s right,” said Iimmi. “Besides, we have to get going.”
“Would you two consider it an imposition to set your minds to procuring us some food?” Urson asked.
“Wait a minute,” Iimmi said. “Here’s a section on the burial of the dead. Yes, I thought so.” He read out loud now:

===Chinese===
“多半有。”伊米说，“尤其是，倘若阿普托的第一次入侵正好发生在那场大清洗（the purges）之前——而且多半正是它的起因。”
“吃的呢？”厄尔森突然开了口，他这会儿正坐在祭坛台阶上。“你们两位学究有的是来日方长好吵架。可只怕还没等你们消受这份清闲，我们先饿死了。”
“他说得对。”伊米说，“再说，咱们也该动身了。”
“有劳二位费费脑子，给咱们弄点吃食来——不会嫌这是强人所难吧？”厄尔森问道。
“且慢。”伊米说，“这儿有一节讲丧葬的。果然，跟我猜的一样。”他朗声读道：

===Original===

> “Sink the bright dead with misgiving
 

 from the half-light of the living﻿ ﻿…”


===Chinese===

> “将光鲜的死者沉埋，怀着疑惧，
 

 离开生者半明的微光……”

“这是什么意思？”厄尔森问。

===Original===
“What does that mean?” asked Urson.
“It means that the dead are buried with all the accoutrements of the living. That means that they put food in the graves.”
“Over here,” cried Iimmi. With Snake following, they came to the row of sealed doors behind the columns along the wall. Iimmi looked at the inscription. “Tombs,” he reported. He turned the handles, a double set of rings, which he twisted in opposite directions. “In an old, uncared-for temple like this, the lock mechanisms must have rusted by now if they’re at all like the ancient tombs of Leptar.”
“Have you studied the ancient tombs?” asked Geo excitedly. “Professor Eadnu always considered them a waste of time.”
“That’s all Welis ever talked about,” laughed Iimmi. “Here, Urson, you set your back to this a moment.”
Grumbling, Urson came forward, took the rings, and twisted. One snapped off in his hand. The other gave, with a crumbling sound inside the door.
“I think that does it,” Iimmi said.

===Chinese===
“意思是说，死者下葬时，要随葬生者用度的一切。也就是说，坟里是放了吃食的。”
“这边。”伊米喊道。蛇跟在后面，他们来到沿墙柱子后头那一排封着的门前。伊米看了看铭文。“是墓室。”他报告说。他转动把手——那是双环的构造——朝相反方向拧。“这样一座年久失修的老庙，要是机关跟莱普塔的古墓一个样，这会儿早该锈死了。”
“你研究过古墓？”吉奥兴奋地问，“埃阿德努教授一直认为那是浪费时间。”
“韦利斯张口闭口全是这个。”伊米笑道，“来，厄尔森，你抵着这个使把劲。”
厄尔森嘟嘟囔囔地走上前，抓住双环，一拧。一只环咔地断在他手里。另一只松了——门里传来一阵碎裂的闷响。
“我看成了。”伊米说。
众人一齐上来拉，门忽然开了一寸，再一使劲，便豁然洞开。

===Original===
They all helped pull now, and suddenly the door gave an inch, and then, on the next tug, swung free.
Snake proceeded them into the tiny stone cell.
On a rock table, lying on its side, was a bald, shriveled, sexless body. Around the floor were a few sealed jars, heaps of parchment, and a few piles of ornaments.
Iimmi moved among the jars. “This one has grain,” he said. “Give me a hand.” Geo helped him lug the big pottery vessel to the door.
Suddenly a thin shriek scarred the dusty air, and both boys stumbled. The jar hit the ground, split, and grain heaped over the floor. The shriek came again.
Geo saw, there on the edge of the broken wall across the temple from them five of the apelike figures crouched before the thickly shingled leaves, just visible in the uneven light. One leapt from the wall now and ran wailing across the littered temple floor, straight for the door of the tomb. Two others followed, and then two others. More had mounted the broken ridge of stone.
Only a greenish rectangle of light fell through the tomb’s door as the loping forms burst into the room, one, and then its two companions. Claws and teeth closed on the shriveled skin. The body rolled beneath the ripping hands and mouths, for one arm swept into the air above their lowered heads and humped backs. It fell on the edge of the rock table, broke at the mid-forearm, and the skeletal hand fell to the floor, shattering like china, into a dozen pieces.
They backed to the temple door. Then they turned and ran down the temple steps. The sunlight on the broad rocks touched them; they became still, breathed deeply. They walked quietly. Hunger returned slowly after that, and occasionally one would look aside into the faces of the others in attempt to identify the horror that still pulsed behind their eyes.

===Chinese===
蛇先行一步，进了那间小小的石室。
一张石桌上，侧躺着一具秃顶干瘪、不辨男女的躯体。地板四下散着几只封口的陶罐、一堆堆羊皮纸，还有几摞饰物。
伊米在陶罐间转。“这只装的是谷子。”他说，“搭把手。”吉奥帮他抬起那只大陶瓮，挪向门口。
突然，一声尖啸划破了浮尘弥漫的空气，两个小伙子都打了个趔趄。陶瓮砸在地上，裂开，谷粒泻了一地。尖啸又起。
吉奥看见了：在神庙那头断墙的边上，五个人猿般的身影蹲伏在层叠如瓦的密叶前，在参差的光线里隐约可辨。这时一个从墙上跳下来，嚎叫着奔过狼藉的庙面，直扑墓室的门。又跟上来两个，再两个。还有更多的正攀上断裂的石脊。
那些颠扑的身影冲进墓室时，只有一方泛绿的光从门口斜落进来——一个，随即是它的两个同伴。爪和牙咬上干瘪的皮肤。尸体在撕扯的手和嘴下翻滚，一条胳膊甩上半空，高过它们低下的头和隆起的背。胳膊砸在石桌边上，前臂中段应声而断，那只骸骨的手摔到地上，像瓷器一样碎成了十几片。
他们退到庙门口。随后他们转身奔下庙前的台阶。宽石上的阳光抚上他们；他们静了下来，深深呼吸。他们安安静静地走。那之后，饥饿缓缓地卷土重来，偶尔会有人侧过脸，去端详旁人的脸，想认出那仍在各人眼底搏动的惊怖。

## VII / 七、死城夜行

===Original===
It was Urson who first pointed it out. “Look at the far bank,” he said.
Across from them, they could make out an obviously man-made stone embankment.
A few hundred feet further on, Iimmi sighted the spires above the trees, still across the river from them. They could figure nothing for an explanation, till suddenly the trees ceased on the opposite bank and the buildings and towers of a great city broke the sky. Elevated highways looped tower after tower, many of them broken, their ends dangling colossally to the streets. The docks of the city just across from them were completely deserted.
It was Geo who suggested, “Perhaps Hama’s temple is in there. After all, Argo’s largest temple is in Leptar’s biggest city.”

===Chinese===
最先指出来的是厄尔森。“瞧对岸。”他说。

在他们对面，可以辨出一道显然是人工筑成的石堤。

再往前几百英尺，伊米望见了树梢上方的尖顶，依然在河对岸。他们想不出任何解释，直到对岸的树木戛然而止，一座大城的楼宇和高塔划破了天空。高架公路在一座座塔楼间盘绕，其中许多已经断裂，断头庞大无比地垂荡到街面上。正对着他们的那片城市码头，空无一人。

开口提议的是吉奥：“哈马的神庙说不定就在里面。毕竟，阿戈最大的神庙就设在莱普塔最大的城里。”

===Original===
“And what city in Leptar is *that* big?” breathed Urson, awfully.
“How do we get across?” asked Iimmi.
But Snake had already started down to the water.
“I guess we follow him,” said Geo, climbing down over the rocks.

===Chinese===
“莱普塔哪座城有*那么*大？”厄尔森骇然吸了口气。

“咱们怎么过河？”伊米问。

可蛇已经动身朝水边走去。

“看来只有跟上他了。”吉奥说着，翻下岩石。

===Original===
Snake dove into the water. Iimmi, Geo, and Urson followed. Before he had taken two strokes, Geo felt familiar hands suddenly grasp his body from below. This time he did not fight, and there was a sudden sense of speed, of sinking through consciousness.
Then he was bobbing up through chill water with the rising embankment of stones to one side and the broad river to the other. He switched from sculling into a crawl now, wondering how to scale the stones when he saw the rusted metal ladder leading into the water. He caught hold of the sides and pulled himself up.
Snake came up now, and then Urson. And, at last Iimmi joined them on the broad ridge of concrete that walled the flowing river. Together now on the wharf, they turned to the city.

===Chinese===
蛇潜入水中。伊米、吉奥和厄尔森跟着下了水。吉奥还没划上两下，便觉得那双熟悉的手从下面猛地抓住了他的身体。这一回他没有挣扎，只觉一阵疾速，意识随之沉落。

随后他从冰冷的水里浮上来，一侧是渐渐升高的石堤，另一侧是宽阔的河面。他从拨水改成了自由泳，正盘算怎么攀上石堤，就看见一道锈迹斑斑的铁梯没入水中。他抓住梯边，把自己拽了上去。

蛇上来了，接着是厄尔森。最后，伊米也上了那道拦住奔流的河水的宽阔混凝土堤棱，同他们会合。四人一齐站在码头上，转身面向城市。

===Original===
Near them, piles of debris lay between two taller buildings. After a few minutes’ walk the building walls had reached canyon size. “Now, how are you going to go about looking for the temple?” Urson asked.
“Maybe we can take a look from the top of one of these buildings,” Geo suggested.

===Chinese===
他们近旁，两座较高的楼之间堆着一堆堆瓦砾。走出几分钟，楼壁已经高得成了峡谷。“喂，你们打算怎么着手找那座神庙？”厄尔森问。

“也许可以到其中一座楼顶上看看。”吉奥提议。

===Original===
They turned toward a random building. A slab of metal had torn away from the wall, and stepping through, they found themselves in a huge hollow room. Dim light came from a number of white tubes set around the wall. Only a quarter of them were lit, and one was flickering. Hung from the center of the room was a metal sign which read:
> New Edison Electric Company
and beneath it, in smaller letters:
> “Light Down The Ages”

===Chinese===
他们朝一座随手挑中的楼走去。墙上撕裂开一块金属板，跨进去，他们发现自己置身在一个巨大的空腔房间里。沿墙四周嵌着许多白色灯管，透出微光。只有四分之一还亮着，其中一支忽明忽暗。房间正中悬着一块金属招牌，上面写着：

> 新爱迪生电气公司（New Edison Electric Company）

招牌下方，还有一行小字：

> “光照万代”（Light Down The Ages）

===Original===
One of the huge cylinders, across the floor, was buzzing.
As they mounted a spiral staircase to the next floor the great room turned about them, sinking. At last they stepped up into a dark corridor. A red light glowed at the end which said: EXIT.
Doors outlined themselves along the hall in a red haze. Geo moved to one at random and opened it. Natural light fell in on them as the others came to see. They entered a room whose outer wall was torn away. The floor broke off irregularly over thrusting girders.

===Chinese===
房间那头的一排巨大圆筒里，有一只嗡嗡作响。

他们沿一道旋梯爬上二层，那个大房间绕着他们旋转、下沉。终于，他们踏进一条黑暗的走廊。尽头亮着一盏红灯，上面写着：出口（EXIT）。

一扇扇门在红雾中显出轮廓。吉奥随手挑了一扇走过去，推开门。其他人凑上来看时，自然光洒落在他们身上。他们走进一个外墙整个被撕掉的房间。地板参差不齐地断裂，架在一根根探出的桁梁上。

===Original===
“What could have happened to it?” Urson asked.
“See,” Iimmi explained. “That roadway must have crashed into the wall and knocked it away.”
A twenty-foot ribbon of road veered into the room at an insane angle. The railing was twisted, but there were the stalks of street lights still intact along the edges.
“Do you think we could climb that?” asked Geo. “It doesn’t look too steep.”

===Chinese===
“它到底出了什么事？”厄尔森问。

“瞧，”伊米解释道，“准是那条车道撞进了墙里，把它撞飞的。”

一条二十英尺宽的路带，以一个疯魔的角度斜插进房间。护栏拧得七扭八歪，可沿边的路灯灯杆倒还完好。

“你看咱们能爬上去吗？”吉奥问，“瞧着不算太陡。”

===Original===
“For what?” Urson wanted to know.
“To get some place high enough to see if there’s anything that looks like a temple.”
“Oh,” said Urson in a reconciled voice.
In general the walk was in good shape. Occasional sections of railing had twisted away, but the road itself mounted surely between the sheering faces of the buildings on either side of them through advancing sunset.

===Chinese===
“爬上去干什么？”厄尔森想知道。

“找个够高的地方，看看有没有什么像神庙的东西。”

“哦。”厄尔森的口气算是认了。

这条便道大体还算完好。偶尔几段护栏拧脱了，但路面本身笃定地攀升，在两侧大楼陡直的楼壁之间，穿过渐渐深浓的落日。
===Original===
It branched before them and they went left. It branched again and again they avoided the right-handed road. A sign, half the length of a three masted ship, hung lopsidedly above them on a building to one side.
> WMTH
> The Hub Of World News, Communication, & Entertainment
As they rounded the corner of the building, Snake suddenly stopped and put his hand to his head.

===Chinese===
路在前面分了岔，他们走了左边。又分岔时，他们又一次避开了右手的道。一块足有三桅船一半长的招牌，歪歪斜斜地悬在他们头顶一侧的楼上。

> WMTH

> 世界新闻、通讯与娱乐的中枢（The Hub Of World News, Communication, & Entertainment）

他们绕过楼角时，蛇突然停住，抬手按住脑袋。

===Original===
“What is it?” asked Geo.
Snake took a step backward. Then he pointed to WMTH. It﻿ ﻿… hurts.
“What hurts?” asked Iimmi.
Snake pointed to the building again.

===Chinese===
“怎么了？”吉奥问。

蛇退了一步，随即指向WMTH。它……疼。

“什么疼？”伊米问。

蛇又指了指那座楼。

===Original===
“Is there someone in there thinking too loud?”
Thinking﻿ ﻿… machine, Snake said. Radio﻿ ﻿…
“A radio is a thinking machine and there’s one in there that’s hurting your head?” interpreted Iimmi, tentatively, and with a question mark.
Snake nodded.

===Chinese===
“是里面有人在想事情，想得太响了吗？”

思维……机器，蛇说。电台……

“电台就是思维机器，里面有一台正弄得你脑袋疼？”伊米试探着解读，语气里挂着个问号。

蛇点头。

===Original===
“How come the one he showed us before didn’t hurt him?” Urson wanted to know.
Iimmi looked up at the imposing housing of WMTH. “Maybe this one’s a lot bigger.”
“Look,” Geo said to Snake, “you stay here, and if we see anything, we’ll come back and report, all right?”
“Maybe it stops later on,” Urson said, “and if he ran forward, he could get out the other side. It may just stop after a hundred feet or so.”

===Chinese===
“那他先前指给咱们看的那台，怎么没弄疼他？”厄尔森想知道。

伊米抬头看了看WMTH那气势逼人的楼体。“也许这一台要大得多。”

“听我说，”吉奥对蛇说，“你待在这儿，我们要是看见了什么，就回来告诉你，好不好？”

“兴许再往前就停了，”厄尔森说，“他要是一口气往前跑，就能从另一头跑出去。说不定跑个一百英尺左右就没事了。”

===Original===
“Why so anxious?” asked Iimmi.
“The jewels,” said Urson. “Who’s going to get us out of trouble if we should meet up with anything else?”
They were silent then. Their shadows faded over the pavement as the yellow tinge in the sky turned blue. “I guess it’s up to Snake,” Geo said. “Do you think you can make it?”
Snake paused for a moment, then shook his head.

===Chinese===
“你急什么？”伊米问。

“还不是为了珍宝，”厄尔森说，“万一咱们再撞上别的东西，谁来救咱们脱身？”

一时间没人作声。天上的黄色转成蓝色，他们的影子在路面上渐渐淡去。“我看这就由蛇定了，”吉奥说，“你觉得自己撑得住吗？”

蛇顿了顿，摇了摇头。

===Original===
“Well,” Geo said to the others, “come on then.”
Around them was a sudden click, and lights flickered all along the edges of the road.
“Come on,” Geo said again, and once more they started, passing the lights which wheeled double and triple shadows about them over the road and the opposite railing. When they reached the next turn off that led to a still higher ramp, Geo looked back. Snake’s miniature figure sat on the edge of the road’s railing, his feet on the lower rung, one pair of arms folded, one pair of elbows on his knees. The light above him.
“Keep track of the turns,” said Geo.

===Chinese===
“那好，”吉奥对众人说，“走吧。”

他们周围咔哒一响，灯光顺着路缘一路闪烁亮起。

“走吧。”吉奥又说了一遍，他们重新上路，经过一盏盏灯，灯光把重重叠叠的影子投在他们身上、路面上和对面的护栏上。走到通往更高一层坡道的下一个岔口时，吉奥回头望去。蛇小小的身影坐在路边护栏上，双脚踩着下面一格横杆，一双手臂环抱着，另一双的肘支在膝上。灯就在他头顶。

“记着拐弯。”吉奥说。

===Original===
“I’m keeping,” Iimmi assured him.
“By the time we get to the top of whatever we’re trying to get to the top of,” rumbled Urson, “we won’t be able to see anything. It’ll be too dark.”
“Then let’s hurry,” Geo admonished.

===Chinese===
“记着呢。”伊米向他保证。

“等咱们爬上那个不管什么顶的顶，”厄尔森瓮声瓮气地说，“就什么都瞧不见了。天就黑透了。”

“那就快走。”吉奥催促道。

===Original===
Sunset stained one side of the towers copper while blue shadows hugged the other. By way of a plastic-domed stairway, they mounted another eighty feet to a broader highway where they could look down on the band of lights which was the one they had just left. They were beginning to clear the roofs of the lower buildings now.
On this road fewer lights were working. They were just about to enter a dark section when a figure appeared in silhouette at the other end.
They stopped, but the figure was suddenly gone. A little farther, Geo suddenly halted and said, “There!”
Two hundred feet ahead of them, what may have been a naked woman rose from the ground, and began to walk backwards until she disappeared into the next dark length of road.

===Chinese===
落日把塔楼的一面染成铜色，蓝影则紧贴着另一面。他们经一道塑料穹顶的楼梯，又登上八十英尺，来到一条更宽的公路上，从这里可以俯瞰他们刚刚离开的那条灯光带。此刻他们已开始越过那些较矮楼房的屋顶。

这条路上亮着的灯更少。他们正要走进一段黑路，对面尽头出现了一个人的剪影。

他们停下，那影子却倏地不见了。又走出一小段，吉奥猛地站住说：“在那儿！”

前方两百英尺处，一个像是裸体女人的东西从地上立起，开始倒退着走，直到消失进下一段黑黢黢的路影里。

===Original===
“Do you think she was running away from us?” Iimmi asked.
Urson reached out and touched Iimmi’s jewel. “I wish we have some more light around here.”
“Yeah,” Iimmi agreed. They continued.
The skeleton lay at the twilight edge of the next stretch of functioning lights. The rib cage marked sharp lines on the pavement with shadow from the lamps’ glare.

===Chinese===
“你看她是在躲着咱们走？”伊米问。

厄尔森伸出手，碰了碰伊米的珍宝。“真希望这周围能再亮一些。”

“可不是。”伊米应道。他们继续走。

下一片亮灯路段的暮色边上，躺着一具骷髅。灯光灼照，肋骨在路面上投下棱角分明的影子。

===Original===
“Do we turn back now?” Urson asked.
“A skeleton can’t hurt you,” Iimmi said.
“But what about the live one we saw?” countered Urson.
“… and here she comes now,” Geo whispered in a cynical stage voice.

===Chinese===
“咱们现在往回走？”厄尔森问。

“骷髅又伤不了你。”伊米说。

“可咱们瞧见的那一个活人呢？”厄尔森反驳。

“……瞧，她这就来了。”吉奥用一种冷嘲的舞台腔悄声说。

===Original===
In fact two figures approached them through the shadow. As Urson, Geo and Iimmi moved closer, one stopped, and then the other a few steps before the first. Then they dropped. Geo couldn’t tell if they fell, or lay down quickly on the roadway. But they seemed to have disappeared.
“Go on?” asked Urson.
“Go on,” said Geo.
Pause. “Go on,” from Geo.

===Chinese===
果然有两个身影穿过阴影朝他们走来。厄尔森、吉奥和伊米往前逼近时，一个停住了，另一个又走出几步，停在头一个前面几步远。随后他们倒了下去。吉奥分不清他们是栽倒了，还是飞快地趴到了路面上。总之他们像是消失了。

“还走吗？”厄尔森问。

“走。”吉奥说。

停了停。“走吧。”还是吉奥。

===Original===
Two more skeletons lay on the road where the figures had disappeared a minute before. “They don’t seem dangerous,” Geo said. “But what do they do? Die every time they see us?”
“Hey,” Iimmi said. “What’s that? Listen.”
It was a sickly liquid sound, like mud dropping into itself. Something was falling from the sky. No, not the sky, but from the roadway that crossed fifty feet above them. Looking down again, they saw that a blob of something was growing on the pavement ten feet from them.
“Come on,” Geo said, and they skirted the mess dripping from above them, and continued up the road, passing four more skeletons. The sound behind them turned into a wet sloshing. Turning, they saw it emerge into the light﻿—shapeless and jelly-green under the white flare. Impaling its membrane on the skeletons, the mass flowed around them, faster, covering them, molding to them. There was a final surge, a shrinking, and its shapelessness contracted into limbs, a head, feet. The naked man-thing pushed itself to its knees and then stood straight, the flesh by now opaque. Eye sockets caved into the face. A mouth ripped apart on the skull, and the chest began to move with a wet steamy sound in irregular gasps.

===Chinese===
方才那两个身影消失的地方，这会儿躺上了两具骷髅。“它们瞧着不凶，”吉奥说，“可它们这是干什么？每回见着咱们就死一回？”

“喂，”伊米说，“那是什么？听。”

那是一种黏腻发闷的液体声，像泥浆往自己身子里塌。有什么东西正从天上掉下来。不，不是天上，是从他们头顶五十英尺处横穿的公路上。再往下一看，只见一团什么东西正在离他们十英尺的路面上越长越大。

“走。”吉奥说。他们绕开头顶滴落的脏东西，继续沿路而上，又经过四具骷髅。身后的声响变成一片湿漉漉的泼溅。他们回头，看见它涌进了灯光里——在白色光焰下它无形无状，透着冻胶的绿。这团东西把它的膜体扎上骷髅，绕着它们漫流，越来越快，把它们罩住，贴着它们塑形。最后一涌，一缩，那团无形的东西收缩出四肢、一颗头、一双脚。那赤裸的人形之物撑地跪起，随即站直，这时的肉体已经不透明了。眼窝陷进脸孔。颅骨上一张嘴豁然裂开，胸腔开始起伏，伴着湿漉漉、热腾腾的声响，喘得忽紧忽慢。

===Original===
It began to walk toward them, raising its hands from its sides. Then, behind it in the darkness, they saw more coming.
“*Damn*,” said Urson. “What do they﻿ ﻿… ?”
“One, or both, of two things,” Iimmi answered, backing away. “More meat, or more bones.”

===Chinese===
它朝他们走来，双手从身侧抬了起来。

“*该死*，”厄尔森说，“它们是要……？”

“两样东西里的一样，或者两样都要，”伊米一边后退一边答道，“更多的肉，或者更多的骨头。”

===Original===
“Whoops,” Geo said. “Look back there!”
They whirled and saw seven more figures standing quietly behind them, while the ones in front advanced.

===Chinese===
“嗬，”吉奥说，“瞧后头！”

他们霍然回身，只见又有七个身影悄没声地立在他们身后，而前头的那些正步步进逼。

===Original===
A covered flight of stairs had its entrance nearby, leading to the next level of highway. They ducked into it and fled up the steps. Geo glanced back once; one of the forms had reached the entrance and had started to climb. He was also, he realized, high enough to get some idea of the city, which stretched, beyond the transparent covering of the steps, away in a web of lighted roadways, rising, looping, descending. Two glows caught him: one, beyond the river, a red haze that flickered behind the trees and was reflected on the water itself. The other was within the city itself, orange white, nested among the buildings.
He turned back up the steps. A gurgling sound neared them as they reached the top entrance. Geo had only gotten half clear of the entrance when he yelled, “Yikes,” and then, “Duck!”

===Chinese===
近旁有一道带顶的楼梯，通向再上一层的公路。他们一头钻进去，奔上台阶。吉奥回头瞥了一眼：一个人形已经到了楼梯口，开始往上爬。他还发觉，自己也已经站得够高，能大致看清这座城了——透过台阶透明的顶罩，城池在一张亮着灯的公路网里铺展开去，升起，盘绕，又降下。有两处光攫住了他：一处在河对岸，一团红雾在树后明灭，倒映在水面上；另一处就在城里，橙白色，栖在楼群之间。

他转回身继续上台阶。他们到达顶部出口时，一阵咕噜咕噜的声音逼近了。吉奥刚从出口探出半个身子，就叫了一声“哎呀”，接着喊：“低头！”

===Original===
They slipped from the doorway and nearly fell, avoiding a mass of jelly the size of a two-story house which flopped against the entrance. They edged by its pulsing, transparent sides. The lamp light pierced into it a yard, and once a skull swirled toward the surface and then sank again.
Suddenly it sucked away from the entrance and shivered ponderously toward them. Something was happening at the front. Figures, three or four of them, were detaching themselves from the mother mass and preceding it.
They turned and ran along the road, plunging suddenly into an extended darkened section. A moment later there was a glow in front of them and suddenly Urson yelled, “Watch it!”

===Chinese===
他们从门口溜出来，险些摔倒，躲开一大团两层楼高的冻胶——它正啪嗒一声扑在出口上。他们贴着它搏动的、半透明的侧体蹭过去。灯光扎进它体内一码深，有一颗骷髅一度打着旋浮向表面，又沉了下去。

突然，它从出口上抽身，笨重地颤动着朝他们涌来。前头起了变化。三个人形，也许是四个，正从母体上剥离出来，走在它前头。

他们转身沿路狂奔，一头扎进一段长长的黑路。片刻后，前方亮起一片光，厄尔森猛地大吼：“小心！”

===Original===
Abruptly the road sheered off in front of them; they halted, and then approached the edge slowly. The surface of the road tore away and the girders descended, webbing toward the ruined stump of a building from which the orange-white glow rose. The glow came from the heart of the edifice. “What do you think it is?” asked Geo.
“I don’t know,” said Iimmi.
They looked, and in the shadow, numberless figures were marching after them. Suddenly the figures fell to the ground, and flesh rolled forward from bone, congealed, and rose quiveringly into the edge of the light.

===Chinese===
公路在他们面前陡然断掉；他们收住脚，然后慢慢挪到边缘。路面撕裂开去，桁梁节节而下，织向一座建筑残断的墩座——那橙白色的光就是从那里升起来的。光发自那建筑的心脏。“你说那是什么？”吉奥问。

“我不知道。”伊米说。

他们望去，阴影里，数不清的人影正列队追来。骤然间那些身影扑倒在地，血肉从骨头上滚涌出来，凝成形，颤巍巍地立进了灯光的边缘。

===Original===
Iimmi started out first on the skeletal, twisted structure that descended to the glowing pit. “You’re crazy,” Geo said. The thing flopped forward another yard with a sick sound. “Hurry up,” Geo added. With Urson in the middle, they started out along the twenty-inch wide girder. Lit from beneath, their bodies were in the shadow of the girder. Only their outstretched arms burned in the pale orange light as they balanced themselves.
Before them, faintly legible on the broken building into which they were descending was the sign:
> Atomic Energy for the Betterment of Man
It was flanked by two purple trefoils. The beam twisted sideways, and then dropped. Iimmi made the turn, dropped to his knees and hands, and then started to let himself down the four feet to the next small section of concrete. Once he saw something, let out a low whistle, but continued to lower himself to the straightened girder. Urson made the turn next, while Geo knelt in front of him. When Urson saw what Iimmi had seen, his hand shot to Geo’s chest and grabbed the jewel. Geo took his wrist. “That won’t help us now,” he said.

===Chinese===
伊米头一个踏上那副通往发光深坑的、骸骨似的扭曲结构。“你疯了。”吉奥说。那东西又啪地朝前瘫出一码，声响发腻。“快点。”吉奥补了一句。厄尔森居中，他们沿着那根二十英寸宽的桁梁走了出去。光从底下照上来，他们的身子都隐在桁梁的阴影里。只有他们张开以保持平衡的手臂，在惨橙色的光里灼灼发亮。

他们正往下走去的那座残楼上，依稀可辨一块招牌：

> 原子能造福人类（Atomic Energy for the Betterment of Man）

招牌两侧各有一个紫色的三叶符号。梁身一拐，随即垂落。伊米转过弯，落到双膝和双手上，开始往下探那四英尺，落向下一小块混凝土。他一眼瞥见什么，低低吹了声口哨，但还是继续把自己放到那根扳直了的桁梁上。厄尔森接着转弯，吉奥跪在他前头。待厄尔森看清伊米看见的东西，他的手猛地探向吉奥的胸口，一把攥住了珍宝。吉奥扣住他的手腕。“这会儿它帮不上咱们了。”他说。

===Original===
Urson expelled a breath, and then continued down, slowly. Quickly Geo turned to drop now.
The entire beam structure over which they had just come was coated with a trembling thickness of the stuff. Globs dripped from the steel shafts, glowing in the light from below, quivering, smoking, splashing off into the darkness. Here and there something half human would rise either to look around or to pull the collective mass further on, but then it would fall back and dissolve. It bulged forward, smoking now, bits of it shriveling off and falling away. Geo was about to descend, but suddenly he called, “Wait a minute.” The others stayed still.
It wasn’t making progress. It rolled to a certain point in the pale, sherbert-colored light, globbed up, smoked, and fell away. And smoked. And dripped.
“Can’t it get any farther?” Urson asked.

===Chinese===
厄尔森吐出一口气，随后慢慢接着往下。吉奥也赶紧转身往下落。

他们刚才走过的整副梁架，已经裹上了一层颤巍巍的、厚厚的那种东西。一团团黏块从钢梁上滴淌，在下方来的光里发亮、颤动、冒烟，飞溅进黑暗。到处有半人半形的东西立起来，或是四下张望，或是把那团集体的物质再往前拽，随即又倒回去，化开。它鼓涌着向前，此刻冒着烟，碎块一片片焦缩脱落。吉奥正要下降，忽然喊了一声：“等等。”另外两人一动不动。

它进展不下去了。它滚到那片浅淡的果子露色光亮里的某一点，团起来，冒烟，塌下去。又冒烟。又滴淌。

“它到不了更远的地方了？”厄尔森问。

===Original===
“It doesn’t look it,” said Geo.
A skeleton stood up, flesh-covered in the orange light. It tottered, its surface steaming, and then fell with a sucking noise, down into the hundreds of feet of shadow. Geo was holding tight onto the girder in front of him.
The pale light fell cleanly over his hand, wrist, and midway up his forearm.
What happened now made him squeeze until sweat came: the entire Gargantuan mass, which had only extended tentacles till now, pulsed to the edge of the jagged road, draped itself over the web of girders, and flung itself forward on the spindly metal threads. It careened toward them, and the three jerked themselves back.

===Chinese===
“瞧着是这样。”吉奥说。

橙光里，一具裹着血肉的骷髅站了起来。它摇摇晃晃，表面蒸腾着热气，随即带着一声吮吸似的声响栽了下去，落进几百英尺深的阴影。吉奥紧紧扒住面前的桁梁。

惨淡的光清晰地照在他的手、手腕上，直到小臂一半的地方。

接下来发生的事，让他攥得出了汗：那整个巨灵般的庞然大物——此前只是伸出过触须——这时搏动着涌到豁牙咧嘴的路缘，把自己披挂上桁梁之网，顺着一条条细弱的金属线猛地朝前甩过来。它朝他们横冲直撞，三个人猛地缩身后仰。

===Original===
Then it stopped, quivering. It boiled, it burned, it writhed, sinking, smoking through the spaces in the naked girder work. It tried to crawl backwards. Human figures leaped from its mass toward the edge of the road, missed, and plummeted like smoking bullets. It hurled a great pseudopod back toward the safety of the road; it fell short, flopped downward, and the whole mass shook beneath the smoke that rose from it. It pulled free of the support, tentacles sliding across steel, whipping into the air. Then it dropped into the shadows, breaking into a half dozen pieces before they lost sight of it below.
Geo released his hand. “My arm hurts,” he said, shaking it.
They climbed up to the road again, carefully. “Any ideas what happened?” asked Iimmi.
“What ever it was, I’m glad it did,” said Urson.

===Chinese===
随后它停住了，颤抖着。它翻滚，它燃烧，它扭动，在赤裸的梁架空隙间下沉、冒烟。它想往回爬。一个个人形从它身上朝路缘跃去，扑了空，像冒着烟的子弹一样坠落。它把一条巨大的伪足甩回路面那安全的一侧；差了一点，软塌塌地垂了下去，整个躯体在自身升起的烟下簌簌发抖。它从支撑上挣脱，触须滑过钢材，抽向空中。然后它坠进阴影，在他们的视线追到底下之前，摔成了六块。

吉奥松开手。“我胳膊疼。”他说着甩了甩胳膊。

他们小心翼翼地重新爬上公路。“想明白刚才是怎么回事了吗？”伊米问。

“不管是怎么回事，反正发生了，我挺高兴。”厄尔森说。

===Original===
Something clattered before them in the darkness.
“What was that?” asked Urson, stopping.
“My foot hit something,” Geo said.
“What was it?” asked Urson.

===Chinese===
黑暗中，有什么东西在他们前面哐啷响了一声。

“那是什么？”厄尔森停下来问。

“我的脚碰到什么东西了。”吉奥说。

“是什么？”厄尔森问。

===Original===
“Never mind,” said Geo. “Come on.”
Fifteen minutes brought them to the stairway that went to the lower highway. Iimmi’s memory proved good, and for an hour they went quickly, Iimmi making no hesitation at turnings.
“God,” Geo said, rubbing his forearm with his other hand. “I must have pulled hell out of it back there. It hurts like the devil.”
Urson looked at his hand and rubbed them together.

===Chinese===
“别管了，”吉奥说，“走。”

一刻钟后，他们到了通往下层公路的楼梯。伊米的记性果然不差，整整一个钟头他们都走得飞快，每到拐弯，伊米毫不迟疑。

“天哪，”吉奥用另一只手揉着小臂，“刚才我准是把它拼到头了。疼得要命。”

厄尔森看看自己的手，又把两只手互相搓了搓。

===Original===
“My hands feel sort of funny too,” Iimmi said. “Like they’ve been windburned.”
“Windburned nothing,” said Geo. “This hurts.”
Twenty minutes later, Iimmi said, “Well, this should be about it.”
“Hey,” said Urson. “There’s Snake.” As they ran forward, now, the boy jumped off the rail, grabbed their shoulders, and grinned. Then he began to tug them forward.

===Chinese===
“我这双手也觉得有点不对劲，”伊米说，“像叫风皴了似的。”

“什么风皴了，”吉奥说，“这是疼。”

二十分钟后，伊米说：“好了，差不多该到了。”

“喂，”厄尔森说，“蛇在那儿。”这时他们跑上前去，那少年从护栏上一跃而下，抓住他们的肩膀，咧嘴直笑。接着他就开始拽着他们往前走。

===Original===
“You lucky little so-and-so,” said Urson. “I wish you’d been with us.”
“He probably was, in spirit, if not in body,” Geo laughed.
Snake nodded.
“What are you pulling for?” Urson asked. “Say, if you’re going to get headaches like that, you’d better teach us what to do with them beads there.” He pointed to the jewel at Iimmi’s and Geo’s necks.

===Chinese===
“你这个走运的小东西，”厄尔森说，“真希望你刚才跟咱们在一块儿。”

“精神上嘛，他八成一直跟着，缺的只是身子。”吉奥笑道。

蛇点头。

“你拽什么呀？”厄尔森问，“喂，你要是打算老是那么头疼，最好教教咱们怎么摆弄那些珠子。”他指了指伊米和吉奥脖子上的珍宝。

===Original===
Snake nodded and tugged forward again.
“He wants us to hurry,” Geo said. “We better get going.”
The road finally tore completely away, and four feet below them, over the twisted rail, was the mouth of a street that led into the waterfront. Snake, Iimmi and then Urson vaulted over. Urson shook his hands painfully when he landed.
“Give me a hand, will you?” Geo asked. “My arm is really shot.” Urson helped his friend over.

===Chinese===
蛇点点头，又往前拽。

“他要咱们快走，”吉奥说，“咱们最好赶紧。”

路面最后彻底断了，隔着拧变形的护栏，往下四英尺，是一条通向滨水区的街口。蛇先翻了过去，然后是伊米，然后是厄尔森。厄尔森落地时疼得直甩手。

“拉我一把，行吗？”吉奥问，“我这胳膊真是不中用了。”厄尔森把朋友扶了过去。

===Original===
Almost as though it had been in wait, thick liquid gurgling sounded behind them. Like a wounded thing it emerged from behind the broken highway, bulging up into the light which shone on the ripples in its shriveled membrane.
“Run it!” bawled Urson, and they took off down the street. In the moonlight, the ruined piers spread along the waterfront to either side of them, some even slanting into the silvered water.
Turning once, they saw it bloat the entrance of the street, fill it, and then pour across the broken stones, slipping across the rubble of the smashed wharf.

===Chinese===
几乎像是早就埋伏着专等他们似的，身后响起了浓稠液体的咕噜声。它像头受伤的野兽，从断裂的公路后面涌出来，鼓进灯光里，灯光正照在它皱缩的膜体上的涟漪。

“快跑！”厄尔森吼道，他们顺着街一溜烟奔了下去。月光下，残破的码头沿着滨水区向他们两侧铺开，有几座甚至斜插进泛着银光的水里。

他们回头一瞥，只见它把街口堵得胀了起来，填满，随即漫过碎裂的石头，淌过砸烂的码头的瓦砾。

===Original===
When Geo hit water, he was aware of two things immediately as the hands reached for his body. First, the thong was yanked from around his neck. Second, pain seared his arm as if the bones and ligaments were suddenly replaced by white-hot cords of steel, and every vein and capillary had become part of a webbing of red fire.
It was a long time before consciousness. Once he was lifted. And when he opened his eyes, the white moon was moving incredibly fast above him toward the dark shapes of leaves. Was he being carried? And his arm hurt. There was more drowsy half consciousness, and once a great deal of pain. When he opened his mouth to scream, however, darkness flowed in, swathed his tongue, and he swallowed the darkness down into his body and into his head, and called it sleep﻿—
---

===Chinese===
吉奥一头扎进水里，当那些手伸向他的身体时，他立刻觉察到两件事。第一，皮绳从他脖子上被猛地扯掉了。第二，剧痛灼过他的手臂，仿佛骨头和韧带刹那间换成了白热的钢索，每一条静脉和微血管都成了一张红火的网的一部分。

很久以后他才恢复知觉。有一回他被抬了起来。他睁开眼睛时，白色的月亮正在他头顶，以难以置信的速度朝着一团团暗色的树叶移动。他是被人抱着吗？而且他胳膊疼。又是更多昏昏沉沉的半知觉，还有一回是剧痛。可当他张开嘴想喊时，黑暗涌了进来，裹住他的舌头，他把黑暗咽进身体、咽进脑袋，管它叫作睡——

---

===Original===
*A spool of copper wire unrolled over the black tile floor. Scoop it up quick. Damn, let me get out of here. I run past the black columns, glimpsing the cavernous room, and the black statue at the other end, huge, and rising into shadows. Men in dark robes are walking around. (Not only could they see, this time; they could hear the thinking.) Just don’t feel up to praying this afternoon. I am before the door, and above it, a black disk with three white eyes on it. Through the door, up black stone steps. Wonder if anyone will be up there now. Just my luck I’ll find the Old Man himself. Another door with a black circle above it. Push it open slowly, cool on my hands. A man is standing inside, looking into a large screen of glass. Figures moving on it. Can’t make them out, he’s in the way. Oh, there’s another one.*
*“I don’t know whether to call it success or failure,” one says.*
*“The jewels are﻿ ﻿… safe or lost?”*
*“What do you call it?” the first one asks. “I don’t know any more.” He sighs. “I don’t think I’ve taken my eyes off this thing for more than two hours since they got to the beach. Every mile they’ve come closer has made my blood run colder.”*

===Chinese===
*一卷铜线在黑色瓷砖地上散开。赶紧捡起来。该死，我得离开这儿。我跑过一根根黑柱子，瞥见那洞穴般的大殿，还有殿尽头那尊黑色雕像，巨大，耸入暗影。穿深色袍子的男人们走来走去。（这一回他们不但看得见，还听得见思维。）今天下午我就是不想祷告。我到了门前，门上方是一面黑盘，上面有三只白眼睛。穿过门，走上黑石阶。不知这会儿上头有没有人。可别倒了个大霉，撞见老爷子（the Old Man）本人。又一扇门，门上方一个黑圈。慢慢推开，门上凉凉的，手上也是。一个男人站在里面，正看着一大面玻璃屏。屏上有影子在动。看不清，他挡着呢。哦，那儿还有一个人。*

*“我说不上这该算成功还是失败，”一个说。*

*“珍宝是……保住了，还是丢了？”*

*“你说该叫哪个？”头一个问，“我自己也说不准了。”他叹了口气。“打他们上沙滩起，我就没让眼睛离开这玩意儿超过两个钟头。他们每走近一英里，我的血就凉一分。”*

===Original===
*“What do we report to Hama Incarnate?”*
*“It would be silly to say anything now. We just don’t know.”*
*“Well,” says the other, “at least we can do something with the City of New Hope since they got rid of that super-amoeba.”*
*“Are you sure they really got it?”*

===Chinese===
*“我们向化身哈马（Hama Incarnate）怎么禀报？”*

*“现在说什么都荒唐。我们就是不知道。”*

*“这个嘛，”另一个说，“既然他们除掉了那条超级变形虫（super-amoeba），至少新望城那边，咱们总算能有所作为了。”*

*“你肯定他们真除掉了？”*

===Original===
*“After the burning it received over that naked atom pile? It was all it could do to get to the waterfront. It’s just about fried up and blown away already.”*
*“And how safe would you call them?” the other asks.*
*“Right now? I wouldn’t call them anything.”*
*Something glitters on the table by the door. Yes, there it is. In the pile of strange equipment is a U-shaped scrap of metal. Just what I need. Hot damn, adhesive tape too. Quick, there, before they see. Fine. Now, let the door close, real slow. Ooops. It clicked. Now come on, look innocent, in case they come out. I hope the Old Man isn’t watching. Guess they’re not coming. And down the stairs again, the black stone walls moving past. Out another door, into the garden, dark flowers, purple, deep red, some with blue in them, and big stone urns. Some priests are coming down the path. Ooops again, there’s old Dunderhead. He’ll want me inside praying. Duck down behind that urn. Here we go. What’ll I do if he catches me? Really sir, I have nothing under my choir robe. Peek out.*

===Chinese===
*“挨了那座裸原子堆上头那一通烧之后？它拼了老命才挪到滨水区。这会儿差不多已经烤干吹散了。”*

*“那你说他们有多安全？”另一个问。*

*“眼下？我不会拿任何字眼去形容他们。”*

*门边桌上有什么东西闪了一下。对了，就在那儿。一堆稀奇古怪的器械里，有一块马蹄形的金属片。正合我用。太好了，还有胶布。快，趁他们没看见。好了。现在让门关上，慢慢的。哎呀，咔哒了一声。来吧，装出没事的样子，万一他们出来呢。但愿老爷子没在瞧。看来他们不出来了。再下楼去，黑石的墙一面面从身旁过去。出了另一扇门，进了花园，深色的花，紫的，深红的，有几朵带蓝，还有大石瓮。几个祭司正沿小径走来。又糟了，老呆子来了。他准要叫我进屋祷告。躲到那口石瓮后面。来了来了。要是他抓住我，我可怎么办？真的老爷，我唱诗袍底下什么都没有。探头瞧瞧。*

===Original===
*Very, very small sigh of relief, now. Can’t afford to be too loud around here. They’re gone. Let’s examine the loot. The black stone urn has one handle above. It’s about eight feet tall. One, two, three: jump, and﻿ ﻿… hold﻿ ﻿… on﻿ ﻿… and﻿ ﻿… pull. And try to get to the top. There we go. Cold stone between my toes. And over the edge, where it’s filled with dirt. Pant. Pant. Pant.*
*Should be just over here, if I remember right. Dig, dig, dig. Damp earth feels good in your hands. Ow! my finger. There it is. A brown paper bag under granules of black earth. Lift it out. Is it all there? Open it up, peer in.
