Down at the bottom, beyond the folds of the edges where the top had been twisted tightly together, are the tiny scraps of copper, a few long pieces of dark metal, a piece of board, some brads. To this my grubby little hand adds the spool of copper wire and the U-shaped scrap of metal. Now, slip it into my robe and﻿—once you get up here, how the hell do you get down? I always forget. Turn around, climb over the edge, like this, and let yourself down. Damn, my robe’s caught on the handle.*
*And drop.*
*Skinned my shin again. Some day I’ll learn.*

===Chinese===
*好啦，轻轻的、轻轻的松一口气。在这种地方可不敢出大声。他们走了。来验验赃物。黑石瓮上头有一只提耳。约莫八英尺高。一、二、三：跳——抓——住——再——拉。想法子上到顶。上去喽。脚趾间是凉凉的石头。翻过瓮沿，里头盛着土。喘。喘。喘。*

*要是没记错，就该在这一带。挖，挖，挖。湿土攥在手里真舒服。哎哟！手指头。在这儿呢。一只棕色纸袋，埋在黑土粒下面。提出来。东西都在吗？打开，往里瞅。袋底上，在拧得紧紧的袋口褶边底下，是那些碎铜丝，几根长长的深色金属，一块小木板，几枚小钉。我这只脏兮兮的小手又把那卷铜线和那块马蹄形金属片添了进去。现在，把它塞进袍子——可一旦爬上来了，到底怎么下去呢？我老忘。转个身，翻过瓮沿，像这样，慢慢出溜下去。该死，袍子挂提耳上了。*

*撒手，落下去。*

*又蹭破小腿了。哪天我总能学会。*

===Original===
*Now let’s see if we can figure this thing out. Gotta crouch down and get to work. Here we go. Open the bag, and turn the contents out in the lap of the dark-colored robe, grubby hands poking.*
*The U-shaped metal, the copper wire, fine. Hold the end of the wire to the metal, and maneuver the spool around the end of the wire to the metal, and maneuver the spool around the end of the rod. Around. And around. And around. Here we go round the mulberry bush, the mulberry bush, the mulberry bush. Here we go round the mulberry bush; I’ll have me a coil by the morning.*
*Suddenly a harsh voice in the distance: “And what do you think you’re doing?”*
*Dunderhead rides again. “Nothing, sir,” as metal and scraps and wires fly frantically into the paper bag.*

===Chinese===
*现在来瞧瞧能不能把这玩意儿弄明白。得蹲下来干活。动手。打开纸袋，把里面的东西倒进深色袍子的袍襟，脏兮兮的小手扒拉着。*

*马蹄形金属片，铜线，齐了。把线头抵住铁片，把线轴绕着线头转，绕着铁片转，绕着铁杆转。绕。再绕。再绕。我们绕着桑树丛呀，桑树丛呀，桑树丛呀。我们绕着桑树丛呀，天一亮我就有只线圈。*

*远处忽然一个严厉的声音：“你这是在干什么？”*

*呆子又来了。“没干什么，老爷。”金属片、碎件和铜线手忙脚乱地飞进纸袋。*

===Original===
*The voice: “All novices under twenty must report to afternoon services without fail!”*
*“Yes, sir. Coming right along, sir.” Paper bag jammed equally frantically into the folds of my robe. Not a moment’s peace. Not a moment’s! Through the garden with lowered eyes, past a dour-looking priest with a small paunch. There are mirrors along the vestibule, huge slabs of glass that rise thirty feet, reflecting the blue and yellow light back and forth from the colored windows of the temple. In the mirror I see pass: a dour-looking priest, proceeded by a smaller figure with short red hair and a spray of freckles over a flattish nose. And as we pass into prayer, there is the maddening, almost inaudible jingling of metal scraps, muffled by the dark robe.*
---
Geo woke up, and almost everything was white.

===Chinese===
*那声音：“所有二十岁以下的见习修士，一律不得缺席午后礼拜！”*

*“是，老爷。这就来，老爷。”纸袋又同样手忙脚乱地塞进我袍子的褶子里。一刻也不得安生。一刻也不得！垂着眼睛穿过花园，走过一位面相阴沉、带着小肚腩的祭司。门廊两侧立着一面面镜子，一整块一整块的玻璃高达三十英尺，把神庙彩窗的蓝光黄光来回映照。镜子里，我看见走过去的是：一位面相阴沉的祭司，前面走着一个矮小些的身影，一头红色短发，塌鼻梁上撒着一层雀斑。而当我们走进祷告时，响起那折磨人的、几乎听不见的金属碎件叮当声，被深色的袍子闷住了。*

---

吉奥醒了过来，周围几乎是一片白。

## VIII / 八、白寺夜遁

===Original===
The pale woman with the tiny eyes rose from over him. Her hair dropped like white silk threads over her shoulders. “You are awake?” she asked. “Do you understand me?”
“Am I at﻿—at Hama’s temple?” he asked, the remnants of the dream still blowing in at the edges of his mind, like shredding cloth. “My friends, where are they?”
The woman laughed. “Your friends are all right. You came out the worst.” Another laugh. “You ask if this is Hama’s temple? But you can see, can you not? You have eyes. Don’t you recognize the color of the White Goddess Argo?”
Geo looked around the room. It was white marble, and there was no direct source of light. The walls simply glowed.

===Chinese===
那个眼睛细小的苍白女人从他身上方直起身来。她的头发像一绺绺白丝线，披落在肩头。“你醒了？”她问，“听得懂我的话吗？”

“我这是在——在哈马的神庙里？”他问，梦的残片还像撕碎的布一样，从他意识的边缘阵阵灌进来。“我的伙伴们呢？他们在哪儿？”

女人笑了。“你的伙伴们没事。就数你伤得最重。”又是一笑。“你问这儿是不是哈马的神庙？可你自己看得见，不是吗？你有眼睛。白女神阿戈的颜色，你认不出来？”

吉奥环顾房间。这里是白色大理石，没有任何直接的光源。四壁自己在发光。

===Original===
“My friends.﻿ ﻿…” Geo said again.
“They are fine. We were able to completely restore their flesh to health. They must have exposed their hands to the direct beam of the radiation for only a few seconds. But the whole first half of your arm had apparently lain in the deadly rays for some minutes. You were not as lucky as they.”
Another thought rushed Geo’s mind now. “The jewels﻿ ﻿…” he started to say, but instead of sounding the words, he reached to his throat with both hands. One fell on his naked chest. And there was something very wrong with the other. He sat up in the bed quickly, and looked down. “My arm,” he said.

===Chinese===
“我的伙伴们……”吉奥又说了一遍。

“他们没事。他们的肉身，我们已经能够完全复原。想来他们的手只在那种射线的正底下照了几秒钟。可你那条胳膊的前半截，显然在致命的射线里躺了好几分钟。你不如他们走运。”

这时另一个念头猛地冲进吉奥的脑海。“珍宝……”他刚要开口，话没出声，两只手已经摸向喉咙。一只落在赤裸的胸口上。另一只却大大不对劲。他猛地在床上坐起来，低头看去。“我的胳膊。”他说。

===Original===
Swathed in white bandages, the limb ended some foot and a half short of where it should have.
“My arm﻿ ﻿… ?” he asked again, with a child’s bewilderment. “What happened to my arm?”
“I tried to tell you,” the woman said, softly. “We had to amputate half of your arm. If we had not, you would have died.”
“My arm,” Geo said again, and lay back in the bed.

===Chinese===
那条肢体裹着白色绷带，在离本该终止之处还差一英尺半的地方，就到了头。

“我的胳膊……？”他又问，带着孩子似的茫然。“我的胳膊怎么了？”

“我本想告诉你的。”女人轻声说，“我们不得不截掉你半条胳膊。不截，你就没命了。”

“我的胳膊。”吉奥又说了一遍，躺回床上。

===Original===
“It is difficult,” the woman said. “It is only a little consolation, I know, but we are blind here. What burned your arm away, took our sight from us when it was much stronger, generations ago. We learned how to battle many of its effects, and had we not rescued you from the river, all of you would have died. You are men who know the religion of Argo, and adhere to it. This another of your party has told us. Be thankful then that you have come under the wing of the Mother Goddess again, for this is a hostile country.” She paused. “Do you wish to talk?”
Geo shook his head.
“I hear the sheets rustle,” the woman said, smiling, “which means you either shook or nodded your head. I know from my study of the old customs that one means ‘yes’ and the other ‘no.’ But you must have patience with us who cannot see. We are not used to your people. Do you wish to talk?” she repeated.
“Oh,” said Geo. “No. No, I don’t.”

===Chinese===
“这滋味不好受。”女人说，“我知道这话没多大安慰，可我们这儿的人都是瞎子。烧掉你胳膊的那东西，在好几代人之前、还强得多的时候，夺走了我们的视力。我们学会了如何对抗它的许多恶效；若不是我们把你们从河里救起，你们四个都得死。你们是知晓阿戈教义、并且信奉不渝的人——这是你们中另一位告诉我们的。那么就感谢吧，感谢你们重又回到母神的羽翼之下，因为这是个凶险之地。”她顿了顿。“你想聊聊吗？”

吉奥摇了摇头。

“我听见床单窸窣响，”女人微笑着说，“这说明你要么摇了头，要么点了头。我从对古老习俗的研究里得知，一个意思是‘是’，另一个意思是‘否’。但你们得对我们这些看不见的人有点耐心。我们还不惯于同你们这样的人打交道。你想聊聊吗？”她又问了一遍。

“哦，”吉奥说，“不想。不，我不想。”

===Original===
“Very well,” the woman said. She rose, still smiling. “I will return later.” She walked to a wall in which a door slipped open, and then it closed again, behind her.
He lay still on the bed for a long time. Then he turned over on his stomach. Once he brought the stump under his chest and held the clean bandages in his other hand. Very quickly he let go, and stretched the limb sideways, as far as possible away from him. That didn’t work either, so he moved it back down to his side, and let it lay by him under the white sheet.
After a long while, he got up, sat on the edge of the bed, and looked around the room. It was completely bare, with neither windows nor visible doors. He went to the spot through which she had exited, but could find not seam or crack. His tunic, he saw, had been washed, pressed, and laid on the foot of the bed. He slipped it over his head, fumbling with only one arm. Getting the belt together started out to be a problem, but he hooked the buckle around one finger and maneuvered the strap through with the other. He adjusted his leather purse, now empty, on his side. Then he saw that the sword was gone.

===Chinese===
“那好吧。”女人说。她站起身，仍旧微笑着。“我晚些再来。”她走向一面墙，墙里有一扇门悄然滑开，又在她身后合拢。

他一动不动地在床上躺了很久，然后翻了个身，趴下。有一次，他把残臂收到胸下，用另一只手攥住那干净的绷带。可他立刻就松开了，把残肢朝旁边伸直，尽可能离自己远远的。那也不管用，于是他又把它放回身侧，让它贴着自己，卧在白床单下。

好半天之后，他起身坐在床沿，环顾房间。屋里空空如也，没有窗，也看不见门。他走到她出去的那个地方，却找不到一丝接缝或裂缝。他看见自己的上衣已经洗净熨平，叠放在床脚。他用单臂笨拙地把它套过头顶。系腰带起初是个难题，但他把带扣勾在一根手指上，用另一只手把皮带穿了过去。他把如今空空如也的皮钱袋在腰侧摆正。然后他发现，剑不见了。

===Original===
An unreal feeling, white like the walls of the room, was beginning to fill him up like a pale mixture of milk and water. He walked around the edge of the room once more, looking for some break.
There was a sound behind him and the tiny-eyed woman in her white robe stood in a triangular doorway. “You’re dressed,” she smiled. “Good. Are you too tired to come with me? You will eat and see your friends if you feel well enough. Or, I can have the food brought.”
“I’ll come,” Geo said.

===Chinese===
一种虚幻之感，白得如同房间的四壁，开始把他注满，像一杯掺水的淡乳。他沿着房间的边缘又走了一圈，想找出一处豁口。

身后传来声响，那个小眼睛的女人身穿白袍，站在一个三角形的门口。“你穿好衣服了。”她微笑道，“很好。累不累，还跟我来得了吗？要是撑得住，你去吃点东西，见见你的伙伴。要不，我叫人把饭送来。”

“我去。”吉奥说。

===Original===
She turned, and he followed her into a hall of the same luminous substance. Her heels touched the back of her white robe with each step, but she was silent. His own bare feet on the cool stones seemed louder than those of the blind woman before him. Suddenly he was in a larger room, with benches. It was a chapel, obviously of Argo because of the altar at the far end, but its detail was strange. Everything was arranged with the white simplicity that one would expect of a people to whom visual adornment meant nothing. He sat down on a bench as the woman said, “Wait here.” She disappeared down another hall.
Suddenly the woman returned from the other hallway, followed by Snake. Geo and the four-armed boy looked at each other, silently, as the woman disappeared again. A wish, like a living thing, suddenly writhed into a knot in Geo’s stomach, that the boy would say something. He himself could not.

===Chinese===
她转过身，他跟着她走进一条同样发光的材质砌成的走廊。她每迈一步，脚跟都碰一下白袍的后襟，人却悄然无声。他自己的赤脚踩在凉石头上，声音似乎比前面那个盲女人的脚步还响。忽然间他进了一个大些的房间，里面有长凳。这是一座礼拜堂，远远那端设有祭坛，显然是阿戈的，但细部却透着古怪。一切都按白色的简朴布置——对一个视觉装点毫无意义的民族，你所能指望的正是这种简朴。他在一条长凳上坐下，女人说：“在这儿等等。”她顺着另一条走廊消失了。

女人忽然从另一条走廊回来，身后跟着蛇。吉奥和那个四臂少年默默对视，女人又不见了。一个愿望，像活物一般，猛地在吉奥的胃里绞成一个结：但愿这少年开个口。他自己开不了口。

===Original===
Again she returned, this time with Urson. The big man stepped into the chapel, saw Geo, and exclaimed, “Friend, what happened?” He came to him quickly and placed his warm hands on Geo’s shoulders. “What﻿ ﻿…” he began, and shook his head.
Geo grinned suddenly, and patted his stump with his good hand. “I guess jelly-belly got something from me after all.”
Urson held his own forearm next to Geo’s and compared them. There was paleness in both. “I guess none of us got out completely all right. I woke up once while they were taking the scabs off. It was pretty bad, and I went to sleep again fast.”

===Chinese===
她又一次回来了，这回带着厄尔森。大汉跨进礼拜堂，一眼看见吉奥，喊道：“伙计，你怎么了？”他快步走到他跟前，把温热的手按在吉奥肩上。“怎么……”他起了个头，又摇了摇头。

吉奥忽然咧嘴一笑，用好手拍了拍自己的残臂。“看来那果冻肚（jelly-belly）到底还是从我这儿捞走了一样东西。”

厄尔森把自己的前臂凑到吉奥的旁边比了比。两条都透着苍白。“看来咱们谁也没囫囵着出来。他们给我揭痂的时候我醒过一回。够呛，我赶紧又睡着了。”

===Original===
Iimmi came in now. “Well, I was wondering﻿ ﻿…” He stopped, and let out a low whistle. “I guess it really got you, brother.” His own arms looked as though they had been dipped in bleach up to the mid forearms.
“How did this happen?” Urson asked.
“When we were back doing our tightrope act on those damn girders,” explained Iimmi, “our bodies were in the shadow of the girders and the rays only got to our arms. I’ve got something you’ll be interested in too, Geo.”

===Chinese===
伊米这时进来了。“呃，我正纳闷呢……”他顿住，低低打了个呼哨。“看来它真把你逮着了，兄弟。”他自己的两条胳膊，看上去像在前臂中段以下浸过了漂白水。

“这是怎么弄的？”厄尔森问。

“咱们回去在那些破大梁上走钢丝的时候，”伊米解释道，“咱们的身子在大梁的影子里，射线只够到胳膊。对了，我还有样东西你准感兴趣，吉奥。”

===Original===
“Just tell me where the hell we are,” Urson said.
“We’re in a monastery sacred to Argo,” Iimmi told him. “It’s across the river from the City of New Hope, which is where we were.”
“That name sounds familiar; in the﻿ ﻿…” began Urson. Snake gave him a quick glance, and he stopped, and then frowned.

===Chinese===
“只消告诉我，咱们这到底是在什么鬼地方。”厄尔森说。

“咱们在一座供奉阿戈的修道院里。”伊米告诉他，“就在新望城的河对岸——咱们先前待的就是新望城。”

“这名字听着耳熟；在那……”厄尔森刚开口，蛇飞快地瞟了他一眼，他住了嘴，随即皱起眉。
===Original===
“We knew of your presence in the City of New Hope,” explained the blind Priestess, “and we found you by the riverside after you swam across. You managed to cling to life long enough for us to get you back to the monastery and apply what art we could to sooth the burns from the deadly fire.”
Geo suddenly saw that there was no jewel around Iimmi’s neck either. He could almost feel the hands ripping it from his neck in the water. Iimmi must have made the same discovery, because his pale hand raised to his own chest.
The Priestess beckoned and started down another hall, and again they followed. They arrived at an even larger room, this one set with white marble benches and long white tables. “This is the main dining room of the monastery,” their guide explained. “One table has been set up for you. You will not eat with the other priestesses, of course.”

===Chinese===
“你们在新望城的时候，我们就知道你们在了。”盲眼女祭司解释道，“你们游过河后，我们在河边找到了你们。你们总算吊住了那口气，撑到我们把你们运回修道院，用我们力所能及的医术，敷治那致命之火烙下的灼伤。”

吉奥忽然看见，伊米的脖子上也没有珍宝。他几乎能感觉到水中那双手把它从他脖子上扯下去的情形。伊米想必也发现了同样的事，因为他那只苍白的手抬向了自己的胸口。

女祭司招了招手，沿另一条走廊走下去，他们又跟了上去。他们来到一间更大的屋子，摆着白色大理石长凳和长长的白桌。“这是修道院的主餐厅。”引路人解释道，“给你们单独摆了一张桌子。你们当然不会同其他女祭司共餐。”

===Original===
“Why not?” asked Iimmi.
Surprise flowed across the blind face. “You are men,” she told them, matter of factly. Then she led them to a table where wine, meat, and bowls piled with strange fruit were placed. As they sat down, she disappeared once more.
Geo reached for a knife. For a moment there was silence at the table as the nub of the arm jutted over food. “I guess I just have to learn,” he said after the pause.

===Chinese===
“为什么不能？”伊米问。

惊讶掠过那张失明的脸。“你们是男人。”她就事论事地告诉他们。随后她把他们领到一张桌前，桌上摆着酒、肉，还有几碗堆成尖的稀奇果子。他们在桌边坐下，她又不见了。

吉奥伸手去取刀。残臂的断头杵在饭菜上方，桌上静了一瞬。“看来我只得从头学了。”静默过后他说。

===Original===
Halfway through the meal, Urson said, “What about the jewels? Did the Priestess take them from you?”
“They came off in the water,” said Iimmi.
Geo nodded corroboration.

===Chinese===
饭吃到一半，厄尔森说：“珍宝呢？是女祭司从你们身上拿走的吗？”

“在水里就脱落了。”伊米说。

吉奥点头作证。

===Original===
“Well, now we really have a problem,” said Urson. “Here we are, at a temple of Argo’s where we could return the jewels and maybe even get back to the Priestess on the ship, and out of the silly mess, and the jewels are gone.”
“I guess that also means our river friends are working for Hama,” said Geo.
“Well,” Iimmi said, “Hama’s got his jewel then, and we’re out of the way. Perhaps he delivered us into Argo’s hands as a reward for bringing them this far?”

===Chinese===
“唉，这下咱们可真有麻烦了。”厄尔森说，“你瞧，咱们正待在阿戈的神庙里，本来可以把珍宝归还，说不定还能回到船上那位女祭司身边，从这件荒唐差事里脱身——可珍宝没了。”

“我猜，这也意味着咱们那些河里的朋友是替哈马办事的。”吉奥说。

“这么说，”伊米说，“哈马拿到他的珍宝了，咱们也被人顺势除掉了。说不定他把咱们送进阿戈手里，是奖赏咱们把他们一路送到这儿？”

===Original===
“Since we would have died anyway,” said Geo, “I guess he was doing us a favor.”
“And you know what that means,” Iimmi said, looking at Snake now.
“Huh?” asked Urson. Then he said, “Oh, let the boy speak for himself. All right, Four Arms, are you or are you not a spy for Hama?”

===Chinese===
“反正咱们横竖都是死，”吉奥说，“我猜他这还算帮了咱们一个忙。”

“那你知道这意味着什么。”伊米说着，此刻望向蛇。

“啊？”厄尔森问。随即他说：“哦，让这孩子自己说。好吧，四臂的，你到底是不是哈马的奸细？”

===Original===
A pained expression came over Snake’s face, and he shook his head not in denial but bewilderment. Suddenly he got up from the table, and ran from the room. Urson looked at the others. “Now don’t tell me I hurt his feelings by asking.”
“You didn’t,” said Iimmi, “but I may have. I keep on forgetting that he can read minds.”
“What do you mean?” Urson asked.

===Chinese===
蛇脸上掠过一道痛楚，他摇了摇头——不是否认，而是发懵。忽然他从桌边站起来，跑出了房间。厄尔森看看其他人。“别告诉我，我这一问伤了他的心。”

“你没伤着，”伊米说，“我倒可能伤着了。我老是忘了他会读心。”

“你什么意思？”厄尔森问。

===Original===
“Just when you asked him that, a lot of things came together in my mind that would be pretty vicious for him if any of it were true.”
“Huh?” asked Urson.
“I think I know what you mean,” said Geo.

===Chinese===
“就在你问他那句话的时候，我脑子里一下串起了好些事——只要里头有一件是真的，对他可就够狠的了。”

“啊？”厄尔森问。

“我想我明白你的意思。”吉奥说。

===Original===
“I still﻿—”
“It means that he is a spy,” explained Iimmi, “and among other things, he was probably lying about the radio back at the city. And that cost Geo his arm.”
“Why the﻿—” began Urson, and then looked down the hall where Snake had disappeared.

===Chinese===
“我还是不——”

“这就是说，他是个奸细，”伊米解释道，“而且除此之外，他在城里那会儿关于无线电的事多半也在撒谎。那可让吉奥赔上了一条胳膊。”

“怎么这——”厄尔森刚起头，随即望向蛇消失的那条走廊。

===Original===
They didn’t eat much more. When they got up, Urson felt sleepy and was shown back to his room.
“May I show my friend what you showed me?” Iimmi asked the Priestess when she returned. “He is also a student of rituals.”
“Of course you may,” smiled the Priestess.

===Chinese===
他们没再吃多少。起身时，厄尔森困了，被领回了他的房间。

“您给我看的那些，我可以让我朋友也看看吗？”女祭司回来时，伊米问道，“他也是研习仪式的学生。”

“当然可以。”女祭司微笑道。

===Original===
A door opened and they entered another room similar to the one in which Geo had awakened. As she was about to leave, Iimmi asked, “Wait. Can you tell us how to leave the room ourselves?”
“Why would you want to leave?” she asked.

===Chinese===
一扇门开了，他们走进另一个房间，同吉奥醒来的那间差不多。她正要离开，伊米问：“等等。能告诉我们，怎么自己走出这个房间吗？”

“你们为什么要出去？”她问。
===Original===
“For exercise,” offered Geo, “and to observe the working of the monastery. Believe us, we are true students of Argo’s religion.”
“Simply press the wall with your hand, level at your waist, and the door will open. But you must not wander about the monastery. Rites which are not for your eyes are being carried out. Not for your eyes,” she repeated. “Strange, this is a phrase that has never left our language. Suddenly, confronted by people who can see, it makes me feel somehow﻿ ﻿…” she paused. “Well, that is how to leave the room.”

===Chinese===
“活动活动筋骨，”吉奥接口道，“也见识见识修道院的运作。相信我们，我们是阿戈教门真正的学子。”

“只消用手按墙，按在与腰齐平的地方，门就会开。但你们不许在修道院里乱逛。有些仪式正在进行，不是给你们眼睛看的。不是给你们眼睛看的，”她又重复了一遍，“说来奇怪，这句话从没离开过我们的语言。忽然之间，面对着看得见的人，它让我觉得自己不知怎么……”她顿了顿。“好了，出房间就是这么出。”

===Original===
She stepped out, and the door closed behind her.
“Here,” said Iimmi, “this is what I wanted to show you.” On his bed were a pile of books, old, but legible. Geo flipped through a few pages. Suddenly he looked up at Iimmi.
“Hey, what are they doing with *printed* books?”

===Chinese===
她跨出门去，门在她身后合上了。

“瞧，”伊米说，“这就是我要给你看的东西。”他床上有一堆书，老旧，字迹却清楚可读。吉奥翻了几页，忽然抬头看向伊米。

“嘿，他们这儿怎么会有*印刷*的书？”

===Original===
“Question number one,” said Iimmi. “Now, for question number two. Look here.” He reached over Geo’s shoulder and hastened him to one page.
“Why it’s the﻿ ﻿…” began Geo.
“You’re darn right it is,” said Iimmi.

===Chinese===
“这是问题一。”伊米说，“接下来是问题二。看这儿。”他探过吉奥的肩，急急把书翻到某一页。

“咦，这不就是……”吉奥开口。

“你算说对了，就是它。”伊米说。

===Original===
> Forked in the eye of the bright ash
 

 there the heart of Argo broke
 

 and the hand of the goddess would dash
 

 through the head of flame, and the smoke.
> Burn the grain speck in the hand
 

 and batter the stars with singing.
 

 Hail the height of a man,
 

 and also the height of a woman.
> The eyes have imprisoned a vision,
 

 the ash-tree dribbles with blood.
 

 Thrust from the gates of the prison,
 

 smear the yew-tree with mud.

===Chinese===
> 在明亮白蜡树的眼中分杈，
 

 阿戈的心便在那里碎裂，
 

 女神的手挥斩而下，
 

 穿过火焰的头颅，和烟。
> 把手中的谷粒烧掉，
 

 用歌声捶打星辰。
 

 欢呼男人的身量，
 

 也欢呼女人的身量。
> 众眼囚住了一个幻象，
 

 白蜡树滴淌着血。
 

 把它逐出牢狱之门，
 

 拿泥涂抹紫杉。

===Original===
“That must be the full version of the poem I found the missing stanza to back in the library at Leptar.”
“As I was saying,” said Iimmi, “question number two: what is the relation between the rituals of Hama and the old rituals of Argo. Apparently this particular branch of the religion of the Goddess underwent no purge. And no one at Olcse Olwnh was supposed to know about them.”

===Chinese===
“这准是那首诗的全本——我在莱普塔的图书馆里，发现过它缺失的那一节。”

“我正要说呢，”伊米说，“问题二：哈马的仪式和阿戈的古老仪式，究竟是什么关系。显然，女神教门的这一支未曾经历清洗。而在奥尔克塞·奥尔温，本不该有人知道它们。”

===Original===
“I wonder why?” Geo asked.
“That is question number three.”
“How did you get a hold of them?”
“Well,” said Iimmi, “I sort of suspected they might be here. So I just asked for them. And I think I’ve got some answers to those questions.”

===Chinese===
“为什么呢？”吉奥问。

“那就是问题三了。”

“你是怎么弄到手的？”

“这个嘛，”伊米说，“我隐隐猜到它们可能就在这儿。于是我张口就要了来。而且我想，这几个问题我已经有了几分答案。”

===Original===
“Fine. Go ahead.”
“We’ll start from three, go back to one, and then on to two. Nice and orderly,” said Iimmi. “Why wasn’t anybody supposed to know about the rituals? Simply because they were so similar to the rituals of Hama. You remember some of the others we found in the abandoned temple? If you don’t, you can refresh your memory right here. The two sets of rituals run almost parallel, except for a name changed here, a color switched from black to white, a switch in the vegetative symbolism. I guess what happened was that when Hama’s forces invaded Leptar five hundred years ago, it didn’t take Leptar long to find out the similarity. From the looks of the City of New Hope, I think it’s safe to assume that at one time or another, say five hundred years ago, Aptor’s civilization was far higher than Leptar’s, and probably wouldn’t have had too hard a time beating her in an invasion. So when Leptar captured the first jewel, and somehow did manage to repel Aptor, the priests of Leptar assumed that the safest way to avoid infiltration by Hama and Aptor again would be to make the rituals of the two as different as possible from the ones of their enemy, Hama.

===Chinese===
“好。说吧。”

“咱们从三讲起，倒回一，再进到二。规规矩矩，有条有理。”伊米说，“为什么本不该有人知道那些仪式？就因为它们同哈马的仪式太像了。还记得咱们在那座废弃神庙里见过的其他几篇吗？不记得的话，就着这儿正好温习温习。两套仪式几乎并行，只差这儿改个名字、颜色由黑换白、植物象征换个花样。我猜事情是这样的：五百年前哈马的军队入侵莱普塔，莱普塔没多久就看出了这层相似。照新望城的样子看，我想不妨这样断定：在某个时期，比如说五百年前吧，阿普托的文明远高于莱普塔，真要入侵，打败她多半不费什么劲。于是，当莱普塔夺到第一颗珍宝、好歹击退了阿普托之后，莱普塔的祭司们便认定：要想不再被哈马和阿普托渗透，最稳妥的法子，就是把自家的两套仪式改得同敌人哈马的那套尽可能不同。”

===Original===
“The ghouls, the bats, they parallel the stories I’ve heard other sailors tell too closely to be accidents. How many people do you think have been shipwrecked on Aptor and gotten far enough into the place to see what we’ve seen, and then gotten off again to tell about it?”
“I can think of two,” said Geo.

===Chinese===
“食尸鬼，蝙蝠——它们同我听别的水手讲过的故事对得太齐了，齐得不可能是巧合。你想，多少人在阿普托失事搁浅，又走得够深，见到了咱们见到的这些，然后还能活着脱身、回去讲给人听？”

“我能想出两个。”吉奥说。

===Original===
“Huh?” said Iimmi.
“Snake and Jordde,” answered Geo. “Remember that Argo said there had been spies from Aptor before. And Jordde is definitely one, and I guess so is Snake.”
“True enough,” said Iimmi. “I guess that fits into Rule Number One.” He got up from the bed. “Come on. Let’s take a walk. I want to see some sunlight.” They went to the wall. Geo pressed it and a triangular panel slipped back.

===Chinese===
“啊？”伊米说。

“蛇和约德。”吉奥答道，“记得吧，阿戈说过，早先就有阿普托来的奸细。约德铁定是一个，我猜蛇也是。”

“很有道理。”伊米说，“我看这正合规矩一。”他从床上起来。“走，散散步去。我想见见太阳。”他们走到墙边。吉奥一按，一块三角形的壁板滑开了。

===Original===
When they had rounded four or five turns of hallway, Geo said, “I hope you can remember where we’ve been.”
“I’ve got a more or less perfect memory for directions,” Iimmi said.
Suddenly the passage opened onto steps, and they were looking out upon a huge, unrelieved white chamber. Down a set of thirty marble steps priestesses filed below them in rows, their heads fixed blindly forward.

===Chinese===
拐过四五个走廊弯道后，吉奥说：“但愿你记得咱们走过的路。”

“我对方向的记忆，差不多算是过目不忘。”伊米说。

走道忽然敞成一段台阶，他们正俯瞰一个巨大、纯然一色的白厅。一列列女祭司顺着三十级大理石阶从他们脚下鱼贯而行，头颅一径定定地朝前，茫然无所视。
===Original===
At the far end was a raised dais with a mammoth statue of a kneeling woman, sculptured of the same effulgent, agate material. “Where do these women come from?” whispered Geo. “And where do they keep the men?”
Iimmi shrugged.
Suddenly, the figure of the blind Priestess was beside them.

===Chinese===
尽头处是一座高台，台上一尊跪姿女子的巨型雕像，用的还是那种莹莹发光的玛瑙质料。“这些女人都是从哪儿来的？”吉奥低声问，“她们把男人搁在哪儿？”

伊米耸耸肩。

忽然，盲眼女祭司的身影已到了他们身旁。

===Original===
“Excuse me, ma’am,” Iimmi said, sensing her disapproval of their presence, “we didn’t mean to be disrespectful, but we are creatures who are used to natural day and night. We are used to fresh air, green things. This underground whiteness is oppressive to us and makes us restless. Is there any way that you could show us a way into the open?”
“There is not,” returned the blind Priestess quietly and motioned them to follow her from the chamber. “Besides, night is coming on and you are not creatures who relish darkness.”

===Chinese===
“恕我冒昧，夫人。”伊米说，觉出了她对他们的出现并不赞许，“我们无意冒犯，只是我们是惯于自然昼夜的生灵。惯于新鲜空气，惯于青绿草木。这地底的白让我们憋闷，让我们烦躁。有没有法子，能请您指给我们一条去户外的路？”

“没有。”盲眼女祭司平静地答道，招手让他们跟她离开此厅，“再说，夜快到了，你们又不是喜欢黑暗的生灵。”

===Original===
“The night air and the quiet of evening is refreshing to us,” countered Iimmi.
“What do you know of the night,” answered the priestess with faint cynicism in her low voice. Now they reached the chapel where the friends had first met after their rescue.
“What can you tell us about the Dark God Hama?” Geo asked.

===Chinese===
“夜里的空气和傍晚的宁静，恰恰能让我们焕然清爽。”伊米反驳道。

“你们懂得什么夜晚。”女祭司低声答道，嗓音里带着一丝淡淡的讥诮。这时他们到了那座礼拜堂——获救之后，几个伙伴初次重逢的地方。

“关于黑暗之神哈马，您能告诉我们些什么？”吉奥问。

===Original===
The blind Priestess shrugged, and sat down on one of the benches. “There is little to say. Today he is a fiction, he does not exist. There is only Argo, the One White Goddess.”
“But we’ve heard﻿—” Geo began.
“You were at his abandoned temple,” said the Priestess. “You saw yourselves. That is all that is left of Hama. Ghouls prey on the dust of his dead saints. Perhaps, somewhere behind the burning mountain a few of his disciples are left. But Hama is dead in Aptor. You have seen the remains of his city, the City of New Hope. You have also been the first ones to go in and return in nearly five hundred years.”

===Chinese===
盲眼女祭司耸耸肩，在一条长凳上坐下。“没什么可说的。如今他只是一则虚构，他并不存在。存在的只有阿戈，那独一无二的白女神。”

“可我们听说过——”吉奥刚开口。

“你们到过他那座废弃的神庙。”女祭司说，“你们亲眼看见了。那就是哈马留下的一切。食尸鬼们猎食他死去圣徒的尘埃。也许，在燃烧的大山背后的什么地方，还剩着几个他的门徒。但哈马在阿普托已经死了。你们见过他城市的残躯，新望城。你们也是近五百年来，头一批走进去又活着出来的人。”

===Original===
“Is that how long the city has been in ruin?” asked Geo.
“It is.”
“What can you tell us about the city?” Iimmi said.

===Chinese===
“那座城荒废了这么久了？”吉奥问。

“正是。”

“关于那座城，您能告诉我们些什么？”伊米说。

===Original===
The Priestess sighed again. “There was a time,” she began, “generations ago, when Hama was a high God in Aptor. He had many temples, monasteries, and convents devoted to him. We had few. Except for these religious sanctuaries, the land was barbaric, wild, uninhabitable for the most part. There had once been cities in Aptor, but these had been destroyed even earlier by the Great Fire. All that we had was a fantastic record of an unbelievable time before the rain of flame of tremendous power, vast science, and a towering, though degenerate, civilization. These records were extensive, and entirely housed within the monasteries. Outside the monasteries, there was only chaos, where half the children were born dead, and the other half deformed. And with the monstrous races that sprang up over the island now as a reminder to us, we declared that the magic contained in these chronicles was evil, and must never be released to the world again. But the priests of Hama, decided to use the information in these chronicles, spread it to the people, and declared they would not commit the same mistakes that had brought the Great Fire. They opened the books, and the City of New Hope grew on the far shore of the river. They made giant machines that flew through the air. They constructed immense boats which could sink into the sea and emerge hundreds of miles away in another harbor in another land. They even harnessed for beneficial use the fire metal, uranium, which had brought such terror to the world before and had brought down the flames.”
“But they made the same mistake as the people before the Great Fire made?” suggested Iimmi.

===Chinese===
女祭司又叹了口气。“曾有过那样一个时代，”她开口道，“好几代人之前，哈马还是阿普托的至高之神。他有众多的神庙、修道院和女修道院供奉，而我们的圣所寥寥无几。除了这些宗教圣地，这片土地野蛮荒凉，大半不宜人居。阿普托从前也有过城市，但更早的时候就被大火灾毁了。我们手中所有的，只是一份奇诡的记载，记着烈焰之雨前那段难以置信的岁月——伟力无穷，科学浩瀚，还有一个巍峨却已颓坏的文明。这些记载卷帙浩繁，全都收藏在修道院里。修道院之外只有混沌：生下的孩子一半是死胎，另一半带着畸形。如今满岛冒出的这些怪异种族，正是对我们的警示，于是我们宣告：这些编年史里封存的法术乃是邪恶，绝不可再放归人世。可哈马的祭司们决意动用编年史里的知识，将其传布给民众，并宣称他们不会重蹈招致大火灾的覆辙。他们打开了那些书，新望城便在河对岸生长起来。他们造出在空中飞行的巨型机器，建造能沉入海底、再从几百英里外另一片陆地另一处港口浮出水面的大船。他们甚至收服了火金属——铀——使之造福于人；正是这东西，先前给世界带来如许恐怖，唤下过那漫天烈焰。”

“可他们还是犯了同大火灾之前的人们一样的错？”伊米试探道。

===Original===
“Not exactly,” said the Priestess. “That is, they were not so stupid as to misuse the fire metal which ravaged the world so harshly before. History is cyclic, not repetitive. A new power was discovered that dwarfed the significance of the fire metal. It could do all that the fire metal could do, and more efficiently: destroy cities, or warm chilly huts in winter; but, it could also work on men’s minds. They say, that before the Great Fire, men wandered the streets of the cities terrified that flames would descend on them any moment and destroy them. They panicked, bought flimsy useless contraptions to guard themselves from the fire. Geo, Iimmi, have you any idea how terrifying it would be to know that while walking the streets, at any moment, your mind might be snatched from you, raped, violated, and left broken in your own skull?
“Only three of these instruments were constructed. But the moment their existence was made known by a few fantastic demonstrations, the City of New Hope began the swerve down the arc of its own self-destruction. It lasted for a year, and ended with the broken wreck you escaped from last night. During that year invasions were launched on the backward nations across the sea with whom months before there had been friendly trade. Civil wars broke out and internal struggles caused the invasions to fall back to the homeland. The instruments were hopelessly lost, but not before the bird machines had even dropped bombs on the City of New Hope itself. The house of the fire metal was broken open to release its death once more. For a hundred years after the end, say our records, the city flamed with light from the destroyed power house. During the first hundred years more and more of our number were born blind because of the sinking fire in the city. At last we moved underground, but it was too late.” She rose from her seat. “And so you see, Hama destroyed himself. Today, loyal to Argo, are all the beasts of the air, of the land﻿ ﻿… and of the water.”

===Chinese===
“不完全是。”女祭司说，“也就是说，他们还不至于蠢到滥用火金属——先前它已把世界蹂躏得够惨了。历史是循环的，不是重复的。人们发现了一种新的力量，令火金属的意义都为之失色。火金属能做的它都能做，而且更高效：毁掉城市，或者在冬天温暖漏风的小屋；可它还能作用于人的心智。据说，大火灾之前，人们在城市的街道上彷徨，惶惶不可终日，生怕烈焰随时当头降下，把他们烧成灰。他们惊慌失措，买来单薄无用的破烂玩意儿护身防火。吉奥，伊米，你们可知道那会是何等的恐怖——走在街上，随时随刻，你的心智都可能被人夺走、被强暴、被糟蹋，然后破碎着扔回你自己的头骨里？”

“这样的器械，统共只造了三件。可就在几场惊天动地的演示让它们的存在公之于众的那一刻，新望城便开始拐上那道通往自我毁灭的弧线，一路滑落。这段下坡持续了一年，终点就是你们昨夜逃出来的那堆残骸。在那一年里，他们向海外那些落后邦国发动了入侵——而就在几个月前，双方还在友好通商。内战爆发，内部的争斗使入侵的兵锋折返本土。那几件器械彻底失落，无迹可寻；而在失落之前，飞鸟机器甚至已经把炸弹丢到了新望城自己头上。火金属的库房被砸开，死亡再度被释放出来。据我们的记载，末日之后足有一百年，那座城还借着被毁的发电站的光，彻夜燃亮。头一个百年里，我们当中出生便失明的越来越多，皆因城里那渐渐沉落的火。最后我们迁入地下，但为时已晚。”她从座位上站起。“所以你们明白了吧，哈马毁掉了他自己。如今，忠于阿戈的，是空中、陆上……以及水里的全部兽类。”

===Original===
“What about the﻿—the three instruments?” Geo asked. “What happened to them?”
The blind Priestess turned to him. “Your guess,” she said, smiling, “is as good as mine.” She turned again and glided softly from the room.

===Chinese===
“那——那三件器械呢？”吉奥问，“它们怎么样了？”

盲眼女祭司转向他。“你猜，”她微笑着说，“跟我猜的一样准。”她又转过身，轻盈地飘出了房间。

===Original===
When she left, Iimmi said, “Something is fishy.”
“But what is it?” said Geo.
“Well, for one thing,” said Iimmi, “we know there is a Hama. From the dream I would say that it’s just about the size and organization of this place.”

===Chinese===
她走后，伊米说：“这里有蹊跷。”

“蹊跷在哪儿？”吉奥说。

“喏，头一件，”伊米说，“咱们知道哈马是存在的。照那个梦来判断，它的规模和组织，同这地方不相上下。”

===Original===
“Just how big is this place anyway?” Geo asked.
“Want to do some more exploring?”
“Sure,” he answered. “Do you think she does know about Hama but was just pretending?”

===Chinese===
“这地方到底有多大？”吉奥问。

“还想再探探吗？”

“当然想。”他答道，“你觉得她其实知道哈马的事，只是装蒜？”
===Original===
“Could be,” said Iimmi. They started off down another corridor. “That bit about going into men’s minds with the jewels,” Iimmi went on. “It gives me the creeps.”
“It’s a creepy thing to watch,” said Geo. “Argo used it on Snake the first time we saw her. It just turns you into an automaton.”
“Then it really is our jewels she was talking about.”

===Chinese===
“很可能。”伊米说。他们沿另一条走廊出发了。“就是珍宝能钻进人心智的那一段，”伊米接着说，“听得我直发毛。”

“亲眼看着更发毛。”吉奥说，“咱们头一回见阿戈的时候，她就对蛇用过。它干脆把人变成一具自动木偶。”

“那她说的就真是咱们的珍宝了。”

===Original===
Stairs cut a white tunnel into the wall before them, and they mounted upward, coming finally to another corridor. They turned down it and for the first time saw recognizable doors in the wall. “Hey,” said Iimmi, “maybe one of these goes outside.”
“Fine,” said Geo. “This place is beginning to get me.” He pushed open a door and stepped in. Except for the flowing white walls, it duplicated in miniature the basement of the New Edison building. Twin dynamos whirred and the walls were laced with pipes.
“Nothing in here,” said Iimmi.

===Chinese===
一段台阶在面前的墙上凿出一条白色的隧道，他们拾级而上，最后到了另一条走廊。他们拐进去，头一回在墙上见到了形状眼熟的门。“嘿，”伊米说，“说不定这其中有扇门通外面。”

“好啊。”吉奥说，“这地方开始让我心里发毛了。”他推开一扇门走进去。除了流线形的白墙，这屋子就是新爱迪生大楼（the New Edison building）地下室的缩影。两台孪生的发电机嗡嗡转着，墙上管道纵横交错。

“这儿没什么。”伊米说。

===Original===
They tried a door across the hall now. In this one sat a white porcelain table and floor to ceiling cases of glittering instruments. “I bet this is the room your arm came off in,” Iimmi said.
“Probably,” replied Geo.
They came out and continued even farther. In the next room the glow was dimmer, and there was dust on the walls. Iimmi ran his finger over it and looked at the gray crescent left on the bleached flesh.

===Chinese===
他们又试了走廊对面的一扇门。这间屋里摆着一张白瓷桌，还有从地板顶到天花板的柜子，装着闪闪发亮的仪器。“我敢打赌，你那条胳膊就是在这么间屋里卸下来的。”伊米说。

“多半是。”吉奥答道。

他们出来，继续往深处走。到下一间屋子，光就暗了，墙上积着灰。伊米用手指抹了一下，看着漂白的皮肤上留下的那弯月牙形的灰痕。

===Original===
Two huge screens leaned out from the face of a metal machine. A few dials and a glass meter hung beneath each two yard rounded-rectangle of opaque glass. In front of each was a stand which held something like a set of binoculars and what looked like a pair of ear muffs.
“I bet this place hasn’t been used since before these girls went blind,” said Geo.
“It looks it,” Iimmi said. He stepped up to one of the screens, the one with the fewer dials on it, and turned a switch.

===Chinese===
两块巨大的屏幕从一台金属机器的正面斜探出来。每块两码长、圆角矩形的不透明玻璃下面，都挂着几个表盘和一只玻璃计量表。每块屏前立着一个架子，架着一副像双筒望远镜似的东西，还有一样看着像耳罩的物件。

“我敢说，这些姑娘瞎掉之前，这地方就没人用过了。”吉奥说。

“看得出。”伊米说。他走到其中一块屏幕前——表盘较少的那块——拧动了一个开关。

===Original===
“What did you do that for?” Geo asked.
“Why not?” said Iimmi. Suddenly a flickering of colored lights ran over the screen, swellings of blue, green, shiny scarlets. They blinked. “That’s the first color I’ve seen since I’ve been here,” Iimmi said. The colors grayed, dimmed, congealed into forms, and in a moment they were looking at a bare white room in which stood two barefoot young men. One was a dark Negro with pale hands. The other had an unruly shock of black hair and only one arm.
“Hey,” gestured Iimmi, and the figure on the screen gestured too. “That’s us.” He walked forward and the corresponding figure advanced on the screen. He flicked a dial and the figures exploded into colors and then focused again. “What’s that?” asked Iimmi.

===Chinese===
“你干吗开它？”吉奥问。

“怎么，不行吗？”伊米说。忽然，一阵彩色的光在屏上跑动起来，一团团蓝色、绿色、亮闪闪的猩红。光闪了几闪。“这是我来这儿以后见到的头一抹颜色。”伊米说。色彩转灰、转暗、凝成形影，转眼间他们看到的已是一间空荡荡的白屋子，屋里站着两个赤脚的年轻人。一个是皮肤黝黑的黑人，一双手却泛着苍白。另一个顶着一头桀骜的黑发，只剩一条胳膊。

“嘿。”伊米做了个手势，屏上的人影也做了个手势。“是咱们。”他往前走，屏上相应的人影也往前挪。他弹了一下表盘，人影哗地炸成一片色彩，随即又聚焦成形。“那是什么？”伊米问。

===Original===
In a room stood three of the blind women. On one wall was a smaller screen similar to the one in their own room. The women, of course, were oblivious to the picture on it, but it was the picture on the screen that had stopped Geo. It was a face. A man’s face.
One of the women had on an ear muff apparatus and was talking into a small metal rod which she carried with her as she paced.

===Chinese===
一间屋里站着三个盲眼女人。一面墙上有一块较小的屏幕，同他们那间屋里的一样。那些女人自然对屏上的画面毫无知觉，可让吉奥收住脚步的，恰是屏上那画面。是一张脸。一张男人的脸。

其中一个戴着那副耳罩似的装置，一边踱步，一边对着一根随身携带的小金属杆说话。

===Original===
“But the picture! Don’t you recognize him?” demanded Geo.
“It’s Jordde!” exclaimed Iimmi. “They must have gotten in contact with our ship and are arranging to send us back.”
“I wish I could hear what they’re saying,” said Geo.

===Chinese===
“可那画面！你认不出他吗？”吉奥急问。

“是约德！”伊米失声道，“她们准是同咱们的船接上了头，正安排送咱们回去呢。”

“要能听见她们说什么就好了。”吉奥说。

===Original===
Iimmi looked around and then picked up the metal ear muffs from the stand in front of the screen. “That’s what she seems to be listening through,” said Iimmi, referring to the Priestess in the picture. “Try them. Go on.” He helped Geo fit them over his ears. “Hear anything?”
Geo listened.
“Yes, of course,” the Priestess was saying.

===Chinese===
伊米四下看了看，从屏前的架子上拿起那副金属耳罩。“她像是就在通过这个听。”伊米说，指的是画面里的女祭司。“试试。快。”他帮吉奥把耳罩扣到耳朵上。“听见什么了吗？”

吉奥听着。

“是的，当然。”女祭司正说着。

===Original===
“She is set upon staying in the harbor for three more days, to wait out the week,” reported Jordde. “I am sure she will not remain any longer. She is still bewildered by me, and the men have become uneasy and may well mutiny if she stays longer.”
“We will dispose of the prisoners this evening. There is no chance of their returning,” stated the Priestess.
“Detain them for three days, and I do not care what you do with them,” said Jordde. “She does not have the jewels, she does not know my﻿—our power; she will be sure to leave at the end of the week.”

===Chinese===
“她打定主意在港里再停三天，挨满这一周。”约德报告道，“我确信她不会再多留。她对我还是摸不着头脑，水手们也躁动起来了，她再不走，多半要哗变。”

“我们今晚就处置掉那几个俘虏。他们没有回去的可能了。”女祭司说道。

“扣他们三天，之后随你怎么处置，我不在乎。”约德说，“珍宝不在她手里，她也不知道我的——我们的力量；这一周过完，她一定走。”

===Original===
“It’s a pity we have no jewels for all our trouble,” said the Priestess. “But at least all three are back in Aptor, and potentially within our grasp.”
Jordde laughed. “And Hama never seems to be able to keep hold of them for more then ten minutes before they slip from him again.”
“Yours is not to judge either Hama or Argo,” stated the Priestess. “You are kept on by us only to do your job. Do it, report, and do not trouble either us or yourself with opinions. They are not appreciated.”

===Chinese===
“费了这许多周折却没拿到珍宝，可惜了。”女祭司说，“不过至少三个都回到了阿普托，都在我们掌心边上。”

约德笑了。“而且哈马好像从来攥不住他们超过十分钟，一转眼他们又从他指缝里溜了。”

“轮不到你来评判哈马，也轮不到你评判阿戈。”女祭司说道，“留用你，只是为了当差。当你的差，报你的信，别拿你那些见解来烦我们，也烦你自己。没人领情。”

===Original===
“Yes, mistress,” returned Jordde.
“Then farewell until next report.” She flipped a switch and the picture on the little screen went gray.
Geo turned from the big screen now, and was just about to remove the hearing apparatus when he heard the Priestess say, “Go, prepare the prisoners for the sacrifice of the rising moon. They have seen enough.” The woman left the room, Geo finished removing the phones, and Iimmi looked at him.

===Chinese===
“是，主母。”约德应道。

“那么别过，等下次报告。”她啪地按下一个开关，小屏上的画面灰了下去。

吉奥从大屏前转过身来，正要摘下那副听音的家伙，忽听女祭司说道：“去，把俘虏们准备好，做升月之祭。他们看得够多了。”那女人出了屋，吉奥摘下耳机，伊米看着他。
===Original===
“What’s the matter?”
Geo turned the switch that darkened the screen.
“When are they coming to get us?” Iimmi asked excitedly.

===Chinese===
“怎么了？”

吉奥拧了拧让屏幕变暗的开关。

“他们什么时候来接咱们？”伊米兴奋地问。

===Original===
“Right now, probably,” Geo said. Then, as best he could, he repeated the conversation he had overheard to Iimmi, whose expression grew more and more bewildered as Geo went on.
At the end the bewilderment suddenly flared into frayed indignation. “Why?” demanded Iimmi. “Why should we be sacrificed? What is it we’ve seen too much of, what is it we know? This is the second time it’s come close to getting me killed, and I wish to hell I knew what I was supposed to know?”

===Chinese===
“多半马上就来。”吉奥说。然后，他把偷听来的那场对话尽量原样复述给伊米；随着他往下讲，伊米的脸色越来越茫然。

听到最后，那茫然骤然蹿起，燃成一腔炸了毛的怒火。“为什么？”伊米质问，“凭什么拿我们献祭？我们什么东西看多了，我们知道了什么？这已经是第二回差点儿送我的命了，我他妈的真想知道，我到底该知道些什么？”

===Original===
“We’ve got to find Urson and get out of here,” said Geo. “Hey, what’s wrong?”
The indignation had turned into something else. Now Iimmi stood with his eyes shut tight and his face screwed up. Suddenly he relaxed. “I just thought out a message as loud as I could for Snake to get up here and to bring Urson if he’s anywhere around.”
“But Snake’s a spy for﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“咱们得找到厄尔森，离开这儿。”吉奥说，“喂，你怎么了？”

那股怒火变成了别的东西。此刻伊米紧闭双眼，五官拧作一团。忽然他松了下来。“我刚在心里拼命想了一句话，喊蛇到这儿来，要是厄尔森在附近，也把他带来。”

“可蛇是替……”

===Original===
“… for Hama,” said Iimmi. “And you know something? I don’t care.” He closed his eyes again. After a few moments, he opened them. “Well, if he’s coming, he’s coming. Let’s get going.”
“But why﻿ ﻿… ?” began Geo, following Iimmi out the door.
“Because I have a poet’s feeling that some fancy mind reading may come in handy.”

===Chinese===
“……替哈马的奸细。”伊米说，“可你知道吗？我不在乎。”他又闭上眼。过了片刻，睁开。“好了，他要是来，自然会来。咱们走吧。”

“可为什么……？”吉奥一面跟出门口，一面问。

“因为我有种诗人的直觉：到节骨眼上，一手漂亮的心灵感应，兴许能救急。”

===Original===
They hurried down the hall, found the stairs, ducked down, and ran along the lower hall. Rounding a second corner, they emerged into the little chapel simultaneously with Urson and Snake.
“I guess I got through,” said Iimmi. “Which way do we go?”
“Gentlemen, gentlemen,” came a voice from behind them.

===Chinese===
他们匆匆沿走廊跑去，寻到台阶，俯身而下，又顺着下层走廊狂奔。拐过第二个弯，他们同厄尔森和蛇同时涌进了那座小礼拜堂。

“看来我喊通了。”伊米说，“往哪儿走？”

“诸位，诸位。”一个声音从他们身后传来。

===Original===
Snake took off down one of the passages, and they followed, Urson looking particularly bewildered.
The Priestess glided behind them, calling softly, “Please, my friends, come back. Return with me.”
“Find out from her how the hell to get out of this place!” Iimmi bawled up to Snake. The four-armed boy suddenly darted up a flight of stairs, turned a corner, and darted up another. They came out on a hall and followed Snake to the end.

===Chinese===
蛇顺着其中一条通道窜了出去，他们跟着跑，厄尔森一脸大惑不解。

女祭司在他们身后飘行着追来，柔声唤道：“求你们，我的朋友们，回来吧。随我回去。”

“问问她这鬼地方到底怎么出去！”伊米冲蛇吼道。四臂少年忽然蹿上一段台阶，拐个弯，又蹿上另一段。他们出到一条走廊，跟着蛇跑到尽头。

===Original===
All four of the boy’s hands flew at the door handle, turning it carefully, this way, and back.
Two, three seconds.
Geo glanced back and saw the Priestess mount the top of the stairs and begin to come toward them. She seemed to float, her white robes flaring out from her, brushing at the walls.

===Chinese===
少年的四只手一齐扑上门把，小心地拧着，往这边，又往回。

两秒，三秒。

吉奥回头一瞥，见女祭司已登上台阶顶，朝他们走来。她像浮在半空，白袍从身上张开来，扫着两边的墙。

===Original===
The door came open, they broke through leaves, and were momentarily standing in a huge field of grass, surrounded by woods. The night was fully lit by the moon.
As they ran through the silver-washed grass, Geo turned to look behind him. The blind Priestess had slowed, her white face turned to the moon. Her hands went to her throat, she unclasped her robe, and the first layer fell away behind her. As she came on, the second layer began to unfold, wet, deathly white, spreading, growing to her arms, articulating itself along the white spines; then, with a horribly familiar shriek, she leapt from the ground and soared upward, her white wings hammering the air.
They fled.

===Chinese===
门开了，他们冲破枝叶，一时间已站在一大片草场上，四围是树林。夜里洒满月光，亮堂堂的。

他们在银濯濯的草浪里奔逃，吉奥回头望去。盲眼女祭司放慢了速度，白脸仰向月亮。她的双手探到喉间，解开搭扣，第一层袍子在她身后褪落。她继续逼近，第二层开始张开——湿漉漉的，死一样的白，铺展着，长上她的手臂，沿着白色的棘骨一节节支棱起来；随后，带着一声熟悉得可怕的尖啸，她腾地跃起，直冲上天，白翼擂打着空气。

他们亡命奔逃。

===Original===
And other dark forms were shadowing the moon. The priestesses across the field joined her aloft in the moon-bleached sky. She overtook the running figures, turned above them, and swooped. The moon lanced white along bared fangs. The night breeze touched pale furry breasts, filled the bellying wings. Only the tiny, darting, blind eyes were red, rubied in a whirl of white.
They crashed into the protective bushes where the winged things could not follow. Branches raked his face as he ran behind the sound the others made. Once he thought he had lost them, but a second later he bumped against Iimmi, who had stopped behind Snake and Urson, in the darkness. Above the trees was a sound like beaten cloth, diminishing, growing, but constant as once more they began to trod through the tangled darkness.
“What the hell﻿ ﻿…” Iimmi finally breathed softly, after a minute of walking.

===Chinese===
别的黑影也正遮蔽着月亮。草场那头的女祭司们纷纷腾起，加入她，高飞在被月色漂白的天空。她追过奔逃的人影，在他们头顶一个回旋，俯冲而下。月光顺着裸露的獠牙挑起一线惨白。夜风拂过苍白毛茸的胸乳，灌满鼓胀的双翼。只有那双细小、乱转、失明的眼睛是红的，像一圈白色漩涡中两粒红宝石。

他们撞进庇护的灌木丛，有翼的东西追不进来。他跟在其他人弄出的声响后面跑，枝条耙过他的脸。有一阵他以为自己跟丢了，可紧接着撞上了伊米——他已停在黑暗中蛇和厄尔森的身后。树顶上头，有一种像抽打布匹般的声响，时弱，时强，却不曾断过；他们又一次开始跋涉在纠结的黑暗里。

“这他妈的到底……”走了一分钟后，伊米终于低低吐出一句。
===Original===
“You know it’s beginning to make sense,” Geo said, his hand on Iimmi’s shoulder. “Remember that man-wolf we met, and that blob in the city? The only thing we’ve met on this place that can’t change shape is the ghouls. I think most animals on this island undergo some sort of metamorphosis.”
“What about those first flying things we met?” whispered Urson. “They didn’t change into anything.”
“We have probably just been guests of the female of the species,” said Geo.

===Chinese===
“你知道吗，这些事开始能串起来了。”吉奥说，手搭在伊米肩上。“还记得咱们碰上的那个人狼，还有城里那团软塌塌的东西吗？咱们在这地方遇上的玩意儿里，只有食尸鬼不会变形。我看这岛上大多数动物，都要经历某种蜕变。”

“那咱们头一回碰上的那些会飞的呢？”厄尔森低声问，“它们可没变成什么。”

“咱们多半刚在人家雌性的门下做了回客。”吉奥说。

===Original===
“You mean those others could have changed into men too if they wanted?” Urson asked.
“If they wanted,” answered Geo.
In front of them now appeared faint shiftings of silver light. Five minutes later, they were crouching at the edge of the forest, looking down over the rocks at the white shimmerings over the river.

===Chinese===
“你是说，那些别的家伙想变的话，也能变成人？”厄尔森问。

“想变就变。”吉奥答道。

这时，前方浮起了银光隐隐的晃动。五分钟后，他们已伏在林缘，越过岩石，俯瞰河面上泛起的白色微光。

===Original===
“Into the water?” Geo asked.
Snake shook his head. Wait﻿ ﻿… came the familiar sound in their heads.
Suddenly a hand raised from the water. Wet and green, it stood a foot or so from the shore in the silver ripples. The chain and the leather thong dangled down the wrist, and swaying there were two bright beads of light.

===Chinese===
“下水？”吉奥问。

蛇摇了摇头。等等……那熟悉的声音在他们脑海里响起。

忽然，一只手从水里举了起来。湿漉漉，绿莹莹，在银色的涟漪中离岸一英尺光景立着。链条和皮绳从腕上垂下，上面摇曳着两粒亮亮的光珠。

===Original===
Iimmi and Geo froze. Urson said, “The jewels.﻿ ﻿…”
Suddenly, crouched low like an animal, the big man sprang onto the rocks and ran toward the river’s edge.
Three shadows, one white, two dark, converged above him, cutting the moonlight away from him. If he saw them, he did not stop.

===Chinese===
伊米和吉奥僵住了。厄尔森说：“珍宝……”

忽然，大汉像头野兽般伏低身子，蹿上岩石，直奔河边跑去。

三条影子——一白二黑——在他头顶上方合拢下来，把月光从他身上剪断。他就是看见了，也没有停。

===Original===
Iimmi and Geo stood up from their crouched positions.
Urson reached the shore, threw himself along the rock, and swiped at the hand. Instantly he was covered by flailing wings. The membranous sails splashed in the water. Two seconds later, Urson rolled from beneath the layers of membrane that still struggled half on land and half in the water. He started forward up the rocks. He slipped, regained his footing, and then came on, nearly falling into Geo’s and Iimmi’s waiting arms.
“The jewels,” Urson breathed.

===Chinese===
伊米和吉奥从伏低的地方站起身来。

厄尔森冲到岸边，整个人顺着岩石扑出去，朝那只手猛挥一抓。刹那间，扑打的翅膀已把他罩住。膜质的帆翼在水里哗哗拍溅。两秒钟后，厄尔森从那几层还在半陆半水中挣扎的翼膜底下滚了出来。他朝岸上、朝岩石爬去。滑了一下，稳住脚，接着往上走，几乎跌进吉奥和伊米张开等他的怀抱里。

“珍宝。”厄尔森喘着气说。

===Original===
The struggle continued a minute longer on the water. Something was holding them down, twisting at them. Then suddenly, the creatures stilled, and like great leaves, the three forms drifted apart, caught quietly in the current, and floated away from the rocks.
Then two more forms bobbed to the surface, faces down, rocking gently, backs slicked wet and green, shiny under the moonlight.
“But those were the ones who﻿—” Geo began. “Are they dead?” His face suddenly hurt a little, with something like the pain of verging tears.

===Chinese===
水面上的搏斗又持续了一分钟。有什么东西正按着它们、拧绞着它们。忽然，那些生物不动了，三具身影像三大片叶子般散开，静静兜进水流，从岩石边漂走了。

接着又浮上来两具，脸朝下，随水轻轻地起伏，背脊湿绿溜光，在月色下亮闪闪的。

“可那两个明明是——”吉奥刚开口。“它们死了吗？”他的脸忽然有点发疼，像泪水将决未决的那种疼。

===Original===
Snake nodded.
“Are you sure?” asked Iimmi. His voice came slowly.
Their﻿ ﻿… thoughts﻿ ﻿… have﻿ ﻿… stopped, Snake said.
Crouched down in front of them, Urson opened his great hands. The globes blazed even in the dim light through the leaves, and the chain and the wet thong hung over his palm to the ground. “I have them,” he said, “… the jewels!”

===Chinese===
蛇点了点头。

“你确定吗？”伊米问。他的话说得很慢。

它们的……思想……已经……停了，蛇说。

厄尔森在他们面前蹲下，摊开那双大手。珠子即便在枝叶漏下的微光里也灼灼放光，链条和湿透的皮绳从掌心一直垂到地上。“我拿到它们了。”他说，“……珍宝！”

## X / 十、神目窃珠

===Original===
Dawn light lay aslant the crater’s ridge. Argo pointed down the opposite slope. A black temple was visible at the bottom among trees and lawns. “There is Hama’s temple,” Argo said. “You have your task. Good luck.”
They started down the incline of cinders. It took them an hour to reach the first trees that surrounded the dark buildings and the great gardens. Entering on the first lip of grass, they heard a sudden cluster of notes from one of the trees.
“A bird,” Iimmi said. “I haven’t heard one of those since I left Leptar.”

===Chinese===
晨光斜落在火山口的脊线上。阿戈（Argo）指向对面的山坡。谷底的树木和草坪之间，隐约可见一座黑色的神庙。“那就是哈马（Hama）的神庙，”阿戈说，“你们的任务在那儿。祝你们好运。”

他们开始顺着火山灰的斜坡下行。走了一个钟头，才到达环绕着黑楼与巨大花园的第一排树。刚踏上第一片草沿，他们就听见一棵树上突然滚下一串音符。

“是鸟，”伊米（Iimmi）说，“自从离开莱普塔（Leptar），我就再没听过这动静了。”

===Original===
Suddenly, bright blue and the length of a man’s forefinger, a lizard ran halfway down the trunk of the tree. Its sapphire belly heaved in the early light with indrawn breath; then it opened its red mouth, its throat warbled, and there was another burst of music.
“Oh well,” said Iimmi. “I was close.”
They walked further, until Iimmi mused, “I wonder why you always think things are going to turn out like you expect.”

===Chinese===
冷不防，一只蜥蜴——亮蓝色，足有人的一节食指长——顺着树干跑到一半停住。晨光里，它那蓝宝石色的肚皮随着吸气一起一伏；随后它张开红嘴，喉咙一颤，又是一串乐音迸出来。

“唉，”伊米说，“也八九不离十了。”

他们又走了一程，伊米忽然若有所思：“真奇怪，你们怎么总以为事情会照你们料想的样子发展。”

===Original===
“Because when something sounds like that,” declared Urson, “it usually is a bird!” Suddenly he gave a little shiver. “Lizards,” he said.
“It was a pretty lizard,” said Iimmi.
“Going around expecting things to be what they seem can get you in trouble﻿—especially on this island,” Geo commented.
The angle at which they walked made one of the clumps of tree before them seem to fall apart. A man standing in the center raised his hand and said briskly, “Stop!”

===Chinese===
“因为什么东西叫起来是那个动静，”厄尔森（Urson）断言道，“它通常就是只鸟！”话音未落，他打了个小小的寒噤。“蜥蜴。”他说。

“那是只漂亮的蜥蜴。”伊米说。

“总以为事物表里如一，是要吃亏的——尤其是在这座岛上。”吉奥（Geo）评论道。

走动的角度一变，前方一处树丛仿佛忽然散开。站在树丛中央的一个男人举起手，干脆利落地说：“站住！”

===Original===
They stopped.
He wore dark robes, and his short white hair made a close helmet above his brown face.
Urson’s hand was on his sword. Snake stood with his feet wide, his hands out from his sides.
“Who are you?” the dark man declared.

===Chinese===
他们站住了。

他身穿黑袍，一头短短的白发像顶紧盔，扣在棕色的脸庞上方。

厄尔森的手按在剑柄上。蛇（Snake）叉开双腿站着，双手张在身体两侧。

“你们是什么人？”黑衣人朗声问。

===Original===
“Who are you?” Urson parried.
“I am Hama Incarnate.”
They were silent. Finally Geo said, “We are travelers in Aptor. We don’t mean any harm.”
As the man moved forward, splotches of light from the trees slipped across his robe. “Come with me,” Hama said. He turned and proceeded among the trees. They followed.

===Chinese===
“你是什么人？”厄尔森反问。

“我是哈马化身（Hama Incarnate）。”

一时无人作声。末了吉奥开口：“我们是阿普托（Aptor）的旅人，没有恶意。”

那人往前走时，树间洒下的光斑从他袍上滑过。“跟我来。”哈马说。他转身在树间引路，他们跟了上去。

===Original===
They passed into the temple garden. It was early enough in the morning so that the sunlight lapped pink tongues over the giant black urns that sat along the edges of the path. Now they passed into the temple.
As they passed, Hama turned, looked at the jewels on Iimmi’s and Geo’s necks, and then looked up at the gazing eye of the statue at the end of the altar. He made no other sign, but turned again and continued. “The morning rites have not yet started,” he said. “They will begin in a half an hour. By then I hope to have divined your purpose in coming here.”
At the other side of the stairway they mounted a stairway, and then entered a door above which was a black circle dotted with three eyes. Just as they were about to go in, Geo looked around, frowned, and caught Iimmi’s eye. “Snake?” he mouthed.
Iimmi looked around and shrugged.

===Chinese===
他们走进神庙的园子。清晨还早，阳光把粉红的舌头舔在沿小径两侧蹲踞的一口口大黑瓮上。这时他们走进了神庙。

走过时，哈马回过头，看了看伊米和吉奥颈上的珍宝，又抬眼望向祭坛尽头那尊雕像凝视的眼睛。他没有别的表示，只又转回身继续前行。“晨祭还没开始，”他说，“再过半个钟头开始。到时候，我希望能参透你们来此的目的。”

在楼梯的另一侧，他们又登上一道楼梯，随即走进一扇门，门头上是一个黑圆盘，缀着三只眼睛。正要进门时，吉奥环顾四周，皱起眉，朝伊米递了个眼色，无声地动了动嘴形：“蛇呢？”

伊米四下望了望，耸耸肩。

===Original===
The man turned and faced them, apparently unaware of Snake’s departure. As he closed the door, now, he said, “You have come to oppose the forces of Aptor, am I right? You come to steal the jewel of Hama. You have come to kidnap the Incarnate Argo. Is that not your purpose. Keep your hand off your sword, Urson! I can kill you in a moment. You are defenseless.”
---

===Chinese===
那人转过身来面对他们，显然没有察觉蛇已经离开。此时他一边关上门，一边说：“你们是来对抗阿普托的势力的，我说得对吧？你们来偷哈马的珍宝。你们来绑架阿戈化身（the Incarnate Argo）。这不正是你们的目的。把手从剑上拿开，厄尔森！我随时能杀了你。你们毫无防备。”

---

===Original===
“Damn! I’m sleepy.” She rolled over and cuddled the pillow. Then she opened her eyes, one at a time, and lay watching the nearly completed motor of metal bars and copper wire that sat on the table beside her bed. She stood up.
Then she collapsed on the bed and jammed her feet under the covers again. With thirty feet of one and a half inch brass pipe, she mused sleepily, I could carry heat from the main hot-water line under the floor which I would estimate to be about the proper surface area to keep these stones warm; let me see, thirty feet of one and a half inch pipe have a surface area of ²²⁄₇ times ³⁄₂ times 30 which is 990 divided by 7 which is﻿ ﻿… Then she caught herself. Damn, you’re thinking this to avoid thinking about getting up. She opened her eyes once more, put feet on the stone, and held them there while she scratched vigorously at her uneven mop of red hair.
She looked at the clock. “Yikes!” she said softly, and ran out the door, and slammed it behind her﻿—almost. She whirled around, caught it on her palms before it banged shut, and then closed it with gingerly care the final centimeter and a half of the arc. Are you trying to get caught? she asked herself as she tiptoed to the next door.

===Chinese===
“该死！真困。”她翻了个身，抱住枕头。随后她睁开眼睛——一次睁一只——躺着望向床边桌上那台金属棒和铜线扎成的、几近完工的电动机。她站了起来。

接着她又倒回床上，把双脚重新塞进被子。要是用三十英尺长、一英寸半口径的黄铜管，她睡意朦胧地盘算着，我就能把热从地板底下的热水总管引过来，我估计那表面积差不多正好能把这些石头焐热；让我算算，三十英尺一英寸半的管子，表面积是 ²²⁄₇ 乘 ³⁄₂ 乘 30，等于 990 除以 7，也就是……想到这儿她猛然回神。该死，你算这些，就是想逃避想起床这件事。她再度睁开眼睛，把脚放到石地上，一边任脚就那么搁着，一边用力挠着那一头参差不齐的红发。

她看了看钟。“呀！”她轻轻叫了一声，冲出门去，随手把门往身后一摔——几乎是摔上的。她猛地转身，在门撞拢之前用双掌抵住，然后用小心翼翼的轻缓，把门送完最后一厘米半的弧线。你是想被人抓住吗？她一边踮脚走向隔壁那扇门，一边问自己。

===Original===
She opened it and looked in. Dunderhead looks cute when he’s asleep, she thought. There was a cord on the floor that ran from under the table by the priest’s bed, over the stones, carefully following the zigzag of the crevices between them, and at last the end lay in the corner of the door sill. You really couldn’t see it if you weren’t looking for it, which had more or less been the idea when she had put it there last night before the priests had come back from vespers. The far end was tied in a knot of her own invention to the electric plug of his alarm clock. Dunderhead had an annoying habit of resetting his clock every evening making sure that the red second hand was still sweeping away the minutes. (In her plans for this morning she had catalogued his every habitual action, and had observed this one for three nights running, hanging upside down from the bulky stone portcullis above and outside his window.)
Tugging on the string, she saw it leap from the crevices into a straight line and then lift from the floor as she drew it tauter, and then go slack as the plug blipped quietly onto the floor.
Next she pulled the string again until the slack left and raised her end a few inches from the floor. With her free hand now she gave the string a small twit and watched the vibration run up and down the string twice. The knot’s invention was an ingenuous one. At the vibration, two opposed loops shook away from a third, and a four millimeter length of rubber band that had been sewn in tautened and released a fourth loop from around a small length of number four gauge wire with a holding tensure of three quarters of a gram, and the opposing vibration returning up the thread loosed a similar apparatus on the other side of the plug. The knot fell away, and she wound it quickly around her hand. She stood up, closed the door, and the oiled lock was perfectly silent. The door knob was just the slightest bit greasy, she noted. Careless.

===Chinese===
她推开门朝里看。呆子（Dunderhead）睡着的时候还挺可爱，她想。地板上有一根细绳，从祭司床边的桌子底下牵出来，越过石板，仔细循着石板间缝隙的之字形走向，末端最后搭在门槛的角落里。你要不是存心找，还真看不见它——昨晚祭司们做完晚祷回来之前，她把它放在那儿时，打的差不多就是这个主意。另一头用她自己发明的一个绳结，拴在他闹钟的电插头上。呆子有个烦人的习惯：每天傍晚都要重新对闹钟，确信那根红色秒针仍在孜孜地扫过一分一秒。（在今天早晨的计划里，她已把他每个习惯动作一一编目；单为这一个动作，她接连三夜倒挂在他窗外上方那道笨重的石闸门上守着。）

她轻轻一拉绳，眼见它从缝隙里弹成一条直线，随着她越拉越紧而离地绷起，随后又松垂下来——插头轻轻一声，落在了地板上。

接着她又拉了拉绳，直到余松收尽，再把自己这一头抬离地板几英寸。然后她用空着的那只手在绳上轻轻一弹，看着震动沿绳上下跑了两趟。那个绳结是个精巧的发明。震动一到，两个相对的圈从第三个圈上抖脱，一段缝在里头、四毫米长的橡皮筋绷紧了，把第四个圈从一小段四号线规铁丝上松开——那铁丝的咬合张力为四分之三克——而沿丝线返回的反向震动，又松开了插头另一侧一套同样的机关。绳结脱落下来，她迅速把它缠上手。她站起身，关上门，上过油的锁一点声息也没有。门把手上有一丝若有若无的油腻，她注意到。真不小心。

===Original===
Back in her room, it was standing on the table. Sunlight from the high window fell red across the board. It was very early in the morning. She took the parts of the motor up in her hands. “I guess we try you out today? No?” She answered herself, “Yes.” Finally she put the parts in the paper bag, strode out of the room, and slammed the﻿ ﻿… whirled around and caught it once more. “Gnnnnnnn,” she said. “Do you want to get caught?” For the second time she answered herself, “Yes. And remember that too. Or you’ll never get through it.”
As she walked down the hall, she heard through one of the windows the chirp of a blue lizard from the garden. “The sound I wanted to hear,” she smiled to herself. “A good sign.”

===Chinese===
回到自己房里，它正立在桌上。高窗透进的晨光红红地落在台面上。时间还非常早。她把电动机的零件捧在手里。“我猜咱们今天就试试你？不试？”她自问自答：“试。”最后她把零件放进纸袋，大步走出房间，把门砰地一……猛转身又把门接住了。“唔嗯嗯嗯嗯，”她说，“你是想被人抓住吗？”她第二次回答自己：“想。这个也给我记住。不然你永远干不成。”

她沿走廊走去，从一扇窗子里听见花园里一只蓝色蜥蜴的鸣叫。“这正是我想听的声音，”她暗自微笑。“好兆头。”

===Original===
Turning into the temple, she started down the side aisle. The great black columns passed before her. Something moved between the columns along the other side, swift and indistinct as a bird’s shadow. At least she thought she saw something. “Remember,” she reminded herself, “you have guilt feelings about this whole thing, and you could very easily be manufacturing delusions to scare yourself out of going through with it.” She went on, passed two more columns, and saw it again. “Or,” she went on with her monologue, “you could be purposefully ignoring the very obvious fact that there is somebody over there who is going to see you. So watch it.” There were mirrors somewhere in the temple, but they weren’t on the opposite wall, so she couldn’t be seeing herself. In fact the mirrors were out in the vestibule through which she had come and maybe this other person had come, so maybe it was seeing her as a reflection of﻿ ﻿… “Unscramble that syntax,” she told herself. “You think like that and you’ll never make it.”
But there was somebody, with no clothes on (for all practical purposes) sneaking between the pillars. And he had four arms. That made her start to think of something else, but the thought as it arrowed into the past, suddenly got deflected, turned completely about, and jammed into her brain again, because he was staring directly at her.

===Chinese===
拐进神庙，她顺着侧廊往下走。巨大的黑柱一根根从她面前掠过。对面一侧的柱子之间有什么东西动了动，迅疾而模糊，像一道鸟影。至少她觉得自己看见了什么。“记住，”她提醒自己，“你对这整件事怀着罪疚感，所以你完全可能是自己在制造幻觉，好吓退自己，让自己干不下去。”她继续走，又过了两根柱子，又看见了它。“要不然，”她接着独白，“你就是在存心无视一个非常明显的事实：那边有个人，一个将会看见你的人。所以当心。”神庙某处是有几面镜子的，但它们不在对面墙上，所以她看见的不可能是自己。其实镜子在她进来——说不定那一位也是——穿过的前厅里，所以说不定它看见的，是她映在……“把这话理顺了再说，”她告诫自己，“照这么想下去，你什么事都干不成。”

可那边确实有个人，（就实际而言）光着身子，正蹑手蹑脚地在柱子间穿行。而且他有四条胳膊。这让她开始想到别的什么事，可那念头刚箭一般射向过去，突然被折断，整个掉了个头，又扎回她脑子里——因为他正直直地盯着她。

===Original===
If he starts walking toward me, she thought, I’m going to be scared out of my ears. So I better start walking toward him. Besides, I want to see what he looks like. She started out from the columns. Glancing quickly both ways, she saw that the temple was deserted save for them.
He’s a kid, she thought, three quarters of the way across. My age, she added, and again a foreign thought attempted to intrude itself on her but never made it, because he was coming toward her now. At last he stopped before her, silent, muscles like tight wire under the brown skin, black hair massing low on his forehead, his eyes deep beneath the black shrub of brows.
She gulped and asked him, “What are you doing here? Do you know somebody could catch you in here and get mad as hell? I know I couldn’t possibly have, but I think I’ve seen you before some place; if somebody comes along, they might even think you were trying to steal Hama’s eye.” I shouldn’t have said that, she thought, because he moved funny. “You better get out of here because everybody will be up here in a half an hour for morning services.”

===Chinese===
他要是朝我走过来，她想，我会吓得魂都出窍。所以我最好先朝他走过去。再说，我也想看看他长什么样。她从柱子间走出来。朝两头飞快一瞥，神庙里空无一人，只有他们俩。

他还是个孩子，走到四分之三路程时她想。跟我同龄，她补了一句。又有一个陌生的念头试图闯进她脑海，却没能闯进来，因为他正朝她走来。终于，他停在她面前，默不作声，棕色皮肤下肌肉像绷紧的铁丝，黑发低低压在额前，双眼深藏在浓黑如灌木的眉毛之下。

她咽了口唾沫，问他：“你在这儿干什么？你知不知道，被人在这儿撞见，人家会火冒三丈？我知道这不可能，可我好像在哪儿见过你；要是有人过来，他们没准会当你是想偷哈马的眼睛。”这话不该说的，她想，因为他动起来的样子怪怪的。“你最好离开这儿，半个钟头后所有人都要上这儿来参加晨祭。”

===Original===
At that news, he suddenly darted forward, passed her, and sprinted down toward the altar.
“Hey!” she called and ran after him.
Snake vaulted over the brass altar rail.
“Wait a minute,” she called, catching up. “Wait, will you!”

===Chinese===
一听这话，他猛地向前一窜，掠过她身边，朝祭坛直冲下去。

“喂！”她喊了一声，追了上去。

蛇飞身越过黄铜祭坛栏杆。

“等一下，”她追上他喊道，“等等，行不行！”

===Original===
Snake turned as she slung her leg across the brass bar. “Look, I realize I gave away my hand. But that was only guilt feelings. You gave yours away too, though. And if you don’t think you’ve got guilt feelings, boy, you’re crazy.”
Snake frowned, tilted his head, and then grinned.
“So we’ll help each other see,” she said. “You want it too, don’t you.” She pointed up to the head of the statue towering above them. “So let’s cooperate. I’ll get it for a little while. Then you can have it.” He was listening, she saw, so she guessed her strategy was working. Play it by ear now, she thought. “We’ll help each other. Shake on it, huh?” She stuck out her hand.

===Chinese===
她一条腿跨过黄铜栏杆时，蛇转过身来。“听着，我知道自己露了底。可那不过是罪疚感。不过你也露了你的底。小子，你要是以为自己没有罪疚感，那你准是疯了。”

蛇皱起眉，歪了歪头，随即咧嘴一笑。

“那咱们就互相帮忙，试试看，”她说。“你也想要它，对不对。”她朝头顶上高耸的雕像头部一指。“那就合作。我先拿到它，占一阵子。然后就归你。”他在听，她看得出，于是她猜自己的策略奏效了。接下来见机行事，她想。“咱们互相帮忙。握个手，怎么样？”她伸出手去。

===Original===
All four hands reached forward.
Whoops, she thought, I hope he’s not offended.
But the four hands grasped hers, and she added her second to the juncture. “All right,” she said. “Come on. Now I had all this figured out last night. And we don’t have much time. Let’s go around﻿ ﻿…” But he walked over to where the stalks of wheat spired from the altar base up through Hama’s fist, and grabbed a stalk with the three hands, and hand, over hand, over hand, began to hoist himself up to where the first broad sheets of metal leaves leaned out to form a small platform. At first his dirty feet swung out frog-like, but then he caught the stem with his toes and at last hoisted himself to the front and looked down at her.

===Chinese===
四只手一齐伸了过来。

哎呀，她想，但愿他没觉得受了冒犯。

可那四只手握住了她的手，她把另一只手也搭了上去。“好，”她说。“跟我来。这些我昨天夜里全盘算好了。咱们时间不多。咱们绕到……”可他已经走到那些从祭坛座上拔起、穿过哈马的拳头直指而上的麦秆旁，用三只手抓住一根秆子，一手接一手、再接一手地往上攀，一直攀到最初几片宽大金属叶斜伸出来、构成一方小平台的地方。起初他那双脏脚像青蛙似的朝外乱蹬，后来脚趾扣住秆身，终于翻攀到前面，低头看她。

===Original===
“I can’t climb up there,” she said, “I don’t have your elevation power.”
Snake looked down and shrugged.
“Oh damn,” she said. “I’ll do it my way.” She ran across the altar to the great foot of the statue. Sitting cross-legged, Hama’s foot was on his side. Using the ridges made by the toes as steps, she clambered up to the dark bulge of the deity’s godlike bunion. She made her way across the ankle, up the slanting shin, back down the black thigh, until she stood at the crevice where the leg and torso met.

===Chinese===
“我可爬不上去，”她说，“我没有你那攀高的本事。”

蛇低头看了看，耸了耸肩。

“噢，见鬼，”她说，“我按我的法子来。”她跑过祭坛，来到雕像那只大脚前。哈马盘着腿坐，那只脚侧转在身侧。她把脚趾的棱纹当台阶，攀上了这位神祇那副当得起神明的大趾疙瘩的黑色鼓包。她越过脚踝，登上斜伸的胫骨，又下到黑色的大腿上，一直走到腿与躯干相接的那道缝隙前。

===Original===
Out beyond the great knee, Snake regarded her from his perch in the groin of yellow leaf. They were about equal height.
“Yoo-hoo,” she waved. “Meet you at the clavicle.” Then she stuck her tongue out. The bulges in the belly of the god made a treacherous ledge along which she inched until she arrived at the cavernous navel, leaving wet handprints on the black stone.
The god’s belly button from this intimate distance revealed itself as a circular door about five feet in diameter and controlled by a combination lock. She missed the first number twice, dried her hands off, and began again. According to the plans in the main safe of the temple (on which she had first practiced combination breaking) there was a ladder behind this door which led up into the statue. She remembered it clearly; and saved her life by doing so.

===Chinese===
大膝盖之外，蛇栖在黄叶交织的胯窝里望着她。两人差不多一样高。

“喂——嗬，”她挥挥手，“锁骨那儿见。”说完她吐了吐舌头。神腹上的块块肌肉形成一道险恶的窄台，她一寸一寸挪过去，直到抵达那洞穴般的肚脐，黑石上留下一枚枚湿手印。

这么贴近了看，神的肚脐原来是一扇直径约五英尺的圆门，由一把转字密码锁控制。头一个数字她拨错了两回，擦干手，从头再来。据神庙主保险柜里的图纸（她最初练习破译组合锁，练的就是这份图纸），门后有一道梯子，直通雕像体内。她记得清清楚楚；也正是这份记性救了她的命。

===Original===
Because when she caught the second number, reversed the direction and felt the telltale click of the third, she pulled on the handle and was almost pushed from the ledge by the swinging circular door. She grabbed at a handle that she hardly saw on the door’s inside, just as the stone slipped from beneath her feet. Then she was hanging five feet out in the air over the sacred groin some fifty feet below.
The first thing she tried, after closing her eyes and mumbling a few laws of motion, was to swing the door to. When she swung out, however, the door swung closed; and when she swung in, the door swung opened. After a while, she just hung. She gave small thanks that she had dried her hands. When her arms began to ache, she wished that she hadn’t, because then it would be over by now. She went over what she knew about taking judo falls.

===Chinese===
因为等她拨到第二个数字、反转方向、又感到第三个数字暗号似的咔嗒一响时，她一拉把手，荡开的圆门差点把她从窄台上掀下去。就在脚下的石头滑开的一瞬，她一把抓住门内侧一个她几乎看不清的把手。于是她整个人悬在门外五英尺的半空中，底下五十来英尺，正是那神圣的胯裆。

闭上眼睛、念叨了几条运动定律之后，她试的头一件事，就是把门荡回去关上。可她往外荡，门就往里关；她往里荡，门又往外开。折腾了一阵，她索性吊着不动了。她小小的谢了谢天：幸亏把手擦干了。等两条胳膊开始发酸，她又后悔擦干了——不然这会儿早完了。她把知道的柔道落地法在心里过了一遍。

===Original===
Then the door swung closed, and someone grabbed her around the waist. She didn’t open her eyes, but felt her body pressed against the tilting stone. Her arms fell tingling to her sides. The ligaments flamed with pain. Then the pain dulled to throbbing, and she opened her eyes. “How the hell did you get down here?” she asked Snake. With his help she staggered through the open door and stopped to rub her arms. “How did you know about the ladder?”
They were standing in the shaft now, with the ladder beside them running up into the darkness.
He looked at her with a puzzled expression.
“What is it?” she asked. “Oh, I’ll be able to climb up there, never you worry. Hey, can you speak?”

===Chinese===
就在这时门荡拢关上，有人一把搂住了她的腰。她没有睁眼，只觉得身体被按在倾侧的石头上。两条胳膊麻刺刺地垂落身侧，韧带火烧火燎地疼。随后疼痛钝成一阵阵抽动，她睁开眼睛。“你到底是怎么下来的？”她问蛇。靠他扶着，她跌跌撞撞进了敞开的门，站住揉胳膊。“你怎么知道有梯子？”

此刻他们正站在竖井里，身边一道梯子直上黑暗深处。

他一脸困惑地望着她。

“怎么了？”她问。“哦，我爬得上去，你别操心。嘿，你会说话吗？”

===Original===
Snake shook his head.
“Oh,” she said. Something started at the edge of her mind again, a picture of something unpleasant. Snake had started up the ladder, which he had come down so quickly a minute ago. She glanced out the door, saw that the temple was empty, pulled the door to, and followed.
They ascended into complete darkness. Her arms were beginning to ache again, just slightly. She reached up for the next rung, and found it in its proper place. Then the next. And then again the next.
She started counting steps now, and when seventy-four, seventy-five, and seventy-six dropped below her, there was a missing rung. She reached above it, but there was none. She ran her hand up the edge of the ladder and found that it suddenly curved into the wall. “Hey, you,” she said in the darkness.

===Chinese===
蛇摇了摇头。

“哦，”她说。又有什么东西在她脑子边缘冒头，一幅不愉快的画面。蛇已经上梯子了——一分钟前他还顺着它飞快地下来。她朝门外瞥了一眼，见神庙里空无一人，便拉拢门，跟了上去。

他们登入一片纯黑。她的胳膊又开始发酸，只是微微的。她伸手去够上一级横档，它好好地在原处。再上一级。然后又一级。

这回她开始数级数。七十四、七十五、七十六三级落到脚下之后，有一级横档没有了。她往上面摸，也没有。她的手顺着梯子边缘往上捋，发现梯子突然弯进了墙里。“喂，伙计，”她在黑暗里说。

===Original===
Something touched her waist. “Gnnnnnggggg,” she said. “Don’t *do* that.” It touched her on the leg, took hold of her ankle, and pulled. “Watch out,” she said.
It pulled again. She raised her foot, and it was tugged sideways a good foot and a half and set on solid flooring. Then a hand (her foot was not released) took her arm, and another held her waist, and tugged. She stiffened for one instant before she remembered the number of limbs her companion had. Then she came off the ladder, sideways into the dark, afraid to put her other foot down lest she step headlong into the seventy-five foot plus shaft.
But he tugged again, and in losing her balance, her foot came down on cool, solid stone. Holding her arm now, he led her along the tunnel. They passed into a steep incline. Now down the upper arm, she recalled.

===Chinese===
什么东西碰了碰她的腰。“唔嗯嗯嗯呃呃，”她说。“别*这么*干。”它碰到她腿上，抓住脚踝，往外拉。“当心。”她说。

它又拉了一下。她抬起脚，脚被朝旁边拖了足足一英尺半，放到一块实心的地板上。然后一只手（她的脚仍未被放开）抓起她的胳膊，另一只手揽住她的腰，用力一拽。她僵了一瞬，随即想起她这位同伴的胳膊腿有多少。于是她离开梯子，侧身没入黑暗，不敢放下另一只脚，生怕一脚踏空，栽进那七十五英尺开外的竖井。

可他又拽了一下，她失了平衡，脚落在了冰凉坚实的石头上。此刻他挽着她的手臂，领她沿隧道走去。他们进入一道陡峭的斜坡。这回是顺着上臂往下走了，她回想起来。

===Original===
“I feel like Eurydice,” she said aloud.
You﻿ ﻿… funny﻿ ﻿… an echoing voice sounded in her skull.
“Hey,” she said. “What was that?” But the voice was silent. The wall turned abruptly and the floor leveled out. They were in a section of the passage now that corresponded roughly to the statue’s radial artery. At the wrist, there was a light. They mounted a stairway, came out a trap door, and found themselves standing high in the temple. Below them the great room spread, vastly deep, and still empty. Beside them, the stems of the bronze wheat stalks rose up through the fist and spired another fifty feet before breaking into clusters of golden grain and leaves. Across from them, over the dark curve of Gargantuan chest, in the statue’s other hand, the shaft of the scythe leaned away into shadow.

===Chinese===
“我觉得自己像欧律狄刻（Eurydice）。”她出声地说。

你……好玩……一个回声似的声音在她脑壳里响起。

“嘿，”她说，“那是什么？”那声音却沉默了。墙壁猛地一折，地面平了。此刻他们所在的这段通道，大致相当于雕像的桡动脉。手腕处有一盏灯。他们登上一段阶梯，从一道活板门钻出去，发觉自己已高高站在神庙里。脚下，大殿铺展开去，深广空阔，依然空无一人。身边，青铜麦秆的茎干穿过拳头拔起，又直蹿五十英尺，才绽出一簇簇金色的谷粒和叶子。对面，越过巨人胸膛的黑色曲线，在雕像的另一只手里，镰刀的长柄斜倚着没入暗影。

===Original===
“Look,” she said. “You follow me now.” She started back along the top of the forearm and then began the tedious climb over the rippling biceps, till at last they reached the broad shoulder. They walked across the hollow above the collar bone until they stood just below the great scooping shell of the ear.
She took the paper bag she had stuffed into her belt, tied one end of the string around the neck, and then, holding the other, she heaved the bag up and over the ear. She got the other end of the string, knotted it as high as she could reach, and gave it a tug. “I hope this works,” she said. “I had it all figured out yesterday. The tensile strength of this stuff is about two hundred and fifty pounds, which ought to do for you and me.” She planted her foot on the swell of the neck tendon, and in seven leaps she made it to the lobe of the ear. She swung around into the hollow, using the frontal wing as a pivot. Crouching in the hollow trumpet, she looked down at Snake. “Come up,” she said. “Hurry up.”

===Chinese===
“瞧，”她说，“这回你跟着我。”她顺着前臂顶面往回走，接着开始乏味地翻越波纹般隆起的二头肌，终于到了宽阔的肩头。他们走过锁骨上方的凹洼，站到了那大贝壳般深凹的耳朵下方。

她取出塞在腰带上的纸袋，把绳子一端系在袋颈上，然后攥着另一端，把袋子甩过高高的耳朵。她抓住绳子的另一头，在够得着的最高处打了个结，扯了扯。“但愿管用，”她说，“这些我昨天全算好了。这绳子的抗拉强度大约二百五十磅，吊咱们俩该够了。”她一脚蹬在颈部筋腱的鼓包上，七蹿八跳就上了耳垂，又以耳廓前翼为轴，一荡进了耳凹。她蹲在喇叭状的空腔里，低头看蛇。“上来，”她说，“快点。”

===Original===
Snake joined her a moment later.
The ear was hollow, too. It led back into a cylindrical chamber which went up through the head of the god. The architect who had designed the statue had conveniently left the god’s lid flipped. They climbed the ladder and emerged amid the tangle of pipes which represented the hair of the god. They made their way forward through the mass of pipes to where the forehead sloped dangerously forward. They could see the foreshortened nose and the rim of the statue’s middle eye above that. There wasn’t much of anything after that for the next thousand feet until the base of the altar. “Now you can really be some help,” she told him. “Hold on to my wrist and let me down. I’ll get the jewel.”
They grabbed wrists, and Snake’s three other hands, as well as the joints of his knees, locked around the base of five pipes that sprouted around them.
Slowly she slid forward, until her free hand slipped on the stone and she dropped the length of their two arms and swung just above the statue’s nose. The eye opened in front of her. The lid arced above her, and the white of either side of the ebony iris shone faintly in the half darkness. At the center of the iris, in a small hollow, sitting on the top of a metal support, was the jewel.

===Chinese===
片刻之后，蛇也上来了。

耳朵也是空的，通往后部一间圆筒形的腔室，直贯神明的头颅。设计这雕像的建筑师想得周到，把神的眼帘留成了翻开的样子。他们爬上梯子，从代表神之头发的乱管丛中钻出来。他们在管丛中向前挤，挤到前额陡然前倾的地方。从这里望下去，能看见透视缩短的鼻梁，以及它上方那只中眼的边沿。再往下，除了千英尺之下的祭坛座，便几乎什么都没有了。“现在你可真能帮上忙了，”她对他说，“抓住我的手腕，把我放下去。我来取珍宝。”

两人手腕扣着手腕，蛇另外三只手连同两个膝弯，锁死在他们四周生出的五根管子根部。

她缓缓向前滑，直到那只空着的手在石头上一滑，整个人坠下两条手臂的长度，荡在雕像鼻子的正上方。那只眼睛在她面前敞开着。眼帘在头顶弯成拱，乌木色虹膜两侧的眼白在半明半暗中幽幽泛光。虹膜正中一个小凹窝里，珍宝端坐在金属支架的顶上。

===Original===
She reached her free hand toward it as she swung.
From somewhere a gong suddenly sounded. Light flooded over her. Looking up, she saw white sockets of light shining down into her own eyes. Panicking, she almost released Snake’s wrist. But a voice in her head (hers or someone else’s, she couldn’t tell) rang out. Hold﻿ ﻿… on﻿ ﻿… damn﻿ ﻿… it﻿ ﻿…
Then she grabbed the jewel. The metal shaft in which the jewel had stood was not steady, and tilted as her hand came away from it. The tilting must have set off some clockwork mechanism, because the great eyelid was slowly lowering over the ivory and ebony eye. She swung again at the end of the rope of bone and flesh; half blinded by the lights above her, she looked over her shoulder, into the temple below. There was singing, the beginning of a processional hymn. The morning rites had started!

===Chinese===
她一边荡着，一边把空着的手朝它伸去。

不知何处骤然响起锣声。光浪涌过她全身。她抬头，看见一个个白亮的光窟窿直照进她的眼睛。她惊慌之下差点松开蛇的手腕。可一个声音在她脑子里（是她自己的还是别人的，她分不清）高声响起：抓……住……该……死……

于是她攥住了珍宝。珍宝原先立着的金属轴并不稳，她的手一带，它便倾倒了。那倾倒必定触发了什么钟表机关，因为巨大的眼睑正缓缓罩向那象牙与乌木的眼睛。她又荡起来，吊在这条骨肉编成的绳端；头顶的光晃得她半盲，她回头朝下面的大殿望去。有歌声，一支列队圣歌刚起了头。晨祭开始了！

===Original===
Light glinted on the stone limbs of the god. Figures were pouring into the temple. They must have seen her, but the hymn, sonorous and gigantic, rose like flood water, and she suddenly thought that if she fell, she would drown in the sound of it.
Snake was pulling her up. Stone against her arm, against her cheek. She clenched her other fist tightly at her side. Another hand came down and helped pull her. Then another. Then she was lying among the metal pipes, and he was loosening her fingers from his wrist. He tugged her to her feet, and for a moment she was looking out over the now filled temple.
Nervous energy contracted coldly along her body, and the sudden sight of the great drop filled her eyes and her head, and she staggered. Snake caught her and at last helped her back to the ladder. “We’ve got it,” she said to him before they started down. She breathed deeply. Then she checked in her palm to see if it was still there; it was, and again she looked out over the people below. Light on the upturned faces made them look like scattered pearls on the dark floor. An exaltation suddenly burst in her shoulders, flooded her legs and arms and for a moment washed the pain away. Snake, with one hand on her shoulder, was grinning also. “We’ve got it!” she said again.

===Chinese===
光在神明的石头肢体上闪烁。人影正涌进神庙。他们必定看见她了，但那圣歌洪亮浩大，像洪水般涨起来，她突然想到：若是坠下去，她将淹死在那声音里。

蛇在把她往上拉。石头擦着她的胳膊，擦着她的脸颊。她把另一只拳头紧紧攥在身侧。又一只手伸下来帮着拉。然后又一只。随后她已躺在金属管丛之间，他正把她的手指从自己手腕上掰开。他把她拽起来，有那么一刻，她望着此刻已经坐满人的神庙。

一股紧张的力道冰冷地沿着身体收紧，那深不见底的落差猛地撞进她的眼睛和头脑，她踉跄了一下。蛇扶住她，终于把她搀回梯子那儿。“到手了，”下梯子之前她对他说。她深深吸了口气。然后她摊开手掌，看它是否还在；在的。她又望向下面的人群。灯光照在仰起的脸上，那些脸像撒在暗色地面上的珍珠。一阵狂喜突然在她肩头迸发，涌遍双腿双臂，一时把疼痛冲刷干净。蛇一只手搭在她肩上，也在咧嘴笑。“到手了！”她又说了一遍。

===Original===
They went down the ladder into the statue’s skull. Snake preceded her out the hollow ear. He reached around, caught the cord, and let himself down to the shoulder.
She hesitated for a moment, then put the jewel in her mouth, and followed him. Standing beside him once more, she removed it, and then rubbed her shoulders. “Boy, am I going to have some Charley horse by tomorrow,” she said. “Do me a favor and untie my bag for me?”
Snake untied the parcel from the end of the cord, and together now they climbed down the bicep and back over the forearm to the trap door in the wrist.

===Chinese===
他们顺梯而下，进入雕像的头颅。蛇先她一步钻出空耳，四下伸手，抓住绳子，溜下了肩头。

她迟疑了一下，把珍宝含进嘴里，跟着下去了。重新站到他身边时，她把它吐出来，揉了揉肩膀。“好家伙，到明天我准要抽筋抽个够，”她说，“帮个忙，替我把袋子解开？”

蛇解开绳端的纸包。两人一起爬下二头肌，再翻过前臂，回到手腕处的活板门。

===Original===
She glanced down at the faces of the worshipers just before they disappeared into the tunnel. Snake was taking the jewel from her hand. She let him have it, and watched him raise it up above his head.
Immediately, when he raised the jewel, the pearls of faces went out like extinguished flames as heads bent all through the temple.
“That’s the ticket,” grinned Argo. “Come on.” But Snake did not go into the tunnel. Instead he walked around the fist, took hold of one of the bronze wheat stems, and slid down through an opening between the thumb and forefinger. “That way?” asked Argo. “Oh well, I guess so. You know I’m going to write an epic about this.”

===Chinese===
就在他们没入隧道之前，她朝下面礼拜者的脸庞瞥了一眼。蛇正从她手里接过珍宝。她由他拿去，看着他把它高高举过头顶。

他一举起珍宝，那一张张珍珠似的脸便像被掐灭的火苗般黯然熄灭，整座神庙里人头纷纷俯伏下去。

“这就对了，”阿戈咧嘴一笑。“走吧。”可蛇没有进隧道。他绕过拳头，抓住一根青铜麦秆，从拇指和食指间的开口滑了下去。“走那边？”阿戈问，“噢，好吧，随你。你可知道，我要把这事写成一部史诗。”

===Original===
But Snake had already gone. She followed him, clutching her feet around a great bunch of stems. He was waiting for her at the plateau of leaves, and nestled there, they gazed out once more at the fascinated congregation.
Again Snake held aloft the jewel, and again heads bowed. The hymn began to repeat itself, the individual words lost in the sonority of the hall. They started down the last length of stems now, coming quickly. When they stood at last on the base, she put her hand on his shoulder and looked across the brass altar rail. The congregation pressed close, although she did not recognize an individual face. Yet a mass of people stood there, enormous and familiar. As Snake started forward, holding up the jewel, the people fell back from the rail. Snake climbed over the altar rail, and then helped her over.
Her shoulders were beginning to hurt now, and the enormity of the theft ran chills up and down, up and down her spine. The black marble altar step as she put her foot down was awfully cold.

===Chinese===
可蛇早已下去了。她跟上去，双脚夹住一大把茎秆。他在叶片平台上等她；两人偎在那里，再一次望着下面如痴如醉的会众。

蛇再次高举珍宝，人头再次俯伏。圣歌开始重复自身，个别的字句迷失在大厅的宏响里。此刻他们顺着最后一段茎秆往下走，动作很快。终于站到底座上时，她把手搭在他肩上，越过黄铜祭坛栏杆望去。会众挤得很近，虽然她认不出一张具体的脸。可一大群人就站在那里，庞大而熟悉。蛇举着珍宝向前走时，人们从栏杆边退开。蛇翻过祭坛栏杆，又帮她翻了过去。

这时她的肩膀开始疼了，这桩窃案的滔天分量顺着她的脊梁骨一阵又一阵、一阵又一阵地窜着寒气。她一脚踏上黑大理石的祭坛台阶，冷得钻心。

===Original===
They started forward again, and the last note of the hymn echoed to silence, filling the hall with the roaring quiet of the hushed breathing of hundreds.
Simultaneously, both she and Snake got the urge to look back at the great diminishing height of Hama behind them. All three eyes were shut firmly now. A quiet composed of the rustling of a hundred dark robes upon another hundred hissed about them as they started forward again.
There was a spotlight on them, she suddenly realized. That was why the people, hovering back from the circular effulgence over the floor around them seemed so dim. Her heart had become a pulse at the bottom of her tongue. They kept on going forward, into the shadowed faces, into the parting sea of dark cloaks and hoods.

===Chinese===
他们再度向前走。圣歌的最后一个音符回荡着归于寂静，大厅里满是几百人屏息静气时那轰鸣般的安静。

不约而同地，她和蛇都想回头看看身后哈马那渐渐缩小的高大身形。三只眼睛如今都紧紧闭上了。他们再度举步时，一种由上百件黑袍蹭着另外上百件黑袍的窸窣声织成的寂静，在他们四周嘶嘶作响。
